# Slop Cannon — Implementation Progress

Source of truth for building `sc` from `README.md`. Multiple agents work this file in a loop.
Read the **Agent Protocol** and **Decisions** sections before touching any task.

Last regenerated from README: 2026-09-02.

---

## Agent Protocol

1. **Read** this whole file, then `README.md`, then `docs/` (if present). Never work from memory of an older version.
2. **Pick** the first `[ ]` task, lowest phase first, whose `deps` are all `[x]`. Tasks in different phases may run in parallel when their deps allow it (see *Parallelism* below).
3. **Claim** it: change `[ ]` to `[~]` and append `(claimed: <agent-id> <ISO-8601 UTC>)` to the task line. Commit immediately with message `claim <TASK-ID>`. One task per agent at a time.
4. **Implement** it. Every task ends with `make check` green (build + lint + tests). Add tests with the code, not later.
5. **Finish**: change `[~]` to `[x]`, replace the claim note with `(done: <agent-id> <commit-sha>)`, and add a one-line note if you deviated from the task text. Commit with message `<TASK-ID>: <short summary>`.
6. **Blocked**: change to `[!]` with `(blocked: <reason>)`. Pick another task. Do not silently skip.
7. **Stale claims**: a `[~]` task with no commits referencing it for 4 hours may be reclaimed. Note the takeover on the task line.
8. **New work you discover** goes in *Discovered Tasks* at the bottom with the next free ID in the relevant phase (e.g. `P3-08`). Never renumber or delete existing IDs.
9. **Deviations from a Decision** require a new entry in *Decisions* (mark the old one superseded). Do not quietly change architecture.
10. **Do not** edit files that another agent's `[~]` task obviously owns. If you must, coordinate via a note on their task line.
11. A phase is complete when every task in it is `[x]` and its *Exit criteria* pass. Update the *Phase Status* table when that happens.

**Status legend**: `[ ]` todo · `[~]` in progress · `[x]` done · `[!]` blocked
**Size**: S (< 1 hour of focused work), M (1–3 h), L (3–8 h). Split any L you cannot finish in one loop iteration into discovered subtasks.

**Global definition of done** for every task:
- Code compiles, `make check` passes, new behaviour has unit tests.
- Public functions have doc comments. No `TODO` without a task ID next to it.
- User-facing behaviour is reflected in `docs/` or `--help` text.
- No secrets, no absolute paths from your machine, no commented-out code.

a D-08 ruling is binding (a non-`fixed` answer is refused, the item keeps its ruling and stays owed, with a warning and a thread note); `CheckFailed` at `max_review_rounds` takes the `changes_requested → escalated` edge instead of buying a round; `ReviewAgain` publishes the thread's own round; and `ConsolidateItems` is the live merge, so the canonical item id does not depend on which tester replied first. **P6-05** — brief mode gives up after `DefaultPlanFailureLimit` (5) consecutive *non-rejection* planning failures, not only after two rejections; the settle listing is debounced on failure and warns once per distinct failure (40 polls now make ≤ 21 bd listings and one warning, where they made 40 of each); autopilot re-plans while a non-engineer runs, through the new additive `SchedulerStatus.Quiet`; only *consecutive* rejections count; and a Q-04 demotion publishes `events.BeadUpdated{Fields: ["priority"]}` rather than being a silent bd write. **P6-06** — a bd hiccup no longer spends a conflict round (`peekAttempt` numbers it, `spendAttempt` runs only after the `delivering → in_progress` write lands), a conflict round is never empty (`DefaultConflictOutput`, `midRebaseOutput`), and a mid-rebase worktree whose conflicted paths cannot be listed opens its conflict round with a warning instead of being retried for ever. **P6-07** — a failed `AgentFiring.Result` hook is terminal and reported as the new `events.FailureInternal` with an `events.Error` from `supervisor`, never retried as a cartridge crash (`ErrPostSession`); `track` takes the stop flag and the registration under one lock, so `StopAgents` cannot race a firing into a session; the `started` map is pruned on the terminal path; and `copyDir` refuses `".."`, which would have been the run directory itself. **P6-08** — the fold skips a damaged or forward-version line and keeps going, reporting it in `RunSummary.Notes` (an error now means the stream itself failed), a torn last line is terminated on re-open so a resumed run does not glue its next event onto the wreck, `sc status` scrubs the bead timeline and the agents/beads sections before the columns are measured, and a bead the log names without a state is counted as `BeadStateUnrecorded` so the breakdown adds up. **P6-10** — a merge is remembered from the gate id the verdict carries, so a transient `bd gate show` failure cannot strand a bead in `delivering` (`GatePollResult.PendingGates`); the three untested P6-14 escalation branches are covered; `Poll` really is serialised (`pollMu`); and P6-22 and P1-17 shipped with it. Integrator round 3: the package needed no interface unified — `host.go` still owns the shared seams and every lane kept its symbols in its own files — but two coherence fixes landed. Driving `sc tui-demo` in a real 45x200 pty for this round's exit check found the one thing the unit tests had encoded rather than caught: the ammo prompt opens pre-filled with the run's ammo and the field is capped at as many digits as the ceiling has (2 for 99), so at a two-digit ammo every digit typed was silently dropped and `a` `6` `enter` answered "ammo is already 10" — the wiring test had to press backspace twice before its digit to get through. The first digit typed over an untouched pre-fill now replaces it (`ControlsModel.bufFresh`), backspace still edits it digit by digit, and the test types the digit directly with a second case pinning the backspace path (P7-23). Re-driven in the same pty afterwards: `a` `6` `enter` reports `sc: run control: ammo 6` and flashes "ammo 6 for future ticks"; `↓` `x` `y` reports `mark demo-3 done (user)` and flashes "demo-3 marked done"; `S` `y` reports `safety on`; `p` reports `pause (user)`; `r` on an open bead refuses with "demo-3 is open — only a failed, needs_human, escalated or blocked bead can be re-fired"; `?` lists all six run controls; `3` switches to Feedback; and `q` `y` exits 0 with "stop confirmed". (A pty driver has to answer the `OSC 11` background and `CSI 6n` cursor-position queries or Bubble Tea swallows the keystrokes as query responses.) `tui.Controller` still has no orchestrator implementation, so `sc run` must pass a nil controller until P7-20. One flake was chased rather than re-run and turned out to be a production race: the whole suite under `UPDATE_GOLDEN` failed once with `TestE2EKillAndResume` timing out, nine review firings dead on `git commit …: Another git process seems to be running in this repository`, because D-05's two lenses on one bead both build their prompt through `Conductor.BeadContext` and both run git in that bead's single worktree — **P10-12**, fixed here with a mutex per bead and a test that fails without it. Two new discovered tasks: P10-12 (done) and P10-13 (`make check-golden` discards the regeneration output, so a failure prints only `Error 1`). Open work carried forward unchanged: P3-17, P5-14, P6-44, P6-49, P6-52, P7-20, P7-22, P8-25, P8-33, P1-20, P4-17, P6-57, P7-26, P7-27, P8-39, P8-40, P9-32, P9-33, P6-58, P7-28, P8-41, P10-10, P10-11, and the new P10-13. **Round 4 (wf-p10)**: no new task work — this round closed the reviewer gaps on P10-05, and one of them changes a mark. **P10-05 is now `[!]`, not `[x]`**: its "Done when" asks for the pull request link and the dogfood run was built so that one could not exist (the clone's `origin` is removed, `--review-prs=false` is passed and the `gh` stand-in refuses everything but the read-only probes), so under Agent Protocol rule 6 it is **blocked** on the same go-ahead P8-22 needs, rather than `[x]` with a rule-5 deviation note — rule 5 covers the deviation it really has, that sc is fired at a clone instead of at `.`. Two corrections to the round-2 rejected-defect note (repeated verbatim in the round-2 entry below): `gh auth status` has **two** production callers, not one — `gitx.GH.Check` (`internal/gitx/prwatch.go:282`) and `cli.doctorGH` (`internal/cli/doctor.go:335`), the latter reached by `sc doctor` as well as by `AdviseStartup`; `gh --version` does have exactly one, `doctorGH` (`internal/cli/doctor.go:320`). And the "10 `--version` and 10 `auth status`" tally is **not committed evidence**: it lived in the run's temporary machine (`$work/gh/calls`), which `dogfood.sh` deletes unless `--keep` is passed, and no `gh` invocation appears in the committed recording. The rejection itself still stands and is now reproducible for nothing: `scripts/manual/dogfood.sh --preflight --keep` leaves exactly two gh calls, both from the single `sc doctor` the preflight runs, and the fired path runs no doctor at all — so sc issued none of the 20. `dogfood.sh` now writes the stand-in's call log to `testdata/recorded/dogfood-gh-calls.txt` beside the recording, so the next run's tally is evidence a reader can check. P7-28's supporting counts were re-derived from the recording and stand (48 + 51 + 17 = **116** numbered source lines); its task line now carries the pattern to count them with, because the first line of each Bash result carries a `[result Bash ok] ` prefix and a naive recount lands on 109. **Round 4 integration (wf-p10, `71bfe07`)**: the wave's two batches touched disjoint files, so there was nothing to reconcile — P10-03's are `README.md`, `.goreleaser.yaml` and `internal/cli/release_artifacts_test.go`, P10-05's are `scripts/manual/dogfood.sh` and this file — and one definition per concept still holds (the new tests reuse `repoFile` and `goreleaserYAMLBlock` rather than growing copies). **P10-03's two reviewer gaps are closed.** The README's bootstrap one-liner fetched `raw.githubusercontent.com/maxh213/slop-cannon/main/install.sh` while this repository's only branch is `master` and `install.sh` advertises `/HEAD/` for itself, so the command a reader copies 404s before it downloads anything — the first exit criterion failing at step one. It now uses `/HEAD/`, byte-identical to install.sh's own one-liner, and `TestBootstrapInstallURLNamesNoBranch` scans `README.md`, `install.sh`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `.goreleaser.yaml` and every page in `docs/` for a `raw.githubusercontent.com` ref that is a branch name and pins the README's URL to install.sh's. And `.goreleaser.yaml`'s changelog block — conventional-commit `feat:`/`fix:` groups and `^docs:`/`^chore:`/`^claim ` filters, none of which matched a single one of this repository's commits — is rewritten around the history that exists: `use: git` (so the documented offline dry run and the real release produce the same notes), no `sort` (it sorted by *message*, which listed P10-* above P2-*), `abbrev: 7`, a `{{ .SHA }} {{ .Message }}` format that drops the 40-character SHA and the author address, and excludes for the progress-file bookkeeping commits; a new `release.header` links `CHANGELOG.md` at the tag and explains that the subjects carry `P<phase>-<number>` task IDs, which is the readable half of that gap (goreleaser's template engine has no regex function, so the IDs cannot be stripped). `TestChangelogConfigMatchesRealHistory` compiles every filter and group pattern and fails one that matches no real commit subject. **One gap the integrator found and fixed in the round's own new test**: it reads the repository's own `git log`, and CI's `integration` job checks out at the default depth of 1, where `": record "` matches nothing and the test fails — reproduced in a `--depth 1` clone — so it now skips on a shallow clone and is enforced by the `make check` job, which fetches the full history. `go mod tidy` a no-op; `make check` green (golangci-lint 0 issues), `make check-golden` green on a clean tree, `make integration` green with the pinned bd 1.2.2 first on `PATH` — **2343 top-level tests passed, 6 skipped, 0 failed** (2744 subtests, none skipped), the same six by-design skips as rounds 2 and 3, with all 17 `TestIntegration*` beads tests running against bd 1.2.2. Exit criteria re-verified: a fresh clone with this round's changes overlaid builds with `make build` and installs with `make install`, and `sc version` prints; a tagged `goreleaser release --clean --skip=publish,announce` in that clone produced all four archives, `checksums.txt` and the Homebrew cask, and `dist/sc_linux_amd64_v1/sc version` printed `sc v0.1.0`, with `dist/CHANGELOG.md` now 30 entries in git order carrying 7-character SHAs and no bookkeeping commits (it was 48 alphabetically sorted entries with full SHAs and an author address on every line); the README Quickstart was walked literally again in a temp `HOME`, temp XDG directories and a temp repository (`sc cartridge --add claude --model fable`, `sc doctor` 3 pass/3 warn, `sc ./my-project --dry-run`, `sc status`, `sc logs`, `sc stop`, `sc resume`, `sc ammo 12`, the `sc ammo 2` refusal, `sc safety off`, `sc vibes`, `sc rules`, `sc config path`, `sc completion fish`, `sc version`), and `make demo DEMO_ARGS=--check` closed 2 beads through 2 pull requests and 2 merges with every animation exercised. P7-28's supporting counts were re-derived independently from the committed recording and stand exactly as written (721 events, 691 `agent_output`, **116** numbered source lines under the pattern the task line now carries). **P8-22 stays deliberately open**, and it is still the last thing between this repository and a tag: the manual verification against a throwaway GitHub repository would create real repositories, branches and pull requests on the user's own GitHub account, so it needs the user's explicit go-ahead, which no agent in this wave had — and **P10-05 is `[!]` on that same permission**. v0.1.0 stays untagged for the round-1 reason (no remote, so the release URLs are a guess — P10-11). No new discovered tasks: the only defect this round found was in its own new test and was fixed there. Open work carried forward unchanged: P3-17, P5-14, P6-44, P6-49, P6-52, P7-20, P7-22, P8-25, P8-33, P1-20, P4-17, P6-57, P7-26, P7-27, P8-39, P8-40, P9-32, P9-33, P6-58, P7-28, P8-41, P10-10, P10-11, P10-13. |

**Parallelism**: Phase 0 first. Then Phase 1. After that, Phases 2, 3, 4 are independent of each other. Phase 5 needs only Phase 2's interface and mock (P2-01, P2-02). Phase 7 can start once P0-05/P0-06 exist, using the fake event generator (P7-01). Phase 6 needs 2, 3, 4, 5. Phases 8–10 are sequential after 6 and 7.

---

## Glossary (README term → code concept)

| README term | Meaning in code |
|---|---|
| Cannon / firing at a repo | One orchestrator **Run** against a target git repository |
| Ammo | Concurrency budget: maximum number of agent subprocesses alive at once (minimum 3) |
| Cartridge | An LLM CLI backend adapter plus its model. Only Claude Code for now (D-19); the design is pluggable and more cartridges (Kimi, Animus, custom commands, others) will be added in future |
| Bead | One unit of work: an issue in the beads (`bd`) tracker inside the target repo. `bd` embeds Dolt in-process; no separate `dolt` binary is needed (Appendix A) |
| Engineer | Implementor agent; owns a bead in its own git worktree |
| UX / QA / Tester | Reviewer agents; three **lenses**: `ux`, `qa`, `test` |
| Product owner / BA | Planner agent: researches the repo, creates beads with dependencies, arbitrates disputes |
| Feedback loop | Review rounds on a bead until every assigned tester approves |
| Vibes | Style preference files appended to every agent prompt (soft guidance) |
| Rules | Hard constraint files appended to every agent prompt (MUST/MUST NOT) |
| Safety on | Finished work is pushed as a branch and opened as a PR; humans merge |
| Safety off | Finished work is rebased and merged straight into the default branch |
| Brief (`-m`) | A human-written description that the PO decomposes into beads |
| Autopilot | No brief: the PO researches the repo and invents the work itself |

---

## Decisions

Recorded defaults so agents never block on unspecified points. Change only via a new numbered entry.

- **D-01 Language & stack**: Go 1.23+. CLI: `spf13/cobra`. TUI: `charmbracelet/bubbletea`, `lipgloss`, `bubbles`, `harmonica` (spring animations). Config: TOML via `pelletier/go-toml/v2`. Logging: `log/slog`. Rationale: `bd` and Dolt are Go, single static binary, best-in-class TUI animation support. Alternative considered: TypeScript + Ink (the `.idea` module is a generic WEB_MODULE; not treated as a stack signal). If the human overrides this, record D-01a and adjust P0-01. **Confirmed 2026-09-02 (P0-01)**: module path `slop-cannon` (no git remote, so no owner), `go 1.27.0` in `go.mod`; pinned cobra v1.10.2, bubbletea v1.3.10, lipgloss v1.1.0, bubbles v1.0.0, harmonica v0.2.0, go-toml/v2 v2.4.3, plus testify v1.12.1 and `charmbracelet/x/exp/teatest` for tests.
- **D-02 Module layout**: `cmd/sc/` (main), `internal/cli`, `internal/config`, `internal/domain`, `internal/events`, `internal/proc`, `internal/cartridge/{claude,mock}`, `internal/beads`, `internal/gitx`, `internal/agents`, `internal/orchestrator`, `internal/tui`, `internal/logging`, `testdata/`, `docs/`.
- **D-03 Config precedence**: CLI flags > repo config `<repo>/.slop-cannon/config.toml` > global config `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/slop-cannon/config.toml` (default `~/.config/slop-cannon/`) > built-in defaults. Cartridges live in global config only (they are subscriptions). `ammo`, `safety`, `vibes`, `rules` may live in either; config subcommands write to the repo config when cwd is inside a git repo, else global; `--global` forces global.
- **D-04 Built-in defaults** (from README): `safety = true`, `ammo = 10`, `vibes = []`, `rules = []`, `cartridges = []` (a run refuses to start with no cartridge).
- **D-05 Ammo allocation**: `ammo` is a concurrency cap, not a fixed roster. Logical targets: PO = 1 slot when needed; `max_engineers_in_flight = max(1, floor((ammo-1)/4))`; every remaining slot is available to testers so each engineer can have 3 testers (README's ideal ratio). Scheduler priority when slots free up: arbitration > reviews awaiting testers > implementation of ready beads > planning. Minimum ammo 3 = one of each role.
- **D-06 Bead state lives in beads** (revised 2026-09-02 after verifying bd 1.2.2, see Appendix A): workflow state is the bead's **status**, using bd's native custom-status feature (`bd config set status.custom …`, D-07). Run bookkeeping goes in bead **metadata** with `sc_` keys (`sc_run`, `sc_round`, `sc_pr_url`, `sc_pr_number`, `sc_merge_sha`, `sc_agent`, `sc_lease`, `sc_kind`); `bd:` and `_` metadata prefixes are reserved by beads. Labels are used only for cheap filtering: `sc:run:<run-id>`, `sc:role:<role>`, `sc:external-pr`. Structured agent messages are bead comments written with `--actor sc:<agent-id>` and `bd comment <id> --stdin`, containing one fenced JSON block with a `type` field (`impl_report`, `verdict`, `response`, `arbitration`, `plan`). Nothing that matters for resume is kept only in memory.
- **D-07 Bead states** (revised 2026-09-02): logical states map 1:1 onto bd statuses. Built-in: `open` (ready/claimable), `in_progress` (claimed + implementing, set atomically by `bd update --claim`), `closed` (done). Custom, registered at run start with `bd config set status.custom "in_review:wip,changes_requested:wip,approved:wip,delivering:wip,escalated:frozen,needs_human:frozen,failed:frozen"` (merged with any custom statuses the repo already has, never replacing them): `in_review`, `changes_requested`, `approved`, `delivering`, `escalated` (PO arbitration), `needs_human`, `failed`. The `wip`/`frozen` categories keep these beads out of `bd ready` automatically. bd's own `blocked` and `deferred` statuses are never set by `sc`. Flow: `open → in_progress → in_review → (changes_requested → in_progress …) → approved → delivering → closed`. Transitions are pure functions in `internal/orchestrator/state.go`.
- **D-08 Feedback loop limits**: `max_review_rounds = 5`, `max_pushback_rounds = 2` per item, then PO arbitration; PO ruling is binding. Round cap without approval → `escalated`; PO may approve-with-notes, narrow scope, or mark `blocked` for a human.
- **D-09 Which testers re-review**: only testers who did not approve in the previous round re-review. An approval stands unless the engineer's diff changes more than `re_review_threshold` (default 40% of changed lines), in which case all three re-review.
- **D-10 Safety semantics**: on → push `sc/<bead-id>-<slug>` and open a PR with `gh`; never touch the default branch. off → rebase onto default branch, run the repo's `check_command` if configured/detected, fast-forward merge, push if a remote exists. Merge conflicts send the bead back to the engineer as a `conflict` round (does not count against `max_review_rounds`).
- **D-11 External PRs**: when `gh` and a remote exist, the cannon also reviews open PRs it did not create: one review bead per PR, testers post a consolidated review via `gh pr review`. It never merges a PR it did not create, regardless of safety.
- **D-12 Cartridge selection**: the first cartridge added becomes active; `sc cartridge --use NAME` switches. Optional per-role override in config (`[roles.tester] cartridge = "<name>"`); with only Claude supported (D-19) the override is parsed and validated but has no practical effect until a second adapter exists. Every cartridge is invoked as a subprocess in the agent's worktree; no SDKs.
- **D-13 Beads init** (revised 2026-09-02): if the target repo has no `.beads/`, `sc` runs `bd init --non-interactive --init-if-missing --skip-agents --skip-hooks --setup-exclude -p <prefix>` and logs it (autopilot never prompts). Reasons, all verified live: the default `bd init` writes CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.agents/`, installs five git hooks, and **auto-commits** to the user's repo; `--skip-agents --skip-hooks` stops the files and hooks but still commits `.beads/` config; `--setup-exclude` puts `.beads/` in `.git/info/exclude` so nothing is committed; `--stealth` also edits the user's global git config and is not used. `bd init` still appends a "Beads / Dolt files" block to the repo `.gitignore`; `sc` reverts that hunk when it is the only change to `.gitignore`, otherwise leaves it and warns. `sc` writes nothing else into the target repo: run state and worktrees live outside it (D-23). `.slop-cannon/config.toml` is the one committable file and is created only when a config command is used. Prefix defaults to the directory name; `sc` passes it explicitly so it is stable.
- **D-14 Agents commit their own work**; the orchestrator auto-commits any leftovers after an engineer exits (`sc(<bead>): round <n> leftovers`) so testers always review a committed diff.
- **D-15 Headless is a first-class mode** (`--headless`): identical run, events rendered as log lines / JSONL. CI and the dogfooding task use it.
- **D-16 File name**: this file is `IMPLEMENTATION-PROGRESS.md` (lowercase extension, matching `README.md`).
- **D-17 Animus is deferred** (human decision, 2026-09-02): no adapter, no module, not a known cartridge name. `sc cartridge --add animus` fails with `cartridge "animus" is not supported yet`. Revisit only when the human reopens it. Superseded in scope by D-19, which defers every non-Claude adapter the same way.
- **D-18 Autopilot never stops on its own** (human decision, 2026-09-02). An autopilot run ends only when: (a) the user stops it (`s` key, `sc stop`, or SIGINT/SIGTERM), or (b) every configured cartridge is **out of credits**. There is no bead cap and no runtime cap. Out of credits means the cartridge CLI reports quota/billing/credit exhaustion (a distinct error kind from a rate limit, which only triggers backoff). When the ready queue is empty the PO plans more work; when the PO has nothing to add the run idles and re-plans after `idle_replan_interval` (default 10 min). Optional cost/token caps, if the user sets them, **pause** the run with a banner rather than finishing it. Brief mode still finishes when all its beads are settled.
- **D-20 Dependencies gate delivery** (2026-09-02, from bd semantics): `bd ready` only lists beads whose blockers are `closed`, and `bd close` refuses a bead whose blockers are still open. Therefore a bead is closed only when its code has landed on the default branch. Safety off: rebase + fast-forward merge, then `bd close`. Safety on: the bead stays in `delivering` after the PR opens, and `sc` creates a native gate (`bd gate create --type=gh:pr --blocks <bead> --await-id <pr-number>`), polls `bd gate check --type=gh:pr` (which shells out to `gh pr view`), and closes the bead when the gate resolves; dependents therefore wait for the human merge, which is the point of safety. A PR closed without merge escalates the gate and moves the bead to `needs_human`. Dependency direction rule for every writer: `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>`; never use `bd create --deps blocks:<id>` (verified to create the reverse edge); plain `--deps <id>` or the graph plan `deps` field mean "depends on".
- **D-21 bd invocation contract** (2026-09-02): the orchestrator is the **only** beads writer and serialises all `bd` calls through one in-process queue (embedded Dolt is single-writer: the engine takes an exclusive `flock` on the data directory, and beads opens it with unbounded exponential backoff, so a second process waits rather than fails; 30 parallel writers succeeded in testing but queued at ~0.2 s each, and serialising in-process keeps latency predictable and avoids stacking blocked processes). Every call sets `BD_JSON_ENVELOPE=1` (future-proof `{"schema_version":1,"data":…}` shape, errors as `{"error","code","hint"}`), `BD_NON_INTERACTIVE=1`, `BEADS_ACTOR=sc:<orchestrator-or-agent-id>`, `-C <repo>`, `--json`, and `-n 0 --flat` on listings (defaults truncate at 50/100 and render trees). The parser skips any non-JSON preamble (bd prints a one-time metrics notice). Minimum version `1.2.2` (latest release, verified 2026-08-15); features documented upstream but absent in 1.2.2 (events journal, `revision` field) are not relied on. Agents never write to beads: each worktree gets a `bd` shim first on `PATH` that execs the real binary with `--readonly` (P3-09), and prompts say so.
- **D-22 Plans are created atomically with `bd create --graph`** (2026-09-02): the PO's plan JSON is converted to bd's graph-plan schema (`nodes[]` with `key,title,type,priority,description,acceptance_criteria,labels,metadata,deps[{target}]`, plus `parent_key` for the run epic) and applied with `--dry-run` first, then for real; the result maps plan keys to minted IDs. Priorities use bd's scale, `0` (highest) to `4`, default `2`. Each run owns one `epic` bead; work beads are its children so `bd ready --parent <epic>` and `bd swarm status <epic>` show the run. *Schema detail superseded by D-24: node-level `deps` and `acceptance_criteria` are not accepted by bd 1.2.2.*
- **D-23 Nothing of ours lives inside the target repo** (2026-09-02): run state, logs, prompts, and worktrees go under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/slop-cannon/<repo-id>/runs/<run-id>/` (default `~/.local/state/slop-cannon/…`), worktrees at `…/wt/<bead-id>`. This avoids `.gitignore` edits, nested worktrees confusing the user's tools, and dirty-tree noise. Supersedes the `.slop-cannon/run/` paths in D-13/D-15 and Phase 6/7 tasks; treat every `.slop-cannon/run/<id>/` mention below as `<state-dir>/runs/<id>/`.
- **D-19 Claude is the only adapter for now** (human decision, 2026-09-02): the only known cartridge name is `claude`. `sc cartridge --add <anything else>` fails with `cartridge "<name>" is not supported yet (only "claude" for now)`. More cartridges are explicitly planned for the future. The `Cartridge` interface, registry, mock, and structured-output contract stay adapter-agnostic so Kimi, Animus, a custom-command adapter, or any other CLI can be added later by dropping in one package and one registry line, without touching the orchestrator (see P2-10 for the guide). Multi-cartridge fallback in the supervisor (P6-07) is implemented against the interface and tested with two mock cartridges.
- **D-24 Graph-plan dependencies go in top-level `edges[]`** (2026-09-02, supersedes the node schema detail of D-22; evidence captured during P0-09 in `internal/testutil/testdata/bd/create-graph-dry-run.{json,stderr}`): bd 1.2.2 rejects node-level `deps` and `acceptance_criteria` as unknown fields (`warning: graph plan node["max"] has unknown field(s): [acceptance_criteria deps] (silently dropped …)`) and the dry run reports `edge_count: 0`. P3-10 must therefore emit every dependency as `edges[{from_key,to_key,type:"blocks"}]` (from = the dependent bead, to = its blocker, matching `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>`), verify the edge count in the `--dry-run` result equals the plan's dependency count, and set acceptance criteria with `bd update --acceptance` after creation unless P3-11 finds the accepted node field name. Any `warning:` line on the dry-run stderr fails the plan build. The remaining D-22 rules (epic parent, `--dry-run` first, key→id mapping, priorities) stand.
- **D-26 `bd init` runs with the repository root as its working directory** (2026-09-02, amends D-21 for that one command; found in P3-01): bd 1.2.2 refuses `bd -C <dir> init` when `<dir>` has no beads project yet (`cannot use -C directory …: no beads project found`, sample `internal/beads/testdata/bd/init-dash-C.stderr`), so `beads.EnsureInitialised` runs the D-13 command with cwd = root and no `-C`; every other call keeps `-C <repo>`. Also verified: `bd init` writes `[beads] role = maintainer` into `.git/config` (left in place), creates `.gitignore` when absent, inserts a newline before its block when the file lacks one, skips the block when already present, rewrites unacceptable prefixes (`x.y → x_y`, `1abc → bd_1abc`), and exits 1 after creating `.beads/` on a prefix with a space (`sc` removes the partial directory so the next run retries).
- **D-25 Six structured message types** (2026-09-02, amends the message list in D-06; found during P0-05 round 2): bead comments carry a `type` of `plan`, `impl_report`, `verdict`, `response`, `arbitration` **or `escalation`**. The PO's ruling after the review-round cap (D-08) is a distinct message from a per-item `arbitration` and must survive a resume like the others, so it gets its own type (`domain.MessageEscalation`, wrapping `domain.Escalation`). P3-03's comment reader and writer, P5-05's schemas and P6-04 must handle all six. Everything else in D-06 stands.
- **D-27 Conflict rounds are capped separately** (2026-09-05, amends D-10; P1-18): a delivery may send a bead back to its engineer to resolve a merge conflict at most `limits.max_conflict_rounds` times (default 3) before the bead is parked in `needs_human`. D-10 deliberately keeps conflict rounds out of `max_review_rounds` and `max_pushback_rounds`, so this is the only thing bounding them: an engineer that keeps leaving the worktree mid-rebase would otherwise loop forever. Three allows the two rounds a legitimate conflict can need — one for the conflict itself, one more for the default branch moving again underneath the first resolution — plus a spare. It is not a review budget: a conflict round fires one engineer and no testers unless D-09 asks for them, and only a round an engineer was really given is counted (a round whose bd write failed is retried, not spent). The number lives in `config.DefaultMaxConflictRounds`; `orchestrator.DefaultMaxConflictRounds` aliases it and `sc run` passes the configured value.
- **D-28 A run adopts and settles another run's external review bead** (2026-09-05, amends D-11; P8-36): an external-PR review bead raised by an earlier run is adopted and reviewed exactly like this run's own, and is settled under the same `StaleReviewPolicy`, with a reason that says the bead was raised by an earlier run (`gitx.StaleReview.ForeignRun`). It is never settled while its pull request is still open and external, so one pull request keeps one review bead for its whole lifetime, across as many runs as it takes. Rationale: the watcher's dedupe listing is deliberately unscoped (`ListFilter{All: true, Labels: {sc:external-pr}}`) so a pull request never collects a second review bead. Refusing to settle a foreign bead without also scoping that listing would strand it open forever the moment its run is gone — nothing else in the product settles a review bead — and its pull request could never get a fresh one, because the dedupe would keep matching the stale one. Scoping the listing instead would trade that for a review bead per run per pull request, which is the duplication the unscoped listing exists to prevent. `StaleReviewIgnore` is the escape hatch for a user who wants the other behaviour.

## Open Questions for the Human (assumed defaults in force until answered)

- **Q-01** Is Go acceptable, or do you want TypeScript/Ink? *(Assumed: Go, D-01.)*
- **Q-02** ~~What is the "animus" cartridge?~~ Resolved 2026-09-02: ignore for now (D-17).
- **Q-03** ~~Should autopilot ever stop on its own?~~ Resolved 2026-09-02: never, except user stop or all cartridges out of credits (D-18).
- **Q-04** Should the PO be allowed to close/deprioritise beads it created earlier? *(Assumed: yes, in autopilot only.)*
- **Q-05** Does "ammo" count the PO, or only engineers + testers? *(Assumed: counts the PO, D-05.)*
- **Q-06** bd sends anonymous usage metrics by default (`bd metrics off` disables). `sc` leaves that setting alone and documents it. Say so if you want `sc doctor` to warn about it.

---

## Phase 0 — Foundations & Domain Model

**Goal**: the repo builds, tests run in CI, and the shared types every other phase depends on exist.
**Exit criteria**: `make check` green locally and in CI; `go run ./cmd/sc version` prints a version; `internal/domain` and `internal/events` have ≥ 80% coverage.

- [x] **P0-01 — Confirm stack and record it** (deps: none; size: S) (done: wf-p0 be10e4e) — no git remote, so module path is `slop-cannon`; `cmd/sc/deps.go` (build tag `deps`, never compiled) pins the D-01 libraries in go.mod until real packages import them (see P0-10).

## Phase 1 — CLI & Configuration

**Goal**: every command in the README parses, validates, persists, and prints sensibly, without any orchestration yet.
**Exit criteria**: all README example commands succeed or fail with the intended message; `sc config show` displays merged config with sources; `sc doctor` exits non-zero on a machine without `bd`.

- [x] **P1-01 — Root command and global flags** (deps: P0-03; size: S) (done: wf-p1 c7a00b7) — subcommands register factories from `init()` (`register(func() *cobra.Command)`) so every `NewRootCommand` call gets fresh flags; adds shared plumbing `GlobalOpts`, `writeJSON`, `ExitError`, `ExecuteContext` (SIGINT/SIGTERM cancel the context); root accepts a path-like positional as the run target (P1-08). Help Examples mirror the README block (test parses `README.md`; the README line `sc ./repo-path ` has a trailing space that is trimmed). Goldens in `testdata/cli/`, regenerated with `make update-golden`.
  - Do: `internal/cli`: cobra root `sc` with `--repo PATH` (default cwd), `--global`, `--verbose`, `--json`, `--no-color`; `sc version`; help text whose examples mirror the README block verbatim. Unknown subcommand suggests nearest.
  - Done when: `sc --help` and `sc version` work; help golden test.
- [x] **P1-02 — Config schema, load, merge, save** (deps: P1-01, P0-05; size: M) (done: wf-p1 c7a00b7) — `vibes`/`rules` are **unioned** global-then-repo (source `global+repo`), not replaced; `[cartridges]` in a repo file is ignored with an `Effective.Warnings` entry rather than an error; `Load` walks up for `.git` (`FindRepoRoot`, no git binary); durations are a `Duration` struct so bare integers are rejected (go-toml gives that error no line number, see P1-13); `RemoveCartridge` promotes the next name in sorted order; `limits.*` also carries `agent_timeout`, `max_tokens`, `max_cost_usd` (D-18/P9-01); `re_review_threshold` (D-09) is not a key yet; prompt-file paths are stored relative to the config dir when under the repo root (`../vibes.md`). Also `RepoID`/`StateDir`/`RunDir` (D-23) and `RunConfig` (P1-08).
  - Do: `internal/config`: TOML schema (`[cartridges.<name>] model, command, args, env`, `active_cartridge`, `ammo`, `safety`, `vibes[]`, `rules[]`, `check_command`, `limits.*` from D-08/D-18, `idle_replan_interval`, `[roles.<role>] cartridge` (parsed per D-12, inert until a second adapter exists)). `Load(repoPath) (Effective, error)` implementing D-03 with per-key source tracking; `SaveGlobal`, `SaveRepo`; atomic writes; schema version key with migration hook. Defaults per D-04.
  - Done when: tests cover precedence for every key, missing files, malformed TOML error message with line number, atomic write; `docs/config.md` documents every key.
- [x] **P1-03 — `sc cartridge` commands** (deps: P1-02; size: M) (done: wf-p1 c7a00b7) — re-adding without `--model` keeps the existing model; `--list`/bare `sc cartridge` never create the global file; `--json` prints the state after the action. `CartridgeAddHint` is the shared hint text.
  - Do: `--add NAME [--model M]`, `--remove NAME`, `--list`, `--use NAME`. Known name: `claude` only (D-19). Any other name, including `kimi` and `animus`, is rejected with the D-19 message. Adding the first cartridge sets it active; removing the active one promotes the next or leaves it blank with a warning. Always writes global config (D-03).
  - Done when: README examples `sc cartridge --add claude --model fable` and `--remove claude` pass an integration test on a temp `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`; `--add kimi --model 3` and `--add animus` fail with the D-19 message.
- [x] **P1-04 — `sc ammo N`** (deps: P1-02; size: S) (done: wf-p1 c7a00b7) — also `sc ammo` with no argument reports the effective value and its source; negative numbers need `sc ammo -- -1`.

Exit 1 on any fail.
  - Done when: tests using `FakeBin` shims for each row; output golden.

  - Do: `sc <path> [-m|--message BRIEF] [--headless] [--resume] [--ammo N] [--safety on|off] [--cartridge NAME] [--no-anim] [--review-prs=true|false]`. Resolve `RunConfig` (domain.Run + effective config). Refuse when no cartridge is active with: `no cartridge loaded — add one with: sc cartridge --add claude --model <model>`. ## Phase 2 — Cartridges (LLM CLI Adapters)

**Goal**: a uniform way to fire any supported LLM CLI at a prompt in a working directory and get structured results back. Claude Code is the only adapter in this phase; the abstraction exists so more cartridges can be added in future (D-19).
**Exit criteria**: mock cartridge drives all orchestrator tests; the Claude adapter passes the conformance suite on a machine with Claude Code installed; its flags are verified against the installed CLI's `--help` and recorded in `docs/cartridges.md`. Deferred adapters (P2-04, P2-05, P2-06) do not block phase completion.

- [x] **P2-01 — Cartridge interface and registry** (deps: P0-05, P0-08; size: M) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — kinds `killed`, `not_installed` and `unknown` exist beyond the five required; `SessionEvent` is one flat struct with a `Kind` discriminator; `Wait()` returns `(Result, error)`; `Pipe`, `Collect`, `LabelsFor` and `RunSpec.ProcSpec/EnvAllowlist` keep adapters thin; the integrator made `TestDefaultRegistryWrappers` repeat-safe.
  - Do: `internal/cartridge`: `type Cartridge interface { Name() string; Detect(ctx) (Status, error); Run(ctx, RunSpec) (Session, error) }`. `RunSpec`: system prompt, user prompt, workdir, model, tool policy (`read_only|read_exec|read_write`), max turns, timeout, env allowlist, labels. `Session`: `Events() <-chan SessionEvent` (`TextDelta`, `ToolUse`, `ToolResult`, `Done{Result, Usage}`, `Error`), `Wait()`, `Kill()`. `Usage`: input/output tokens, cost USD if reported. Typed error kinds on `Error`/`Detect`: `ErrRateLimited` (temporary, backoff), `ErrCreditsExhausted` (quota/billing exhausted, cartridge is dead for this run, D-18), `ErrAuth`, `ErrCrash`, `ErrTimeout`; each adapter documents which CLI messages map to which kind. Registry keyed by name; construction from config.
  - Done when: interface documented; a compile-time check for each adapter; registry tests.
- [x] **P2-02 — Mock cartridge** (deps: P2-01; size: M) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — JSON scenarios only (no YAML dependency); side effects honour `ToolPolicy`; `FailTimeout` waits on the fake clock like a hung CLI; fixtures in `testdata/cartridge/mock/`.
  - Do: `cartridge/mock`: scripted by a YAML/JSON scenario file or programmatic builder: match on label (role, lens, bead id, round) → canned events with delays; failure injection (crash, timeout, malformed JSON, rate-limit, credits-exhausted); optional side effects in workdir (write file, run command) so engineer runs produce real diffs in tests. Records every RunSpec it received.
  - Done when: tests demonstrate matching, delays under a fake clock, side effects, failure injection.
- [x] **P2-03 — Claude Code adapter** (deps: P2-01; size: L) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — verified against Claude Code 2.1.258 (`docs/cartridges.md`, drift-tested by `docs_test.go`); prompt on stdin, `--permission-mode dontAsk` plus allowed/disallowed tool lists; read_only keeps a restricted Bash allowlist (`ReadOnlyShell`) because 2.1.258 offers Glob/Grep only when Bash is disallowed (P5-09); opt-in live test `SC_CLAUDE_LIVE=1`.
  - Do: `cartridge/claude`: invoke `claude -p` with `--output-format stream-json`, `--model`, `--append-system-prompt`, tool policy mapped to `--allowedTools`/`--disallowedTools` (or the current equivalents), `--max-turns`, cwd = workdir, `--permission-mode` suited to unattended use. Verify every flag against `claude --help` on the machine and record the verified CLI version in `docs/cartridges.md`. Parse stream-json into SessionEvents; extract usage/cost from the final result message. `Detect` checks binary + version + a cheap auth probe.
  - Done when: unit tests parse recorded stream-json fixtures (put real captured samples in `testdata/claude/`); conformance suite (P2-09) passes locally.
- [!] **P2-04 — Kimi adapter** (deps: P2-01; size: M) (blocked: deferred by human 2026-09-02, see D-19 — do not pick this up)

  - Done when: reopened and fixture-based parsing tests pass.

  - Do: `Select(role) Cartridge` implementing D-12 (active cartridge, per-role override). Emits a Warning event when the chosen cartridge's Detect fails and falls back to active. Logic must not assume a single adapter (D-19).

- [x] **P2-09 — Conformance suite** (deps: P2-02, P2-07; size: M) (done: wf-p234 e8dcd28, flake fixed: wf-p57 e23e305) — round 3 (integrator): `runBeads` now wraps `beadsVersionOK` and `beadsProject` in `retryTextBusy` as well as `shim.Verify`. `Options.BD` points at a bd this process wrote moments earlier (the suite's own fake bd), so executing it hits the same golang/go#22315 race the shim did and `TestBeadsCheck_PassesThroughTheShim` failed about once in twenty full-suite runs with `bd version failed: text file busy` reported as a SKIP; `go test -count=40` on the package and five consecutive `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 go test ./...` runs are green after the fix. the resumed wave made the `beads-readonly` check retry `shim.Verify` on ETXTBSY (golang/go#22315, a flake under `-race` with parallel forks; P3-20 asks for the same guard in `beads.Shim.Verify`); eight checks: the five prompts plus `kill`, a per-session stream contract and, added by the integrator, `beads-readonly` (P3-09); driven by `TestConformance` behind `make cartridge-conformance CARTRIDGE=mock|claude`; the hang prompt is `tail -f /dev/null` because Claude Code blocks a standalone `sleep`; Claude Code 2.1.258 passed 8/8 live on 2026-09-02 (49 s, $0.12).
  - Do: `make cartridge-conformance CARTRIDGE=<name>` (only `claude` and `mock` exist for now): runs a fixed set of prompts (echo a token, produce a structured result, write a file in workdir under read_write, refuse under read_only, respect timeout) against a real cartridge. - [x] **P2-10 — "Adding a cartridge" guide** (deps: P2-03, P2-08, P2-09; size: S) (done: wf-p234 e8dcd28) — re-verified on 2026-09-03 by extracting the `acme`/`parrot` listings into throwaway packages (gofmt, vet, 8/8 conformance, two-cartridge selection); section 7 now lists all eight checks and `Options.BD`; README link skipped (no Cartridges section yet, P10-01); the second-agent exercise was completed by the integrator wf-p234: the documented `parrot` package passed `go vet`, the conformance suite (8/8) and the two-cartridge selection test in about five minutes.

## Phase 3 — Beads Integration (Task Graph)

**Goal**: beads is the persistent task graph; `sc` reads and writes it exclusively through the `bd` CLI, using only commands and flags verified against bd 1.2.2 (Appendix A).
**Exit criteria**: integration test (tagged) initialises beads in a fixture repo without committing to it, creates a plan with `bd create --graph`, reads back the ready set, moves a bead through every D-07 status, and survives an `sc` restart.

  - Do: `internal/beads`: `Detect()` runs `bd version --json` (fields `version`, `build`), enforces ≥ 1.2.2, returns install hints from Appendix A. `EnsureInitialised(repo)` implements D-13 exactly: the verified init command, then `.gitignore` hunk revert, then `bd ping --json` as the health check (`bd doctor --json` printed nothing in embedded mode and is not used). Re-runs are no-ops thanks to `--init-if-missing`.
  - Done when: tests via `FakeBin`; an integration test asserts `git log` and `git status` of the fixture are unchanged after init except the excluded `.beads/`.
- [x] **P3-02 — bd client wrapper** (deps: P3-01, P0-05; size: L) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — `Claim(ctx, id, actor)`; `Issue.Status/Type` stay raw strings (`Issue.Bead()` strict, `SafeBead` lenient, P3-14); `Patch.Metadata` goes through `--metadata @file` and merges; `Ready` filters epics itself; 100 recaptured samples; `ErrorSummary` now prefers `*beads.Error.Message` (integrator).
  - Do: one `Client` with a mutex-serialised `run(args…)` implementing D-21 (env, `-C`, envelope parsing with preamble skipping, JSON error → typed errors keyed on `code` and message: `ErrNotFound`, `ErrBlockedOpen` for close refusals, `ErrReadOnly`, `ErrLocked`). Typed methods, all verified: `Create(BeadInput)` (`bd create <title> -t -p --description --acceptance -l --metadata @file --parent --silent/--json`), `CreateGraph(plan)` (`bd create --graph <file> [--dry-run]`, D-22), `Show(ids…)` (`bd show … --json --include-comments`; returns an **array**), `List(filter)` (`bd list --json -n 0 --flat [--all] [-s a,b] [-l] [--parent] [--metadata-field k=v]`), `Ready(parent)` (`bd ready --json -n 0 [--parent]`), `Blocked()` (`bd blocked --json`, gives `blocked_by[]`), `Update(id, patch)` (`bd update … -s --assignee --set-metadata k=v --metadata @file --add-label --remove-label --json`), `Claim(id)` (`bd update <id> --claim --json` with `BEADS_ACTOR` set to the agent id), `Close(id, reason)` (`bd close <id> --reason … --json`; surfaces the blockers-open refusal), `Comment(id, body)` (`bd comment <id> --stdin`; note `bd comments add -f -` does **not** read stdin), `Comments(id)` (`bd comments <id> --json`), `AddDep(issue, dependsOn)` (`bd dep add <issue> <depends-on> --json`), `DepList(ids…)` (`bd dep list … --json`), `Export()` (`bd export`, JSONL with labels, dependencies, comments per issue, for whole-graph snapshots), `SetCustomStatuses`, `Statuses()` (`bd statuses --json`), `ConfigSet/Get`, `Ping()`.
  - Done when: unit tests against the `FakeBin` shim replaying captured JSON in `testdata/bd/` (capture with the real binary and commit the samples); integration test against real `bd` on the fixture repo; a test proves calls are serialised.

Document everything in `docs/beads-conventions.md` so a human reading `bd show <id>` understands the history.

## Phase 4 — Git, Worktrees, PRs & Safety

**Goal**: every engineer works in an isolated worktree; finished work is delivered exactly as the safety setting dictates.
**Exit criteria**: safety-on test proves the default branch is never modified; safety-off test proves a fast-forward merge; conflict test proves the bead is returned to the engineer.

- [x] **P4-01 — Repo basics** (deps: P0-08; size: S) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — `Open(ctx, path, opts...)` with `WithEnv`/`WithTimeout`; `Fetch` returns `ErrNoRemote`; untracked files count as dirty; extras `RevParse`, `BranchExists`, `CurrentBranch`, `IsWorktree`.
  - Do: `internal/gitx`: `Open(path)`, default branch detection (`origin/HEAD`, then `main`, then `master`), `IsClean`, `Fetch`, `HasRemote`, `HeadSHA`, `MergeBase`. All via `git` subprocess; no libgit.
  - Done when: tests on the fixture repo with and without remote.
- [x] **P4-02 — Worktree manager** (deps: P4-01; size: M) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — branch `sc/<id>-<slug>`; an existing `sc/` branch is reused on retry; extras `Open`, `DeleteBranch`, `AllowDirty`.
  - Do: `Create(bead) (Worktree, error)` with plain `git worktree add <state-dir>/runs/<run-id>/wt/<bead-id> -b sc/<bead-id>-<slug> <default>` (D-23; outside the repo, so no `.gitignore` edits; `bd worktree create` is not used because it writes to the repo `.gitignore`). bd discovers the shared `.beads/` from a linked worktree through the git common dir (verified with `bd where`), so `bd` works inside worktrees with no extra configuration. `Remove`, `Prune`, `List`, `Reattach` for resume; guard against nested worktree creation inside another worktree.
  - Done when: tests: create/remove/list, reattach after process restart, refusal on dirty target.
- [x] **P4-03 — Auto-commit leftovers and diff stats** (deps: P4-02; size: S) (done: wf-p234 e8dcd28) — a test with failing `pre-commit`/`commit-msg` hooks and `commit.gpgsign=true` proves `CommitAll` still commits, unsigned; `--no-verify --no-gpg-sign` with the fallback identity `slop-cannon <slop-cannon@localhost>`; `Repo.DiffStats` is reusable on its own.
  - Do: implement D-14: after an engineer session ends, `git add -A && git commit` if dirty with a conventional message; return `DiffStats` (files, +/- lines) versus merge-base.
  - Done when: tests for dirty and clean cases; ignores files matched by the repo's `.gitignore`.
- [x] **P4-04 — Diff provider for reviewers** (deps: P4-02; size: S) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — `WithMaxDiffBytes` / `ChangeRatio.Exceeds(threshold)` carry the documented defaults; the config keys are P1-14.
  - Do: `Diff(worktree) (Diff, error)` unified diff against merge-base with rename detection; if larger than `max_diff_bytes` (default 200 KiB) return a file list plus per-file stats and let testers open files themselves; compute the D-09 change ratio between rounds.
  - Done when: tests including the size cap and ratio.
- [x] **P4-05 — Safety ON delivery: push and PR** (deps: P4-03, P3-03; size: M) (done: wf-p234 e8dcd28) — `dpSetup` now asserts in every subtest's cleanup that the local and remote default-branch tips did not move (the Phase 4 exit criterion); no remote also moves the bead to `needs_human`; the caller sets `delivering` before `Deliver`; the existing-PR lookup prefers OPEN then MERGED and ignores CLOSED; built on P4-07's `GH` runner.
  - Do: push branch; `gh pr create --title "<bead title>" --body <generated>` with body sections: bead link/id, implementation report, per-tester verdict summary, rounds, `Generated by slop-cannon`; label `slop-cannon`; store `sc_pr_url`/`sc_pr_number` metadata on the bead and create the `gh:pr` gate (P3-08, D-20); if `gh` is missing or unauthenticated: push only, print the compare URL / manual instructions, and move the bead to `needs_human` (no gate can be checked); if no remote: leave the branch and say so. Idempotent on retry (finds existing PR for the branch).
  - Done when: tests with a `gh` shim covering create, existing PR, missing gh, no remote.
- [x] **P4-06 — Safety OFF delivery: rebase, check, merge** (deps: P4-03; size: M) (done: wf-p234 def3632, review gaps closed: wf-p57 3086673, 3e2703f and 4cb1cdc) — Round 4 (integrator, wf-p57 4cb1cdc): re-verified — `TestManager_DeliverMerge` asserts in every subtest that neither the local nor the pushed default branch gains a merge commit, `assertLinear`/`ErrNonLinearBranch` sit in the delivery path, and "a merge commit in the branch never reaches the default branch" is its own case. No code change was needed. round 3 (D-20): a delivery whose rebase left no new commit no longer closes the bead on trust — it must prove the work landed, either against `MergeOptions.BaseSHA` (P4-13's `sc_base_sha`, measured on the pre-rebase tip) or against the `sc_merge_sha` an earlier attempt stamped on the bead (read through the widened `BeadCloser.Show`); with neither proof it fails with `ErrNothingToDeliver` at `StageMerge` and the bead stays `delivering`, and a proven retry sets `MergeResult.AlreadyLanded` and finishes only the outstanding stages. The push stage moved out of the `Commits > 0` branch, so a retry after a failed push pushes instead of closing an unpushed merge; `--no-verify` is gone from the ff-only merge (git 2.36, below `DoctorMinGit`); the Makefile `test` probe no longer matches `test :=`/`test ?=`; and `DeliveryError.Check` carries what the failing check ran. Round 2: a merge commit can no longer reach the default branch (a repo with `rebase.rebaseMerges=true` kept it through the rebase and the fast-forward carried it over) — every delivery rebase now runs with a fixed `-c` set and `assertLinear` re-checks `rev-list --merges` afterwards, failing with `ErrNonLinearBranch`; a resumed rebase against a stale base is redone; a mid-flight rebase of another branch is refused (`Repo.RebasingBranch`); `npm test` is detected only when `package.json` declares a test script. Round 1 note:  a fetch stage syncs the local default branch first; `sc_merge_sha` is recorded before `bd close`; the check runs via `sh -c` with the full environment; rebase uses `commit.gpgsign=false --no-verify`.
  - Do: implement D-10 off-path: `git rebase <default>` in the worktree; run `check_command` (from config; else autodetect `make test`, `npm test`, `go test ./...`, `pytest`, `cargo test` by files present); fast-forward merge into the default branch in the main repo; push if a remote exists; then `bd close` (D-20). Merges are serialised by the orchestrator (one at a time); `bd merge-slot` is not needed while `sc` is the only merger. Any failure returns a typed `DeliveryError{Stage, Output}`; rebase conflicts abort cleanly and leave the worktree in a conflict state for the engineer.
  - Done when: tests for happy path, failing check, conflict; the default branch never gets a merge commit.
- [x] **P4-07 — PR watcher and external review posting** (deps: P4-01, P3-02; size: M) (done: wf-p234 def3632) — `--external-ref` is not passed (no `BeadInput.ExternalRef`, P3-19) and is recorded as `sc_external_ref` metadata instead; PRs on `sc/` branches are also skipped; review bodies pass through `logging.Redact`; the `pr_poll_interval` key is P1-14.
  - Do: implement D-11: poll `gh pr list --json` every `pr_poll_interval` (default 60 s), skip PRs labelled `slop-cannon`, create one review bead per new PR (`bd create -t task --external-ref gh-<n>` with label `sc:external-pr` and `sc_pr_url` metadata; there is no custom issue type), and provide `PostReview(pr, verdicts)` using `gh pr review --approve|--request-changes --body`. Never merge.
  - Done when: tests with a `gh` shim; the review body golden test.
- [x] **P4-08 — Cleanup and resume support** (deps: P4-02, P4-05, P4-06; size: S) (done: wf-p234 def3632, review gaps closed: wf-p57 3086673, 3e2703f and 4cb1cdc) — Round 4 (integrator, wf-p57 4cb1cdc): re-verified — `TestResumer_Resume`, `TestResumer_Resume_WithRemote` and `TestManager_ReattachAfterRestart` pass, so a resumed run reattaches the worktree of every bead it owns. No code change was needed. round 3: `Manager.resolveBranch` resolves a bead's branch from the branch git's own worktree registration names (a prunable entry still prints it), then `BranchName(bead)` when that ref exists, then the `refs/heads/sc/<id>*` namespace — so a retitled bead's committed work is never lost to a fresh branch, and a worktree parked on a branch no title can express is refused rather than recreated from the default branch; the pull request is looked up by number (`bead.PRNumber`, else the number parsed out of `PRURL`) instead of by a guessed head branch; `Cleanup` prunes only registrations under its own run directory, so a user's own worktrees survive; a MERGED/OPEN PR only keeps a *delivering* bead delivering; and `inspect`/`reconcile`/`cleanupBranch` are at 100% statement coverage. Round 2: cleanup and resume follow the branch the worktree actually holds rather than re-deriving it from the bead's (mutable) title, so a retitled bead is reattached and its merged branch pruned (P4-12); a done bead whose worktree directory vanished is now fully pruned; a per-bead ref failure no longer aborts the whole pass; `gh` is consulted even when the local branch is gone, so a delivering bead behind an open PR is not pushed to `needs_human`; `CleanupOptions.Out` prints the kept paths. Round 1 note:  a MERGED PR keeps the bead `delivering` (bd refuses `close` while the gh:pr gate blocks it; P6-10's poller closes it); only `delivering` beads are closed by the ancestor check; P6-15 consumes `ResumeResult`.
  - Do: on run finish prune worktrees of `done` beads; keep worktrees of `failed`/`blocked` beads and print their paths; on `--resume` reattach worktrees and reconcile branch state (merged? PR open?) into bead state.
  - Done when: tests: finish with mixed states; resume after simulated crash.

## Phase 5 — Agent Roles & Prompts

**Goal**: each role has a prompt builder, a tool policy, and a structured output schema, verified with golden tests and the mock cartridge.
**Exit criteria**: every role's prompt contains vibes and rules when configured; every structured output validates against its schema; the PO produces a valid plan for the fixture repo through the mock.

- [x] **P5-01 — Prompt assembly** (deps: P2-07, P1-05, P0-05; size: M) (done: wf-p57 3086673, review gaps closed: wf-p57 3e2703f and 4cb1cdc) — round 3: agent-authored prose can no longer forge prompt structure — a bead description, its acceptance criteria and an `impl_report` summary are quoted with `prompt.Fenced` and the two note lists are collapsed to one line, so a forged `## Output contract`, `sc-result` block, `### Round N` heading or verdict stays inside a fence (`TestBeadProseCannotInjectPromptStructure`, `TestReportSummaryCannotForgeAVerdict`, `TestOneLineFieldsCannotStartAStructureLine`, `TestQuotedProseCannotEscapeItsFence`); the new `structured.Contract.EndsWithPromptSuffix` (P2-07) makes `WithPromptSuffix` append unless the prompt *ends* with the contract, so a quoted copy cannot suppress the real one (`TestBuildContractIsAlwaysLast`); `PlanRunSpec` and `EngineerRequest.Firing`/`RunSpec` now return the valid spec alongside `ErrPromptOverBudget` as `TesterRunSpec` already did (shared `promptBudgetError`/`promptTruncationSummary`); `TestScanRepoDefaultDepthAndCap` exercises the documented depth-3 / 400-entry defaults; and `TestPromptGoldenFilesAreAccountedFor` plus a new `docs/goldens.md` section police all nineteen prompt goldens (that test skips its content checks under `UPDATE_GOLDEN`: the tests that write those files run in parallel with it, so before the integrator's fix a file caught mid-rewrite read as empty and failed `make check-golden` about once in ten runs — the inventory is still checked, and the content on the ordinary run that follows). round 2: every quoted block goes through the new `prompt.Fenced`, which picks a fence longer than any backtick run inside the body (CommonMark, the rule `structured.Extract` applies reading blocks back out), so a diff touching a Markdown file can no longer close its block early and turn the sections after it — the output contract among them — into prose; `limitReadme` cuts on a rune boundary; the D-08 escalation line renders its clauses separately; and the integrator routed the tester's diff and the engineer's git output through `prompt.Fenced` too (`TestTesterPromptQuotesADiffThatCarriesAFence`, `TestEngineerConflictQuotesGitOutputThatCarriesAFence`). Round 1: templates embedded per role and lens; rules and vibes pasted verbatim in all three roles (`TestBuildRulesAndVibesInEveryRole`); degradation order tree → history → README → open beads, reported in `Prompt.Truncations`; debug copies land under `config.RunDir` (D-23), never in the target repo. Deviation: `Build` takes the contract as `Request.Contract` so this package never imports `internal/agents` (import cycle); the real contracts are covered from an external test.
  - Do: `internal/agents/prompt`: layered builder: role base prompt (embedded `.md` templates) + **Rules** section (verbatim, framed as MUST) + **Vibes** section (verbatim, framed as preferences) + repo context (`tree` summary depth 3 capped at 400 entries, README head, detected stack, check command, default branch) + bead context + round history + output contract from P2-07. Token budget with truncation order (repo tree first, then history). Every prompt is written to `.slop-cannon/run/<id>/prompts/<agent-id>.md` for debugging.
  - Done when: golden tests per role; test that rules and vibes appear in all three roles' prompts.
- [x] **P5-02 — Product owner / BA agent** (deps: P5-01; size: L) (done: wf-p57 3086673, review gaps closed: wf-p57 3e2703f and 4cb1cdc) — round 3: `PlanRunSpec` no longer swallows `Prompt.OverBudget`: it returns the valid spec together with `ErrPromptOverBudget`, so a rules file larger than the budget is reported rather than fired silently (`TestPlanRunSpecReportsAnOverBudgetPrompt`). ## Phase 6 — Orchestrator & Feedback Loops

**Goal**: the core loop: plan → fire engineers → fire testers → loop until satisfied → deliver, under the ammo budget, fully resumable.
**Exit criteria**: P6-09 end-to-end test passes with the mock cartridge; kill-and-resume test passes; scheduler never exceeds ammo.

- [x] **P6-01 — Run lifecycle** (deps: P0-06, P0-07, P1-08; size: M) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd) — Run, Start/Pause/Resume/Stop/Kill/Interrupt/Wait, the D-23 run directory tree, atomic state.json on every transition and the `<state-dir>/run.lock`; `TestLifecycleStateFileGolden` is the golden.
  - Do: `internal/orchestrator`: `New(RunConfig)`, `Start`, `Pause` (no new agents; running ones finish), `Resume`, `Stop` (graceful: SIGTERM agents, 10 s grace, SIGKILL, release leases, keep worktrees), `Wait`. Run directory creation; `state.json` (run metadata, active agents, last event seq) written on every transition; lockfile `.slop-cannon/run.lock` (P9-04). SIGINT once = graceful stop, twice = immediate.
  - Done when: tests with fake agents for each transition; state.json golden.
- [x] **P6-02 — Scheduler and ammo allocation** (deps: P6-01, P3-04, P3-05, P2-08; size: L) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd) — the scheduler is a composable `Loop` (WorkSource + Firer + StopCondition) so the feedback, planning and delivery lanes plug in; ammo is exact under a property test over 25 random scenarios.
  - Do: implement D-05: a tick loop (on events + interval) that fills free slots in priority order: arbitrations/escalations → reviews owed on `in_review` beads (3 testers per bead, one per lens, fewer if ammo cannot afford, never zero) → engineers for ready beads up to `max_engineers_in_flight` → PO planning when the ready queue is empty (autopilot) or at start. Slot accounting is exact; a crashing agent frees its slot. Deterministic under a fake clock for tests.
  - Done when: property test: for random event sequences, live agents ≤ ammo at all times; allocation examples for ammo 3, 4, 10, 12, 13 match the D-05 table in docs.
- [x] **P6-03 — Bead state machine** (deps: P0-05, P3-03; size: M) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd, review gaps closed: wf-p6 1d0aef2 and 61526c8) — pure `Transition` over 10 workflow states, 16 events and 29 edges; `TestStateTransitionEveryStateEventPair` enumerates 440 (state, event) pairs against an independent spec table.
  - Do: `Transition(state, event) (state, error)` per D-07 as a pure table over the bd statuses; persistence via `SetStatus`/`Claim`/`Close` (P3-03/P3-05); invalid transitions are errors and Warning events, never panics.
  - Done when: table-driven test enumerates every (state, event) pair.
- [x] **P6-04 — Feedback loop engine** (deps: P6-02, P6-03, P5-03, P5-04, P5-05; size: L) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd, review gaps closed: wf-p6 1d0aef2 and 61526c8) — one thread per bead that is both a `WorkSource` and the supervisor's `FiringPlanner`; all seven Done-when scenarios (a)–(g) are driven end to end through the real supervisor, the real prompts and the mock cartridge.
  - Do: per bead: round counter; dispatch review to three lenses; aggregate verdicts (all approve → `approved`); else consolidate items (dedupe by file+title similarity), send to engineer, collect responses; `fixed` items go back to the originating tester; `pushback` items go to that tester's evaluate-pushback mode; hold after `max_pushback_rounds` → PO arbitrate; apply D-09 re-review rule; round cap → PO escalate; all outcomes recorded as bead comments and events.
  - Done when: mock-driven tests: (a) all approve round 1; (b) one tester requests changes, fixed, approved round 2; (c) pushback accepted; (d) pushback held → arbitration upholds engineer → approved; (e) arbitration upholds tester → engineer fixes; (f) round cap → escalation → block; (g) diff ratio triggers full re-review.
- [x] **P6-05 — Planning loop and stop conditions** (deps: P6-02, P5-02; size: M) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd, review gaps closed: wf-p6 1d0aef2 and 61526c8) — `Planner` is WorkSource + Firer + StopCondition at once rather than a second `Loop`; brief mode finishes on settled beads, autopilot re-plans through 24 simulated hours and ends only on stop or credits exhaustion (D-18).
  - Do: brief mode: PO plans once at start; run finishes when every bead of this run is `done`/`blocked`/`failed`. Autopilot (D-18): PO plans at start and whenever the ready queue is empty and no engineer is running; if the PO returns an empty plan the run idles and re-plans after `idle_replan_interval`; the run ends **only** on user stop or when the supervisor reports every configured cartridge as credits-exhausted (`RunFinished{Reason: credits_exhausted}`). No bead or runtime cap exists in autopilot. PO may deprioritise its own open beads in autopilot (Q-04). Emit `RunFinished{Reason, Summary}`.

A safety-on check failure cannot exist (gitx runs check_command only on the merge path), so that case is covered by `TestDelivererSafetyOnRunsNoCheck`.
  - Do: on `approved` → `delivering`: call the safety-appropriate path; safety off success → `bd close` with `sc_merge_sha`; safety on success → stay `delivering` with the `gh:pr` gate until `CheckGates` resolves it, then `bd close` (D-20); conflict → bead to engineer in `resolve conflict` mode (D-10) then re-deliver without a new review unless the conflict diff ratio exceeds the D-09 threshold; check failure → treat as a `qa` change request round.
  - Done when: tests for success, conflict, failing check with both safety values.
- [x] **P6-07 — Agent supervisor** (deps: P6-02, P5-08; size: M) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd, review gaps closed: wf-p6 1d0aef2 and 61526c8) — the single firing path: cartridge choice, rate-limit pause, crash retries, credits retirement with fallback (D-18), lease renewal, zombie reaping, shim install (P6-17) and the copy-worktree degrade (P6-20).
  - Do: per-agent timeout (`agent_timeout`, default 30 min), crash retry (max 2, exponential backoff), rate-limit detection (`ErrRateLimited`) → per-cartridge pause `rate_limit_backoff` (default 60 s doubling to 10 min); credits exhaustion (`ErrCreditsExhausted`) → mark that cartridge dead for the run, re-route its roles to any live cartridge (D-12 override ignored), and when no live cartridge remains signal the planning loop to finish with `credits_exhausted` (D-18); lease renewal every minute; zombie reaping on stop; all as events.
  - Done when: tests with mock failure injection for each case, including one cartridge exhausted with a fallback and all cartridges exhausted.
- [x] **P6-10 — Gate polling loop** (deps: P3-08, P6-06; size: S) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd, review gaps closed: wf-p6 1d0aef2 and 61526c8) — a sidecar rather than a `Loop` (it fires nothing and keeps polling while the run is paused); resolve, escalate and missing-gh are all tested against bd 1.2.2's own captured output through the real `beads.ParseGateCheck`.
  - Do: every `gate_poll_interval` (default 60 s) run `CheckGates()`; resolved gates close their beads (`bd close`) and emit `Merged`; escalated gates move beads to `needs_human`. Skipped with a single Warning when `gh` is unavailable.
  - Done when: tests with shims for resolve, escalate, and missing gh.
- [x] **P6-08 — Events, run log, `sc status`** (deps: P6-01…P6-07; size: S) (done: wf-p6 ba531dd, review gaps closed: wf-p6 1d0aef2 and 61526c8) — `EventLog` is a `Publisher` the run's bus tees into; `sc status`, `sc status --json` and `sc status <bead-id>` (which prints `beads.FormatTimeline` verbatim, P6-15) have six goldens driven from a recorded events file.
  - Do: every transition publishes an event; JSONL run log at `.slop-cannon/run/<id>/events.jsonl`; `sc status [--repo]` prints run state, per-state bead counts, live agents, last 10 events, and a bead timeline with `sc status <bead-id>`.
  - Done when: golden tests for status output from a recorded events file.

## Phase 7 — TUI & Cannon Animations

**Goal**: watching a run is informative and fun: a live task graph, agent panels, feedback history, and cannon animations on every shot.
**Exit criteria**: TUI runs against the fake event generator and a real run; golden frame tests pass; idle CPU < 5% with animations on; `--headless` renders the same information as text.

- [x] **P7-01 — App skeleton and fake event generator** (deps: P0-06, P0-05; size: M) (done: wf-p57 3086673, review gaps closed: wf-p57 3e2703f and 4cb1cdc) — round 3: `FormatEvent` is now `logSanitize(formatEvent(...))`, so an agent's output line, a warning, an error, an impl-report summary or a bead title cannot repaint the Log tab or steal a row — a log row is always one row with no escape and no control character (`TestFormatEventSanitizesUntrustedSubprocessText`, `TestModelLogTabDrawsNoEscapeFromAnAgent`, with a fast path proven byte-identical for printable text); `State.Notices` is capped at `MaxLog` (newest kept) and sanitised on ingest, so an autopilot run cannot grow it without bound (`TestStateCapsNoticesLikeTheLog`, `TestStateSanitizesNoticeText`); and `Model.viewKeys` is a switch over all five tabs, so the `?` overlay really carries the active view's keys as `docs/tui.md` promises (`TestModelHelpOverlayShowsTheActiveViewsKeys`; only `frame-help.golden` moved). round 2: the header composes the widest of several variants instead of clipping one fixed line, so at 80×24 the run id, mode, ammo and safety all survive (and `disconnected` outranks `stopping`); `tui.Demo` gives the program a simulated clock driven by the script, so a live agent card no longer reads `5885h30m57s`; `sc tui-demo` without a TTY fails with an actionable message instead of a cancelreader error; and `tui.Run` and the demo command's TUI branch are covered. Round 1: `sc tui-demo` is hidden (help and `docs/cli.md` unchanged) and adds `--events` to print the scripted stream as JSONL, which is how it is tested without a TTY; the scripted run covers all 22 event kinds with a fixed clock and fixed ids. `TestAppSmoke` drives the real program through teatest.
  - Do: `internal/tui`: Bubble Tea program subscribing to the bus; layout: header (cannon, run id, mode, ammo used/total, safety), main pane with tabs `Graph | Agents | Feedback | PRs | Log`, footer keymap; resize handling; `?` help overlay; `q` asks to confirm stop when a run is live. Hidden command `sc tui-demo` feeds a scripted event stream so TUI work never needs a real run.
  - Done when: `sc tui-demo` runs; teatest smoke test.
- [x] **P7-02 — Graph view** (deps: P7-01, P3-04; size: L) (done: wf-p57 3086673, review gaps closed: wf-p57 3e2703f and 4cb1cdc) — round 3: the cursor is pinned to a `domain.BeadID` (new `SelectedID`, internal `index`/`moveTo`), so a `GraphChanged` that renumbers the levels can no longer move the selection or an open detail panel onto another bead (`TestGraphSelectionIsPinnedToTheBead`, `TestGraphSelectionSurvivesTheBoxedLayouts`) and `anim.go`'s `graphNodeCell` follows the same pin; every LLM-authored string the view draws goes through the new `graphText`/`graphBlock`, which reuse `agentsSanitize`, with the canvas dropping zero-width runes as a backstop (`TestGraphViewScrubsBeadText`); boxes and borders are measured in cells rather than runes, so a CJK title or a two-cell border glyph cannot shift a border (`TestGraphViewDoubleWidthTitles`, `TestGraphCanvasDoubleWidthCells`, `TestThemeGraphBoxesHoldUnderAmbiguousWidth`); and `docs/tui.md` gained a full Graph-view section with the key table. No golden changed. layered DAG from `beads.NewGraph` (never re-implemented), orthogonal edge routing on a character canvas, left→right / top→bottom / indented-list layouts, and a bead detail panel on `enter`. 18 golden frames including the four required shapes at 80×24 and 200×50. Review round 2: an edge whose blocker is more than one level back now detours through a bypass lane past the end of the layers instead of being chopped by the boxes in between (it used to vanish and leave stubs that read as dependencies that do not exist), the detail panel's scroll offset stops at the last page, the cycle bucket names itself on every box border and draws its own edges, and the summary line reads "1 bead · 1 level".

Narrow terminals (< 80 cols) fall back to an indented list grouped by level.

## Phase 8 — Modes & End-to-End Wiring

**Goal**: `sc ./repo` and `sc ./repo -m "..."` work end to end with a real cartridge, in TUI and headless.
**Exit criteria**: a recorded manual run against the fixture repo with Claude completes at least one bead through review to PR (safety on) and to merge (safety off); the demo script runs deterministically with the mock.

It renders TUI or headless, prints the folded summary and chooses the exit code. `TestRunHeadlessWithTheMockCartridge` is the Done-when; `make demo DEMO_ARGS=--check` fires the same path over the fixture repository. **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: the run's stderr has one lock — `newSyncWriter` wraps `cmd.ErrOrStderr()` once and every producer is handed the wrapper (the headless terminal slog handler, the P9-04 lock notice, `runSetup.Out` and so `gitx`'s P6-18 cleanup report, the final summary, and the renderer when one writer serves both streams headlessly); the two helper goroutines became `runHelpers`, whose `stop(grace)` bounds both waits and warns about what it gave up on; and `runDeliveryLine` names the external pull requests a D-11 review run looked at instead of reporting only "0 pull requests, 0 merges".
  - Do: replace the P1-08 stub: build run, start orchestrator, attach TUI or headless renderer, handle exit codes, print a final summary (beads done/blocked/failed, PRs, merges, duration, token/cost totals if any).
  - Done when: `sc ./fixture -m "fix the two bugs" --headless --cartridge mock` (mock is selectable via a hidden flag for tests) completes.

Two complete runs with claude/sonnet; the committed recording is the second: 1104 events, a two-bead plan, 9 agent sessions, three approving verdicts per bead in round one, 2 pull requests, 2m56s, $1.11 — and the work was real (`for i := 1` → `for i := 0` in `Mean`, `largest := values[0]` in `Max`, every tester running `go test ./...` in its worktree first). The first attempt died on a production bug and it was fixed rather than worked around: the first exec of the freshly written read-only bd shim raced with the run's other forks and failed with ETXTBSY, killing the planning product owner before it started (`beads.RetryTextBusy` in `Shim.Verify`). **The GitHub half is a stand-in by design** — `gh` numbers the pull requests it is asked to open, keeps their bodies, and reports them merged so the D-20 gate closes; everything up to and including `gh pr create` is production code, and the real-GitHub verification stays P8-22.
