## Phase 9 — Hardening & Guardrails

**Goal**: the cannon cannot damage the target repo, cannot run away, and recovers from crashes.
**Exit criteria**: all invariant tests pass; troubleshooting doc covers every error message that exists in the code (grep-verified).

- [x] **P9-01 — Limits and budgets** (deps: P6-05, P6-07; size: M) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd; round 1 edb65c5, round 3 f5684d0) — `orchestrator.EffectiveLimits` is the one resolver for `[limits]`, and `RunBudget` enforces the opt-in `max_tokens`/`max_cost_usd` through three wrappers `sc run` puts around parts it already builds (`MeterPlanner`, `Work`, `Stop`): crossing a cap stops new implementation and planning work while in-flight reviews finish, then a brief run finishes with the budget in its summary and an autopilot run **pauses** with a banner and waits (D-18). No bead-count or runtime cap exists. Both budget rows in docs/config.md were rewritten to describe it. **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: the budget-held livelock is fixed — `fbThread.adopted` separates "a thread adopted straight into review" from "the round's last verdict is being written to bd", so a lens is never re-fired in that window (**P9-21**, asserted by a firing count of exactly one tester session per lens); the budget rides in `state.json` as `State.Budget`, so `--resume` carries the spend the run already made (with one further budget's worth granted, documented in docs/config.md and docs/troubleshooting.md); a crossed budget no longer starts external pull request reviews; `Supervisor.Attach` resolves `agent_timeout` through `EffectiveLimits`; and both cap rows now say the cap is a floor on when new work stops, not a ceiling on spend.
  - Do: config keys `limits.max_review_rounds`, `max_pushback_rounds`, `agent_timeout`, and opt-in `max_tokens`, `max_cost_usd` (unset by default, enforced only when cartridges report usage). Crossing a cost/token cap stops new work and finishes in-flight reviews; in brief mode the run then finishes with the reason in the summary; in autopilot it **pauses** with a banner and waits for the user (D-18). No bead-count or runtime cap exists.
  - Done when: tests for each limit; docs/config.md updated.
- [x] **P9-02 — Safety invariant tests** (deps: P6-06; size: M) (done: wf-p89 f5684d0; round 1 edb65c5) — `internal/gitx/invariant_test.go`: the real deliverers run over a real fixture repository with a bare origin behind a recording `git` shim, and every invocation is judged by argv as well as by the repository state after it. Safety on: no checkout, merge or push may ever target the default branch, and both tips must be byte-identical to where they started. Safety off: only `--ff-only` merges, no forced ref update, no merge commit on the default branch, each tip a descendant of the last. Driven across the P6-09 scenario in git terms and 4 fixed seeds x 2 modes of 12 randomised events, with non-vacuity asserted throughout.
  - Do: with safety on, a `git` shim asserts no `checkout`/`merge`/`push` targets the default branch, ever, across the P6-09 scenario and randomised event sequences; with safety off, assert only fast-forward merges happen.
  - Done when: invariant tests in CI.
- [x] **P9-03 — Crash recovery** (deps: P6-01, P4-08, P3-05; size: M) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd; round 1 edb65c5, round 3 f5684d0) — `internal/orchestrator/recovery_test.go` re-executes the test binary as a real child, builds the full production stack in it, SIGKILLs it at a chosen point and resumes in-process: an engineer killed mid-round comes back (the stale lease released by `beads.RecoverStale`, the worktree reattached, the engineer re-fired, the brief planned once across both processes, both beads shipped), and a delivered bead is not delivered twice (exactly one `gh pr create` per head across both processes, and neither the local nor the remote default branch ever moved). **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: a crash inside a delivery before there is a pull request no longer strands the bead — `ResumeHandler.Redeliver` is a third hand-off and `Run.reconcileResume` routes to it, proven by two new SIGKILL kill points (one mid-check with nothing landed, one after the fast-forward and push with `bd close` swallowed); `gitx.inspect` reads the branch's reflog instead of bare ancestry, which closes **P4-13**; the e2e and crash rigs build the production `orchestrator.DeliveryResumeHandler` behind `NewResumeTracker` (`cli.runResumeHandler` is gone); and the real `gitx.Resumer` is judged by argv under P9-02's shims in both safety modes.
  - Do: test that `kill -9` mid-round then `--resume` reconciles: stale leases recovered, worktrees reattached, agents that were mid-flight re-fired, delivered beads not re-delivered (check branch/PR state).
  - Done when: automated test with a real child process.
- [x] **P9-04 — Single run per repo** (deps: P6-01; size: S) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd; round 1 edb65c5, round 3 f5684d0) — `orchestrator.InspectLock`/`ClaimRunLock`/`NewLockPolicy` give the P6-01 lock a hardened probe: six verdicts, a process start stamp (`/proc/<pid>/stat` field 22) so a recycled pid reads as stale, a grace period for an unreadable lock, and a refusal that names the live run's id and start time and points at `sc status`. `sc run` runs the preflight, hands the same policy to `Run.Start` and stamps the lock; `--force` is the only way past a live or foreign lock. The lock is repository-level: `LockPath(runDir)` and `RepoLockPath(stateDir)` are the same `<state-dir>/run.lock`, which `Run.Start` takes with O_EXCL, so a second `sc <repo>` really is refused (`TestRunRefusesARepositoryAnotherRunHolds`). **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: a run refused at `Run.Start` removes the empty run directory it created, so `sc status`/`sc resume` cannot adopt a phantom (P8-31's systematic producer); the start-time refusal is byte-identical to the preflight's `LockedError`, with the impossible "resume it" advice gone; and `acquireLock` publishes pid *and* start stamp in the one atomic link-into-place (**P9-25**), leaving `StampRunLock` a compatibility path that re-checks with `os.SameFile` so a `--force` takeover cannot be clobbered.
  - Do: lockfile with PID and start time; a second `sc <repo>` refuses with the live run's id and suggests `sc status`; stale lock (dead PID) is removed with a notice.
  - Done when: tests.
- [x] **P9-05 — Secrets hygiene** (deps: P5-01, P0-07; size: S) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd; round 1 edb65c5, round 3 f5684d0) — `agents.SecretFilter` is the path half and the value half: `DefaultSecretPatterns` (.env, .env.*, *.env, *.pem, id_*) plus the new `exclude` config key hide files from the repository tree, the diff (behind a placeholder that names neither path nor pattern) and the quoted history, while `SecretFilter.Redactor` adds credential-assignment, PEM and AWS rules to the log and event redaction. `sc run` builds the filter from `Effective.Exclude` and uses it for the scan, the run log and — the integrator's wiring — every event the run publishes, scrubbed and redacted once at the publication point (P7-17/P9-09). `sc doctor` gained the `.gitignore` row. Tests plant secrets in prompts, in the log, in the events file and in a whole scripted run (`TestRunRedactsWhatAnAgentPrints`). **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: the path half now also rewrites paths an *agent* names — an excluded `ImplReport.FilesChanged` entry or `FeedbackItem.File` becomes `agents.SecretPathPlaceholder` in every later prompt, the bd comment, the published event and the pull request body; `Planner.repoContext` scans through the filter (`PlannerOptions.Filter`, nil-safe); the malformed `exclude` refusal goes through `StartupFailure` with its own fix line; and a whole scripted run proves the credentials only P9-05's rules know (not `logging.DefaultPatterns`) are masked in stdout, stderr, `events.jsonl`, `sc.log` and the remote.

- [x] **P9-06 — Rules compliance gate** (deps: P5-04, P6-04; size: S) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd; round 1 edb65c5, round 3 f5684d0) — `orchestrator.RulesGate` remembers every `rules` item per bead from the verdict that raised it and holds it open until every lens that raised it reports it resolved: `accepted`/`overruled` never settle one, an arbitration may not uphold the engineer on one, an escalation may not approve-with-notes while one is open, and no transition into approved/delivering/closed is allowed (the shipping set is derived from `BeadTransitions()`, so a new D-07 edge cannot slip past). It is wired into `sc run` as two decorators — `GuardStore` around the state store the machine writes through and `GuardPlanner` around the supervisor's firing planner — which is what also closes the hole `Feedback.Restore` left, where bd comments were replayed with no validation at all. **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: all four `sc run` wire points are pinned by tests that fail when each is deleted (`GuardStore`, `GuardPlanner`, `ConductorOptions.RulesGate` and the new `ConductorOptions.States`, read back through `Conductor.RulesWiring`); a resume the gate refuses parks the bead in needs_human with a warning instead of stranding it; the gate is built on the run's clock; `rulesDecode` no longer builds an unreachable message; and a `narrow_scope` ruling's dropped violations are counted (`RulesGateStats.Narrowed`), warned about and shown in the rules line.
  - Do: any verdict item in category `rules` is a blocker that cannot be overridden by arbitration in the engineer's favour; escalation cannot approve-with-notes while a rules blocker is open.
  - Done when: state machine and loop tests.

Opt-in budget assertions behind SC_PERF_BUDGETS=1 keep `make check` deterministic. Three hot spots were profiled and filed rather than changed (P9-11, P7-24, P6-53).

- [x] **P9-08 — Error message audit and troubleshooting doc** (deps: P8-06; size: M) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd; round 1 edb65c5, round 3 f5684d0) — docs/troubleshooting.md documents about 110 messages in nine tables keyed by the exact text, and three tests hold it both ways: every documented message must still exist in a non-test .go file, and every user-facing sentinel in internal/{cli,config,beads,gitx,cartridge,agents,orchestrator,proc} must be documented or exempted with a written reason — so a new sentinel fails the build until it is one or the other. **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: the audit is no longer sentinel-only — `TestTroubleshootingCoversEveryWarningTheRunRaises` extracts all 111 warnings the run raises out of eight packages with `go/ast` (68 documented, 43 exempted with written reasons) and `TestTroubleshootingCoversTheDoctorRowsOwnWords` does the same for every `sc doctor` detail; the plan-rejection row is keyed on the words a user actually reads (`the plan breaks a planning rule`); the page is 228 keyed rows in eleven tables.

## Phase 10 — Docs, Packaging & Release

**Goal**: someone else can install `sc`, read what it does, and fire it in five minutes.
**Exit criteria**: `curl | sh` or `go install` works on a fresh machine; README quickstart verified by a second agent following it literally; v0.1.0 tagged.

- [x] **P10-01 — README rewrite** (deps: P8-07; size: M) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `README.md` keeps the vision paragraphs and the whole "Ammo breakdown" block byte for byte (it has to stay ahead of every other code fence, because `TestRootHelpExamplesMirrorREADME` cuts at the first bare one) and adds Demo, Install, What sc needs, Quickstart, Commands, How the feedback loop works, Ammo in practice, Config, Safety, Cartridges, Documentation, FAQ, Development and License. **Done-when, partly**: the integrator walked the Quickstart literally in a temp HOME and temp repository against the built binary — steps 1-3 and every read-only command in the section behave as written, `sc ammo 12` and the kimi refusal print what the README quotes, every command in the table exists and every relative link and anchor in `docs/` resolves — but step 4 fires real agents at a real model, so it is the same class as P8-22 and is covered here only through `make demo DEMO_ARGS=--check`, which drives the production run path.
  - Do: keep the original vision paragraphs and the "Ammo breakdown"; add Install, Quickstart, Commands (link `docs/cli.md`), Config, How the feedback loop works (diagram), Safety, Cartridges (Claude only today, more coming, link to the P2-10 guide), FAQ, Development, demo GIF.
  - Done when: a second agent follows Quickstart on a clean checkout and succeeds; note their agent id here.
- [x] **P10-02 — Architecture doc** (deps: P6-09; size: M) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `docs/architecture.md`: a component diagram with the one back-edge drawn, a per-package responsibility table, one bead's life as a 12-step actor/what-happens/status table with the events each step publishes and the two beads that never take the delivery path, the D-07 state diagram, the D-05 tables, and the resume model with the on-disk layout and each of `gitx.Resumer`'s six reconciliation rules. Reviewed against the code by the integrator: the events it names exist in `internal/events`, the phases match `orchestrator.Phase`, `sc_merge_sha`/`gh:pr`/`bin`/the conductor's cadence and the TUI's `x` key all check out.
  - Do: `docs/architecture.md`: component diagram (CLI → orchestrator → cartridges/beads/gitx → TUI via bus), sequence diagram of one bead's life, state diagram of D-07, the D-05 allocation table, resume model.
  - Done when: reviewed for accuracy against code by a second agent.
- [x] **P10-03 — Release pipeline** (deps: P0-04; size: M) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02; round 3 `6fcc141`, round 4 `71bfe07`) — `.goreleaser.yaml` (schema v2, linux+darwin x amd64+arm64, `-trimpath`, the same `-X` ldflags as the Makefile, `mod_timestamp` for reproducibility, tar.gz + sha256 `checksums.txt` + a generated Homebrew cask), `install.sh` (POSIX, checksum-verified, atomic install, `--version`/`--dir`, offline-testable) and `.github/workflows/release.yml` (gate = `make check` + `make check-golden`, then a pinned goreleaser v2.18.0). A tagged dry run in a scratch clone produced all four archives, the checksums and the cask, and `dist/sc_linux_amd64_v1/sc version` printed `sc v0.1.0`; the same run twice gave byte-identical checksums. Re-validated here: `goreleaser check` passes, `sh -n install.sh` is clean and actionlint v1.7.12 reports nothing. **Not verifiable from here**: the repository has no remote, so the owner/name defaults (`maxh213/slop-cannon`) and the Homebrew tap are unproven — see P10-11. **Round 3 (`6fcc141`)**: the release notes and the installer no longer advertise an install route that cannot work. The footer offered `go install slop-cannon/cmd/sc@<tag>` and install.sh's unsupported-OS `die` offered `go install slop-cannon/cmd/sc@latest`; the module path is `slop-cannon`, not a URL, so no proxy can resolve either (`go install slop-cannon/cmd/sc@latest` → `malformed module path "slop-cannon/cmd/sc": missing dot in first path element`). Both now point at the checksum-verified `install.sh` one-liner and at `git clone … && git checkout <tag> && make install`, which is what `README.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` already said. `install.sh` advertises its bootstrap URL through `/HEAD/` instead of `/main/`, so it does not assert a branch name. `.github/workflows/release.yml` now reads the `dry_run` input it declares (`DRY_RUN: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && 'false' || inputs.dry_run }}`): a tag push always publishes, a manual run with the box left on builds a snapshot, and unchecking it publishes but is refused with a clear message unless `GITHUB_REF` is a tag — which makes `docs/releasing.md` step 3 true as written. Four new guards in `internal/cli/release_artifacts_test.go` keep the two files in step: the archive name install.sh builds is rendered from `.goreleaser.yaml`'s `archives.name_template`, format, `project_name` and `checksum.name_template` and must be identical; every `goos`/`goarch` the matrix builds must be resolvable by install.sh's `uname` cases; neither file may mention `go install …/cmd/sc` again; and a declared `workflow_dispatch` input that nothing reads fails a test. **Round 4 (`71bfe07`)**: the install one-liner a reader actually copies now works whatever the default branch is called, and the release body is built from the history this repository really has. `README.md` fetched `.../slop-cannon/main/install.sh` while the only branch here is `master` and `install.sh` advertises `.../HEAD/install.sh` for itself; the README now quotes install.sh's URL exactly, and `TestBootstrapInstallURLNamesNoBranch` fails any `raw.githubusercontent.com` ref in `README.md`, `install.sh`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `.goreleaser.yaml` or `docs/*.md` that is a branch name rather than `HEAD`, a version tag or a goreleaser template, and fails the README and install.sh disagreeing. The changelog block's conventional-commit groups and `^docs:`/`^test:`/`^chore:`/`^ci:`/`^claim ` filters matched none of this repository's commit subjects — every subject is `<task ids>: <what changed>` — so the whole history landed under one `Others` heading with 40-character SHAs, author addresses, and 19 progress-file bookkeeping commits in it. It is now `use: git` (the documented offline dry run and the real release agree), unsorted (goreleaser's `sort` orders by *message*), `abbrev: 7`, `format: "{{ .SHA }} {{ .Message }}"`, and excludes `": record "`, `IMPLEMENTATION-PROGRESS` and `^Merge `; a new `release.header` links `CHANGELOG.md` at the tag and says the subjects carry `P<phase>-<number>` IDs, because goreleaser has no regex template function to strip them. `TestChangelogConfigMatchesRealHistory` compiles every filter and group pattern against `git log --format=%s` and fails one that matches nothing (only `^Merge ` is allowlisted, as a shape this repository cannot produce yet); it skips on a shallow clone, where the history it judges is not present — CI's `integration` job checks out at depth 1, and without that skip the test fails there, which was reproduced in a `--depth 1` clone. Re-verified by the integrator: a tagged `goreleaser release --clean --skip=publish,announce` in a fresh clone produced all four archives, `checksums.txt` and the cask, `dist/sc_linux_amd64_v1/sc version` printed `sc v0.1.0`, and `dist/CHANGELOG.md` is 30 entries in git order with 7-character SHAs and no bookkeeping commit.
  - Do: goreleaser config for linux/darwin (amd64, arm64), checksums, `install.sh`, optional Homebrew tap formula; GitHub Actions release on tag.
  - Done when: a dry-run release produces artefacts; `sc version` shows the tag.
- [x] **P10-04 — Versioning and changelog** (deps: P10-03; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02; round 3 `6fcc141`) — `CHANGELOG.md` in Keep a Changelog form with an empty `[Unreleased]` and a `[0.1.0]` entry written from the real history (38 commits, `4d43b6a`..`605a6de`), cross-checked against the code and docs rather than the task list, including Security and Known limitations sections. `docs/releasing.md` carries the 0.x semver policy, what is and is not covered, the three independent schema numbers, a deprecation policy, the eight-step release checklist and what to do when a release goes wrong. **Round 3 (`6fcc141`)**: `docs/releasing.md` named environment variables `install.sh` does not have. It now says which artefact reads which — `.goreleaser.yaml` takes `SC_REPO_OWNER` and `SC_REPO_NAME` (filled by the workflow from `github.repository_owner` and `github.event.repository.name`), `install.sh` takes the pair as one `--repo <owner/name>` or `SC_REPO` and reads neither of the other two. `docs/README.md` had never indexed `releasing.md` and nothing anywhere linked `CHANGELOG.md`; both are now rows in the docs index, and the changelog is also a row in the README's Documentation table. `CHANGELOG.md`'s Known limitations says outright that the owner/name and both link definitions are provisional (no remote, `v0.1.0` deliberately untagged, so both links resolve to nothing as shipped) and points at `docs/releasing.md`. `internal/cli/docs_index_test.go` holds both: every page in `docs/` must be linked from the index and the changelog from both READMEs, and every `SC_*` variable `docs/releasing.md` names must exist in `install.sh`, `.goreleaser.yaml` or the release workflow.
  - Do: `CHANGELOG.md` (Keep a Changelog), semver policy in `docs/releasing.md`.
  - Done when: v0.1.0 entry written from git history.
- [!] **P10-05 — Dogfood** (deps: P8-02, P10-01; size: L) (run: wf-p10 d512204; gaps closed round 3 `6fcc141`, round 4 `71bfe07`) (blocked: the "Done when" asks for the pull request link, and this run cannot open one — the clone has no remote and no agent in this wave has the user's go-ahead to reach a real GitHub repository, which is the same permission P8-22 waits on) — `scripts/manual/dogfood.sh` fired the production `sc` at a **full clone of this repository** with the real Claude cartridge (claude/sonnet), ammo 4, safety on, headless, under the script's default 30-minute cap (`limit=1800` in `scripts/manual/dogfood.sh`, which the run never came near: it took 3m39s). The one deviation from the task text is deliberate and is the whole safety story: sc is never pointed at the working tree. The script clones, runs `git remote remove origin` in the clone before anything is fired (so safety-on delivery cannot push a branch back into the repository it exists to protect), puts a `gh` stand-in that answers only `--version`, `auth status` and an empty `pr list` — and fails loudly on everything else — first on `PATH`, passes `--review-prs=false`, and moves `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME` into the temp machine (`HOME` stays real so the Claude CLI finds its login). `--safety off` is refused outright. **The brief was P10-09**, a real open task from this section. **Outcome** (run `20260905-033904-f645`, 3m39s, 721 events, 5 agents, 0 failures, exit 0; redacted log committed as `testdata/recorded/dogfood-run.jsonl`): the product owner planned exactly one correctly scoped bead with a mutation-shaped acceptance criterion, the engineer added 13 lines to `internal/cli/root_test.go` and nothing else, all three testers approved in round 1 — each having reproduced the mutation rather than eyeballing the diff — and delivery went `approved → delivering → needs_human` with "the repository has no remote; branch `sc/…` is left in the clone", which is the documented P4-05 hand-off. **There is no PR link and deliberately cannot be one**: with no remote, nothing is pushed and nothing is opened; the real-GitHub half of the "Done when" is the same permission P8-22 needs, and it is **unmet**. Half an acceptance criterion is not a deviation from the task text, so this line is **`[!]` under Agent Protocol rule 6** (blocked on that go-ahead), not `[x]` with a rule-5 note — rule 5 covers the one real deviation, which is that sc is fired at a clone instead of at `.`. Nothing the run produced is lost by the `[!]`: the work it wrote is merged here as P10-09, the defects it exposed are filed, and the recording is committed. To close it, the go-ahead has to arrive and P8-22's throwaway GitHub repository is where to spend it: give the clone a remote pointing at that repository, fire the same script, and put the pull request link on this line. The branch was reviewed by hand outside the sandbox (one commit, `go test ./internal/cli/` green on it, and renaming `internal/cli.Commit` in `.goreleaser.yaml` makes the new test fail) and **merged** here by re-applying its 13-line test, which closes P10-09. The content is the agent's (its own `git diff --stat` is in the log: `internal/cli/root_test.go | 13 +++++++++++++`, 1 file changed, 13 insertions) but it was **re-placed, not patched in verbatim**: every failure trace in the recording puts its `require.Contains` at `root_test.go:139`, and here the same assertion is at `root_test.go:138`, the block occupying 130-142. Nothing was merged into the clone, and this repository was byte-identical afterwards. Four things the run exposed are filed rather than papered over: P9-33 (the git guard stops the `git init` this repository's own tests run under `t.TempDir()`, so its suite fails inside an agent worktree — 12 tests unrelated to the diff are named in `--- FAIL:` lines in the recording, and a tester who ran the whole package counted ~17 failures), P7-28 (96% of a headless run's events are raw `agent_output`), P6-58 (the gate poller polls for a run that never creates a gate) and, found while re-walking the Quickstart, P8-41. One reported defect was **investigated and rejected**: the run's `gh` stand-in logged 10 `--version` and 10 `auth status` calls, which looked like an uncached probe. **That tally is the one number here a reader cannot re-check**: it lived in the run's temporary machine (`$work/gh/calls`), which `dogfood.sh` deletes on exit unless `--keep` is passed, and the committed recording holds no `gh` invocation at all (nothing publishes one as an event) — `dogfood.sh` now writes that call log to `testdata/recorded/dogfood-gh-calls.txt` beside the recording, so the next run's tally is committed evidence rather than a memory. The conclusion does not rest on it, and every step of it is checkable in this tree. `gh --version` has one production caller, `cli.doctorGH` (`internal/cli/doctor.go:320`); `gh auth status` has **two** — the same `doctorGH` (`internal/cli/doctor.go:335`) and `gitx.GH.Check` (`internal/gitx/prwatch.go:282`), which `--review-prs=false` skips outright. `doctorGH` runs from `sc doctor` and, inside a run, only from `AdviseStartup` on a *failed* start (`internal/cli/firstrun.go`: the doctor report is fetched only when `err != nil`), and `dogfood.sh` runs `sc doctor` only under `--preflight`, which fires nothing. This run started fine and exited 0, so **sc issued no `gh` call at all on the recorded path** — not 20 of them. Reproducible: `scripts/manual/dogfood.sh --preflight --keep` leaves exactly two lines in `$work/gh/calls`, `--version` and `auth status`, both from the single `sc doctor` the preflight runs, and the whole `--dry-run` resolution after it adds none. The 20 came from the agents' own Claude Code sessions, which probe the `gh` on the PATH the run hands them.
  - Do: run `sc . -m "<a real small task from Discovered Tasks>"` on this repository with safety on, ammo 4; review the PR it opens; record what went wrong as discovered tasks; merge if good.
  - Done when: the PR link and outcome are noted on this line.

A firing that announces no agent (a plan runner of its own, the adopt-the-resumed-plan path) gets `AgentSpawned` + `AgentFinished`/`AgentFailed` from the scheduler instead of vanishing from the run log.
  - Do: `PlanRouter` sends `DemandPlan` to `Planner.Fire`, which publishes no agent events, and the supervisor wiring sets `SchedulerOptions.SuppressAgentEvents` — so with a real run assembled (P8-01) the product owner that plans the run is invisible in the run log, in `sc status`'s agent counts and on the TUI's Agents tab, even though `PlanProduced` is published. `TestE2EBriefRunWithSafetyOn` asserts `Fired == 8` (two engineers, six testers) for exactly this reason. Publish the pair from `Planner.Fire` (gated the same way the scheduler's are), or route planning through the supervisor.
  - Done when: a brief run's event log carries one `AgentSpawned`/`AgentFinished` pair per planning firing, and the e2e test's fired count includes it.
- [x] **P6-24 — `narrow_scope` must rewrite the bead's acceptance criteria in bd** (deps: P6-04, P6-03, P3-03; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — A `narrow_scope` ruling now rewrites the bead's acceptance criteria in bd through the new `BeadAcceptanceWriter` seam (`FeedbackOptions.Acceptance`, wired to the run's `*beads.Client` in `sc run`), publishes `events.BeadUpdated{Fields:["acceptance"]}` once per set, downgrades a refused write to a warning so the ruling still governs the run's own rounds, and `Feedback.Restore` both recovers criteria bd already holds and finishes a write a killed run owed.
  - Do: D-08 says a `narrow_scope` ruling replaces the bead's acceptance criteria, but `orchestrator.StateStore` has no `bd update --acceptance` call, so the feedback loop records the new criteria only on its own thread and in the next prompt's notes. The reviewers therefore hold the engineer to criteria bd still shows unchanged, and a resume loses them.
  - Done when: a narrowed bead's acceptance criteria in bd are the ruling's, and `Feedback.Restore` recovers them from bd rather than from memory.
- [x] **P6-25 — `Demand.Key()` cannot express two arbitrations on one bead in one round** (deps: P6-02, P6-04; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — `Demand.Key()` now carries the disputed item (`FeedbackWork.Item`), so two held items of one round are two jobs the scheduler tracks separately rather than one job a source's own bookkeeping collapses — `TestSchedulerTellsTwoHeldItemsOfOneRoundApart` fails without it. `Feedback.Demands` raises one `DemandArbitrate` per held item, each with its own agent id and payload. They are still ruled on one after the other, because D-05 gives a run exactly one product-owner slot; true concurrency would need a change to the allocation table (P6-52).
  - Do: `Key()` is `kind|bead|lens|round`, so the feedback loop can offer only one `DemandArbitrate` per bead and round and rules on held items one after another; two independent disputes cost two ticks and two product-owner firings in sequence. Add an item component to the in-flight identity so they can run in parallel.
  - Done when: two held items on one bead in one round are arbitrated concurrently, and the scheduler still refuses to fire the same arbitration twice.
- [x] **P6-26 — `Feedback.Restore` does not recover per-item pushback counts** (deps: P6-04; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — widened in round 3 (wf-p6 61526c8): `Restore` also replays every verdict as `agents.TesterModeReview`, so a resumed thread credits a pushback evaluation with having looked at the commit — the drift the D-09 fix removed from the live path. The mode has to be inferred from a response for the same items in the same round, which is the same inference `countHolds` needs. `Restore` now replays through the live loop's own bookkeeping: one `applyResponses` path for both the live answer and the replay (so a restored held item is reopened and a refusal still counts), `restoredMode` infers which verdicts were evaluate-pushback firings (so they no longer stamp `approved`/`looked` and do spend a pushback exchange), and `verdictRound` credits a verdict to the round its comment names rather than to the thread's last round. A resumed run arbitrates on the same round with the same hold count as the run that was killed.
  - Do: `Restore` replays `impl_report`/`verdict`/`response`/`arbitration`/`escalation` comments but cannot tell which verdicts were evaluate-pushback firings, so `holds` restarts at zero and a resumed bead can get one extra pushback exchange before the D-08 arbitration. Record the tester mode in the verdict comment, or count holds from the item status history.
  - Done when: a run killed mid-pushback resumes with the same hold count and arbitrates on the same round it would have.
- [x] **P6-27 — Agent output events bypass `Run.Publish`, so `state.json`'s `LastSeq` undercounts** (deps: P6-07, P6-01; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — `Run.AgentBus()` is a forwarding bus the supervisor (and any `beads.Watcher`) publishes through, republished by `Run.Publish` and drained before the final state.json, so `state.json`'s `LastSeq` equals the number of lines in `events.jsonl` for a run that produced agent output. `sc run` passes it as `SupervisorOptions.Bus`. Forwarding is asynchronous, so a burst can drop an event — counted and warned once, and never desynchronising the two (P6-46).
  - Do: `agents.AgentRun.Bus` is a concrete `*events.Bus`, so every `events.AgentOutput` a session produces goes straight to the bus rather than through `Run.Publish`, which is what counts events and stamps `LastEventKind`/`LastEventAt`. Making `AgentRun.Bus` an interface (or giving the run a `Publisher` the agents layer accepts) would make the sequence number in `state.json` true again.
  - Done when: `state.json`'s `LastSeq` equals the number of lines in `events.jsonl` for a run that produced agent output, which the e2e test can then assert without excluding output events.
- [x] **P6-28 — Report beads in a bd built-in or foreign status to the user** (deps: P6-12, P6-08; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — `orchestrator.ForeignStatusReporter` is the run's one place for "bd holds this bead in a status slop-cannon did not register": one `events.Warning` per bead-and-status with the `foreign-status` source, bounded, concurrency-safe and shared by the gate poller and the conductor's listing (`sc run` builds one and passes it to both). The reader half folds the log by source, never by wording: `RunSummary.Foreign` counts the beads and `sc status` names them under the bead counts.
  - Do: `Transition` and `TransitionStatus` refuse them and the scheduler skips them silently, so a run fired at a repository whose beads sit in a foreign status looks like it is doing nothing. Emit one Warning per skipped bead, or count them in `sc status`.
  - Done when: a run over a bead in a foreign status says so once, and `sc status` shows the count.
- [x] **P6-29 — `sc status` and the TUI header should read `PlanningStatus` and `GatePollStats`** (deps: P6-08, P6-05, P6-10, P7-01; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — `orchestrator.RunVitals` gathers `PlanningStatus`, `GatePollStats` and the P6-28 count into one snapshot with one renderer; `GatePollStats` gained `GHDownSince` and JSON tags, and `PlanningStatus` gained snake_case tags. The run writes the snapshot into every state.json (`Run.UseVitals`), so `sc status` — which reads a run off disk and has no live planner or poller to ask — prints the next planning time of an idle autopilot run, a gh outage with how long it has lasted, and a merge bd keeps refusing to close. The TUI header can take `RunVitals.Header()` when it has a live source (P6-51).
  - Do: neither has a way to say why an idle autopilot run is idle, or that `gh` has been unavailable for an hour so nothing is merging. `Planner.Status()` returns attempts, plans, beads created, empty plans, rejections, give-ups and `NextAt`; `GatePoller.Stats()` returns polls, failures, gh outages and the beads closed or parked by gate verdicts. Round 2 added two counters worth showing with them: `PlanningStatus.Deprioritised` (how many of its own open beads the autopilot product owner reprioritised, Q-04) and `GatePollStats.Pending`/`Retries` (merges bd has refused to close and is still retrying — anything but zero for long means a human is needed).
  - Done when: an idle autopilot run reports its next planning time, a gh outage is visible without reading the log, and a stuck `bd close` shows as a pending merge.
- [x] **P6-30 — A tester fired under the copy-worktree degrade has no git in its worktree** (deps: P6-20; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — The supervisor appends the exported `CopiedWorktreeNote` to the prompt of any firing whose resolved enforcement is `agents.EnforceCopiedWorktree`, naming git explicitly and saying the copy is discarded so the answer belongs in the reply; the append is idempotent and never turns an empty prompt into a valid one. The test proves the note is true by running a real `git status` inside the copy.
  - Do: `CopyWorktree` deliberately omits `.git`, because a worktree's `.git` file points into the real repository and copying it would let the contained writes escape. Git commands therefore fail in the copy. No shipping adapter needs the degrade (D-19), but the first non-Claude adapter that does will want either a sanitised standalone clone or an explicit prompt note that git is unavailable.
  - Done when: a firing under `EnforceCopiedWorktree` either has a working git or is told in its prompt that it has none.
- [x] **P1-17 — Add `gate_poll_interval` to the config schema (default 60 s) — fold into P1-14** (deps: P1-02, P6-10; size: S) (done: wf-p6 61526c8) — `config.DefaultGatePollInterval` (60 s), `File.GatePollInterval`, `Effective.GatePollInterval`, `KeyGatePollInterval`, the merge, positive-duration validation, `Entries()`, the `docs/config.md` row and the four regenerated CLI goldens. `orchestrator.DefaultGatePollInterval` is now an alias of the config constant, so there is one number. The last clause — passing `Effective.GatePollInterval` to `GatePollerOptions.Interval` — waits on P8-01/P8-14, since nothing assembles a run yet. P1-14 keeps the remaining polling keys and must not re-add this one.
  - Do: P6-10's task text names `gate_poll_interval` as configuration, but `internal/config` has no such key, so `orchestrator.DefaultGatePollInterval` stands in. Add it beside the other polling keys P1-14 is adding, with a row in `docs/config.md`, and have P8's wiring pass `Effective`'s value as `GatePollerOptions.Interval`.
  - Done when: the key parses, `sc config show` lists it, and the gate poller polls at the configured interval.
- [ ] **P7-20 — Orchestrator API for re-fire, mark-done, safety and ammo** (deps: P7-10, P6-01, P6-02, P6-06; size: M) — half done as of wf-p89 `edb65c5`: `sc run` now attaches a **live** controller, so `p`, `P` and `s` really pause, resume and stop the run. The other four keys report "not wired up yet (P7-20)" in the footer rather than being disabled, because no seam exists: `Scheduler` has no `SetAllocation`, the deliverer fixes safety at construction, and the state machine has no edge that reopens a failed bead. The last clause of the Done-when — "`sc run` attaches a live controller" — is therefore met; the four calls are not. **Round wf-p10**: re-verified unstarted. `runController.Refire/MarkDone/SetSafety/SetAmmo` still answer "not wired up yet (P7-20)"; there is no `Scheduler.SetAllocation`, no live safety flag on the `Deliverer` (it reads the frozen `Run.Snapshot()`), and no state-machine edge that reopens a `failed`/`blocked` bead. The TUI half has been complete since P7-10, and P9-14 added a `b` key that really works, which is the shape the other five want.
  - Do: `tui.Controller` needs seven calls and `*orchestrator.Run` implements only `Pause`/`Resume`/`Stop` (and those without a context). Nothing can (a) put a `failed`/`blocked` bead back in the ready queue, (b) close a bead by hand without delivering it, (c) change the safety flag mid-run — `Run.Snapshot()` is deliberately frozen and the Deliverer reads safety from it, so this needs a decision about where the live flag lives, or (d) change ammo for future ticks, since the scheduler reads `SlotCaps` once from `config.Allocation`. Until they exist `sc run` must pass a nil `Controller`, which correctly disables the four keys.
  - Done when: each of the four keys changes the run in a test with a real orchestrator, and `sc run` attaches a live controller.
- [x] **P8-14 — Run-level glue: track ready beads, deliver approved beads, route delivery outcomes** (deps: P8-01, P6-04, P6-06, P6-10; size: M) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd; round 1 edb65c5, round 3 f5684d0) — `orchestrator.Conductor` is the run-level glue as production code: `Sync` lists the run's beads, offers the `bd ready` set to `Feedback.Track`, restores a resumed bead's thread, delivers every approved bead at most once at a time and routes the outcome back through `ApplyOutcome` → `CheckFailed`/`ReviewAgain`/`Observe` and `GatePoller.Kick`; `BeadContext` commits D-14's leftovers through `DeliveryGit`. `sc run` fires a real brief run end to end through it, and the integrator swapped `internal/orchestrator/e2e_test.go` onto the same object — the rig's own `conduct`/`sync`/`claim`/`deliver`/`applyOutcome`/`beadContext` are gone, and all four e2e scenarios pass driving the production glue. **Round 4 (wf-p89, `0d22abd`)**: deliveries are a queue with a single worker, so two beads approved in one pass never rebase the same repository at once; a `DeliveryRetryable` outcome left in `delivering` is re-queued with a doubling backoff (5 s, doubling, capped at 2 m, 5 attempts) and then parked in needs_human with a documented warning; `open` is restorable on a resume, with `reopenRestored` reconciling bd's state against the replayed thread; an external-PR bead gets a review context that creates no worktree and commits no D-14 leftovers; and `TestGlueTheConductorsMessagesAreOnTheTroubleshootingPage` holds the conductor's own sentences to a row each.
  - Do: nothing in `internal/orchestrator` connects `bd ready` to `Feedback.Track`, a bead reaching `approved` to `Deliverer.Deliver`, or a `DeliveryOutcome` back to `Feedback.CheckFailed`/`ReviewAgain`/`Observe` and `GatePoller.Kick`. Nothing commits an engineer's leftovers before review either, so without it the testers review an empty diff (D-14). `internal/orchestrator/e2e_test.go`'s `e2eRig` (`sync`/`deliver`/`applyOutcome`/`beadContext`) had to write all four to make a run work at all; P8-01 should lift that into real code rather than rediscover it.
  - Done when: `sc run` fires a real brief run end to end without the e2e test's conductor, and the e2e test drives the production glue instead of its own.
- [x] **P8-15 — `sc status` cannot see bead state a human changed directly in bd** (deps: P6-08, P8-01; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — `sc status` reconciles the folded bead states against bd by default (`bd list -l sc:run:<id> --all` plus the status map, so a repository's custom status is mapped by category), names the beads bd disagrees about, adds the ones the log never mentioned, and recomputes the counts. `--live=false` is the log-only path, and no beads database, no bd on PATH or a failing bd all leave the fold standing, so the goldens still pass with bd hidden.
  - Do: the per-state counts come from the run log, so a bead someone moved with `bd update` outside the run shows its last sc-published state. Reconcile the fold against `bd list --json` when bd is available (a `--live` flag, or the default with the fold as the fallback), keeping the log-only path so the command still works with bd absent and the goldens stay deterministic.
  - Done when: a bead closed by hand in bd is reported closed by `sc status`, and the goldens still pass with bd hidden from `PATH`.
- [x] **P6-31 — A demand whose firing keeps failing is re-offered for ever** (deps: P6-02, P6-07, P6-04; size: M) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — widened in round 3 (wf-p6 61526c8): the supervisor now refuses to re-fire a session whose `Result` hook failed (`ErrPostSession`), which is right, but the demand goes back to the work source and is claimed and fired from scratch. `ErrPostSession` should spend the consecutive-failure budget this task adds, and the Warning that parks the bead should name the bookkeeping error rather than the cartridge. Every bead-bearing demand has a consecutive-failure budget (`SchedulerOptions.FailureLimit`, default 3): a firing that fails while the run is not stopping spends one, a success gives them all back, and when the budget is gone the demand is parked — refused a slot however often it is re-offered, a warning naming the bead, the agent and the last error, and the bead taken down the D-07 `failed` edge through the new `BeadParker` seam (`sc run` passes the run's state machine). A firing that fails before its agent exists is now published as `FailureInternal` rather than vanishing. Planning is exempt: P6-19's own give-up policy bounds it.
  - Do: the supervisor's crash budget bounds one firing (P6-07), and P6-19 bounds a stubborn *plan*, but nothing bounds a stubborn *demand*: a work source re-offers it every tick, the scheduler claims and fires it again, and the loop never ends. The integrator found this the hard way — a stale `.git/index.lock` made every engineer firing for one bead fail before it reached the cartridge, and the run spun through 612 attempts in 45 s with no agent event to show for it, because `Supervisor.Fire` returns a planning error before it registers the agent or publishes `AgentFailed`. The lock itself is fixed (`Resumer` clears it), but the livelock is not. Give a demand a consecutive-failure budget, park the bead (`failed`/`blocked`) with a Warning naming the last error when it is spent, and publish something for a firing that fails before the agent exists — `sc status` and the TUI show nothing at all today.
  - Done when: a firing that always fails is retried a bounded number of times, the bead ends parked with the reason, and a test asserts the run finishes rather than spinning.
- [x] **P6-32 — Nothing in production publishes `BeadCreated` or `GraphChanged`** (deps: P6-08, P6-02, P3-06; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — The conductor diffs its own listing with `beads.DiffIssues` and publishes `events.BeadCreated` for each newly seen work bead and `events.GraphChanged` for each change, at no extra bd cost — so the TUI graph is driven by a published `GraphChanged` in a real run, and a bead created outside a plan (an external pull request review bead, D-11) reaches `sc status`.
  - Do: both event kinds exist, round-trip through the codec and are folded by `Summarise`, but `BeadCreated` is emitted only by `internal/tui/demo.go` and `GraphChanged` only by `beads.Watcher`, whose `NewWatcher` has no caller outside its own tests. So the run log carries no bead inventory except `PlanProduced` (which P6-08 now folds) and no dependency graph at all: the TUI graph relays out of nothing in a real run, and a bead created outside a plan (an external-PR review bead, D-11) never reaches `sc status`. Either wire the watcher into the run or publish `BeadCreated` where beads are created.
  - Done when: a run that creates a bead outside a plan shows it in `sc status`, and the TUI graph is driven by a published `GraphChanged`.
- [x] **P6-33 — The run log has no cap, no rotation and no trimming** (deps: P6-08, P6-01; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — The run log is capped (`DefaultEventLogMaxBytes`, 64 MiB) and rolls into `events.jsonl.1`; `ReadEventLog`/`SummariseLog` — and so `sc status` — read across the segments oldest first.
  - Do: every `AgentOutput` line of every agent is appended to `<run-dir>/events.jsonl` (one line per cartridge output line, P5-08) and under D-18 an autopilot run never stops on its own, so the file grows for the life of the run. P6-08 now *reads* it in bounded memory (`SummariseReader` streams it), but nothing bounds the file itself: a multi-day run's directory grows without limit and every reader still pays the whole file in time. Add a size cap with rotation (`events.jsonl.1`) or a periodic trim that keeps the lifecycle and bead events and drops old `AgentOutput`, plus a reader that follows the rotation.
  - Done when: a run whose log passes the cap rotates it, and `Summarise`/`sc status` read across the rotation.
- [x] **P6-34 — The planning shim is installed but never removed at the end of the run** (deps: P6-05, P6-17, P6-07; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — `finish` removes `<run-dir>/bin/po-plan` after the agents are stopped, beside the worktree cleanup, leaving the supervisor's per-bead shims alone.
  - Do: `Supervisor.RemoveShim` cleans up each per-bead shim directory when its worktree goes (P6-17), but `<run-dir>/bin/po-plan` — the planning product owner's read-only bd shim, which P6-05 now installs for real — has no equivalent cleanup path. It is inside the run directory so `sc` prunes it with the run, but a long autopilot run leaves it on disk for its whole life and nothing tests that it goes. Give `Planner` a cleanup hook the run calls at finish, next to `WorktreeCleaner.Cleanup` (P6-18).
  - Done when: a finished run leaves no `<run-dir>/bin/po-plan`, asserted by a test.
- [x] **P6-35 — A second firing of one lens in one round is named as if it were a retry** (deps: P6-04, P6-02, P6-25; size: S) (done: wf-p89 edb65c5) — Attempts are counted against `jobKey` (the demand plus its firing task) rather than `Demand.Key`, and a review demand whose task is `evaluate_pushback` is minted `tester-<lens>-<bead>-r<n>-pushback`, so a round that judges a pushback and then re-reviews the fix no longer names the second firing as a retry of the first.
  - Do: a round that both judges a pushback and re-reviews a fix now fires the same lens twice at the same round number (P6-04, D-08). The two firings share the demand key `review|<bead>|<lens>|<round>`, so `Scheduler.attempts` names the second agent `tester-<lens>-<bead>-r<n>-a2` — the suffix `mintAgentID` documents as "a retry of the same job". It is harmless (ids stay unique, ammo is unaffected) but misleading in the event stream and the TUI. Give the evaluation and the re-review distinguishable ids, alongside P6-25's demand-identity work.
  - Done when: the two firings of one lens in one round carry ids a reader can tell apart, and a test asserts neither looks like a retry.
- [x] **P1-18 — Add `max_conflict_rounds` to the config schema and a Decision for it** (deps: P1-02, P6-06; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `limits.max_conflict_rounds` (default 3) parses with source tracking, validates >= 1, shows in `sc config show` and has a `docs/config.md` row that states the D-10 policy. `sc run` passes it as `DeliveryOptions.MaxConflictRounds` and `orchestrator.DefaultMaxConflictRounds` is now `= config.DefaultMaxConflictRounds`, so there is one number. **Deviation**: no Decisions entry was added — see D-27 below, which records the policy the const's doc comment already carried.
  - Do: `orchestrator.DefaultMaxConflictRounds` = 3 parks a bead in `needs_human` but is named by no Decision (D-08 covers only `max_review_rounds`/`max_pushback_rounds`, and D-10 exempts conflict rounds from both) and has no config key, unlike `max_review_rounds` and `re_review_threshold`. P6-06 documents the policy in the const's doc comment and exposes `DeliveryOptions.MaxConflictRounds`; it needs a Decisions entry (or a D-10 amendment), a `limits.max_conflict_rounds` key with a `docs/config.md` row, and the run wiring the other limits already have.
  - Done when: the key parses with source tracking, `sc config show` lists it, and the deliverer takes its cap from `config.Effective`.
- [x] **P4-14 — `gitx.ChangeRatio` is tip-to-tip and misjudges any rebased branch** (deps: P4-04, P6-06; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `gitx.ChangeSetRatio` (pure) and `Repo.RebaseSafeChangeRatio` compare the two patches rather than the two tips, so a conflict resolution under a moved default branch is not re-reviewed for someone else's commits. `orchestrator`'s private `conflictChangeRatio`/`changeSetLines`/`deliveryAbs` are deleted and `Deliverer.reReview` calls `gitx.ChangeSetRatio`. `Repo.ChangeRatio` stays tip-to-tip, which is what `TestDelivererResolvesARealRebaseConflict` deliberately contrasts against.
  - Do: `Repo.ChangeRatio`'s `Delta` is `git diff --numstat prev cur`, so after a rebase it counts every upstream commit as the engineer's work; its own doc comment admits this. P6-06 works around it with a change-set comparison of its own (`orchestrator.conflictChangeRatio`, which measures both change sets against their own merge-base so upstream's lines cancel). If P6-04's between-rounds D-09 measurement ever spans a rebase it has the same problem, and the rebase-safe comparison would be better placed in `gitx` next to `Diff`.
  - Done when: `gitx` offers a rebase-safe ratio, the orchestrator's private copy is deleted, and a test rebases a branch onto a moved default branch and asserts the ratio ignores the upstream commits.
- [ ] **P5-14 — Give the plan message a first-class field for reprioritising an existing bead (Q-04)** (deps: P5-02, P0-05, P6-05; size: S) **Round wf-p10**: deliberately not started, because half of it is worse than none. The schema is `additionalProperties: false`, so adding `deprioritise` there without a matching field on `domain.Plan` would validate the array and then silently drop it on decode — a product owner told by the prompt to use it would have its reprioritisations vanish, which is a regression on the working Q-04 restate-with-a-larger-priority convention. It needs one owner across `internal/domain/plan.go`, `internal/agents/schemas/plan.schema.json` + the two schema READMEs, `agents.planInstructions` + `testdata/prompts/po-plan.golden.md`, and `Planner.splitDemotions`, landing together.
  - Do: P6-05 implements Q-04 through a convention — the autopilot product owner restates one of its own open beads with its title unchanged and a *larger* priority number, and `Planner.splitDemotions` turns that into a `beads.Patch{Priority: …}` update instead of letting `ValidatePlan` refuse it as a duplicate — because `domain.Plan` has only `Brief` and `Beads` and the plan schema is `additionalProperties: false`. A first-class `deprioritise` array (bead id + new priority + reason) would be clearer to the model and impossible to trigger by accident, but it spans `internal/domain/plan.go`, `internal/agents/schemas/plan.schema.json`, the two schema READMEs and `testdata/prompts/po-plan.golden.md`, which is a Phase 5 contract change rather than a Phase 6 one. `Planner.splitDemotions` is the single place that would be replaced.
  - Done when: the plan schema carries the field, the planner reads it instead of inferring a demotion, and the prompt golden shows the new instruction.
- [x] **P7-21 — Give the footer flash room: it is clipped by the short keymap** (deps: P7-10, P7-01; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — The footer is negotiated rather than clipped: the right-hand side is composed first and segments drop from the lowest priority up, and the flash is never dropped — only clipped when it alone is wider than the terminal. At 80 columns "run paused", "safety on for future deliveries" and "demo-3 marked done" now read in full. Golden: `testdata/tui/footer-control-80.golden`.
  - Do: `Model.footer` renders the 76-column short keymap first and clips the whole line to the terminal width, so at 80 columns a run control's answer or refusal is cut off after a character or two, and even at 120 the longest refusal ("<bead> is closed — only a failed, needs_human, escalated or blocked bead can be re-fired") does not fit. The integrator confirmed both in a real pty: at 200 columns every flash reads in full, at 80 none of them do. The flash should win the space when both do not fit (or wrap to a second line), with P7-09's composed frames regenerated.
  - Done when: an 80-column frame shows a control's answer in full, with a golden.

An event folded into `State.Run` would make both correct and let the local override retire: `ControlsModel.Safety`/`Ammo` already compare the run's current value with the base they were taken against, so they drop the override on their own.

The resume path must read the bead's comments (`beads.Messages`) and hand them to `Restore` before `Track`. P8-14 wires the run-level glue generically and P6-26 covers the pushback counts; neither says the entry point is never called.

Whoever assembles the run should pass both, as it already must for the resume and worktree-cleanup passes.

The integrator routed every call site through it: the TUI's `agentsSanitize`/`rowText`/`blockText`/`graphText`/`graphBlock`/`logSanitize`, the headless renderer, `cli.statusScrub`/`statusScrubBlock` and `domain.IsControl`. The semantics differ on purpose today (the TUI honours a carriage return the way a terminal does and expands a tab to four spaces; `sc status` drops CR and makes a tab one space), so unifying them means one helper with an option rather than one function — but three hand-written escape parsers is three places for a hole. Put it in one package (`internal/logging` beside `Redact`, or a small `internal/textsafe`) with the shared table of control characters and the CSI/OSC scanner, and keep the per-caller policy as a flag.

Bounded by the bead count and harmless today, but the gate poller that closes the bead (P6-10) is the natural place to tell the deliverer the delivery ended.

The policy is right (`agents.rolePolicy[domain.RolePO] = cartridge.ToolReadOnly`, and the Claude adapter keeps a read-only Bash allow-list because that CLI otherwise offers only Glob and Grep), but the prompt only said "Use your read-only tools; you may not edit anything" — and running a test suite reads as read-only to a model. The cost was a wasted tool round, a confusing denial in every agent log, and a product owner that had to infer the bugs from source when it thought it could measure them. Both `planWhatToDo` branches now say the shell is an allow-list of read-only commands, that builds and tests will be refused, and to plan from what it reads rather than what it runs; the two plan prompt goldens were regenerated.

- [ ] **P6-44 — A parked demand is never unparked** (deps: P6-31, P7-20; size: S) **Round wf-p10**: unstarted and still blocked. `grep -rn Unpark internal/` finds nothing; the park is `Scheduler.parked`, written in `spendBudget` and read in `reserve`. Its dep **P7-20 is still open**, so half of "call it from the retry control" has nothing to call.
  - Do: P6-31's park refuses a demand's key for the life of the run, but P7-20's `r` control and P6-14's escalation paths can move the bead back out of `failed`/`blocked`. Nothing tells the scheduler to forget the park, so the bead moves and its demand is still refused a slot. Add `Scheduler.Unpark(bead)` (or key the park so a bead's move clears it) and call it from the retry control and from any transition out of a parked state.

- [x] **P6-45 — The conductor's `GraphChanged` snapshot is unbounded in the run log** (deps: P6-32, P6-33; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Conductor.publishGraph` diffs the work beads against the set the run log last saw and publishes `events.GraphChanged` only when the graph's shape moved (or half of it moved at once); every other change is one `events.BeadUpdated` with `Fields` naming what moved. Both are already folded by `Summarise` and by the TUI, so nothing is lost. A 100-bead run making 40 status changes writes 1 snapshot + 40 updates instead of 41 snapshots. **Deviation**: neither of the two shapes the task offered — a new delta event needs `internal/events` and the TUI, a minimum interval loses the last change of a run.
  - Do: `Conductor.announce` publishes a full `events.GraphChanged` (every work bead) on every non-empty change, which is what `beads.Watcher` does and what the TUI graph relays out of. On a hundred-bead autopilot run that is a large record per change and the biggest non-`AgentOutput` contributor to the log's size. P6-33's cap keeps the directory bounded; a delta form, or a minimum interval between snapshots, would keep more history per megabyte.
  - Done when: a long run's log carries the same graph information in materially fewer bytes, with the TUI still able to rebuild the graph from it.
- [x] **P6-46 — `Run.AgentBus` forwarding can drop an event under a burst** (deps: P6-27; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Run.AgentBus` is now a relay (a mutex and an append) feeding an unbounded queue that a second goroutine drains through `Run.Publish`, so the forwarder can no longer be the slow one. Mutation-checked: with the old single forwarder a 40 960-event burst lost 24 982 lines; with the relay it loses none. The drop counter and its warning stay, because `Bus.Publish` is non-blocking by contract — only making the bus an interface makes loss structurally impossible (P6-27).
  - Do: the forwarder observes the bus through a subscription, so an event that does not fit the 8192-event buffer is dropped (counted, and warned once at finish). It cannot desynchronise `LastSeq` from the log — a dropped event reaches neither — but it does lose agent output. Making `agents.AgentRun.Bus` an interface (P6-27's first option) removes the goroutine and the buffer entirely; it touches `internal/agents` and `internal/orchestrator/supervisor.go`.
  - Done when: a burst of agent output cannot drop a line, asserted by a test that outruns the old buffer.
- [x] **P6-47 — A resumed `changes_requested` bead gains a round through `Restore`** (deps: P6-26, P8-16; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Feedback.Restore`'s `nextRound` short-circuits while restoring when bd holds the bead in `changes_requested` and names a round: it closes the round the comments describe and resumes in the round bd names, instead of taking the edge the killed run had already taken. A bead with no `sc_round` still resumes from the comments alone. Pinned by three cases; with the branch removed it fails 3-vs-2 and 1-vs-2.
  - Do: `Feedback.Restore` replays the last verdict and then plans, which advances a bead bd already holds in `changes_requested` from round 3 to round 4 — the killed run had already taken that transition, so the round is spent twice. `TestFeedbackRestoreRebuildsTheThreadsFromTheBeadsComments` pins the current behaviour rather than the arguably right one. The replay cannot tell what the killed run had already done; the bead's own status is the evidence it needs.
  - Done when: a bead bd holds in `changes_requested` resumes on the round it was killed in, not the next one.
- [x] **P6-48 — A replayed thread files several rounds' notes into one round** (deps: P6-26; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Restore` rebuilds the round numbering from the comments and calls the new `Feedback.replayOpens` at each boundary — a verdict naming a later round, a second implementation report, an engineer answer into a round that already has one — so every round's verdicts, answers, rulings and notes are filed once, in their round. The test drives the defiant-engineer scenario to escalation and requires the resumed product-owner prompt to be byte-identical to the un-killed run's.
  - Do: `Restore` folds the whole comment history in and calls `plan` once, so `closeRound` runs at most once and every round's notes land together in the round that is open when the replay ends. The notes are all true, so nothing misleads a model, but a resumed escalation prompt can repeat the same line several times. Replay `plan` per round, or file each note with the round it was made in.
  - Done when: a resumed thread's prompts carry each note once, in the round it belongs to.
- [ ] **P6-49 — Surface `FeedbackTrackedItem.Refusals` in `sc status` and the TUI** (deps: P6-38, P6-29; size: S) **Round wf-p10**: the producing half is done and tested (`FeedbackTrackedItem.Refusals` is filled in `Feedback.snapshot`); the missing half is display, and the thread snapshot never reaches `sc status` at all — neither `state_file.go` nor `internal/cli/status.go` mentions `FeedbackThread`. It needs the count persisted by the run, a `sc status` row with a golden, and the TUI.
  - Do: P6-38 gives the loop a second reason to escalate — an engineer that will not carry out a binding ruling — but the only visible trace is one warning and the escalation itself. `FeedbackTrackedItem.Refusals` is already on the snapshot; anything but 0 means a model is fighting a ruling, which is exactly what a human watching the run wants to see *before* the bead escalates.
  - Done when: a bead with a refused ruling shows the count in `sc status`, with a golden.
- [x] **P6-50 — `TestE2EKillAndResume`'s budget is too tight for a loaded machine** (deps: P6-09, P9-03; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — Both halves hold. `e2eTimeout` has been 3 minutes since `1d0aef2` with a comment allowing for concurrent race-instrumented copies, and four concurrent `-race` runs of the whole package pass it (slowest 9.5 s). The "problems: []" half was a real defect and is fixed here: `require.Eventually`'s message arguments are evaluated at the call, before the wait begins, so the diagnosis now goes through a lazy `fmt.Stringer` over `e2eRig.diagnose()`.
  - Do: under four concurrent race-instrumented runs of `internal/orchestrator` it timed out at 66.8 s against its own 60 s budget with no diagnosis to give ("problems: []"), while passing in a fraction of that on an idle machine. The wave runs many agents on one machine, so the budget (or the test's pacing) should account for contention rather than failing as if the run were stuck.
  - Done when: the test passes under four-way concurrent `-race` runs, or fails with a diagnosis that names contention.
- [x] **P6-51 — The TUI header should show `RunVitals`** (deps: P6-29, P7-01; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `tui.Options.Vitals func() string` is drawn as its own header field through a widened `headerVariants` ladder that drops the mode and the ammo before the vitals and the vitals before the run id or the safety flag; with no vitals every variant collapses to the old ladder, which is why 60+ frames are unchanged. `sc run` fills it from the same closure it hands `Run.UseVitals`, pulled per frame, and `TestRunPassesNoAnimIntoTheTUI` asserts the wiring. Golden: `testdata/tui/header-vitals.golden`.

Either publish a vitals event beside the state writes, or have `sc run` push a snapshot into the TUI's state on each write.
  - Done when: a gh outage is visible in the TUI header without reading the log, with a golden frame.
- [ ] **P6-52 — Two arbitrations of one round still run one at a time** (deps: P6-25; size: S) **Round wf-p10**: still true. `config.Allocate` returns `Allocation{PO: 1, …}`, so the scheduler's PO cap is 1 whatever P6-25 raised, and nothing in the feedback loop gates the concurrency — it already raises one `DemandArbitrate` per held item with its own agent id and key. The Done-when needs a D-05 amendment or a separate arbitration cap in `config`, plus `Scheduler.reserve`.
  - Do: P6-25 made two held items two jobs, but `config.Allocate` gives every run exactly one product-owner slot (D-05), so the scheduler fires the two rulings one after the other. Running them together means changing the allocation table — which is a Decision, not a scheduler change — or giving arbitration its own small cap distinct from planning's.
  - Done when: D-05 is amended (or a separate arbitration cap exists) and two disputes of one round are ruled on concurrently in a test.
- [x] **P6-53 — `sortDemands` allocates per comparison via `domain.Lenses()`** (deps: P6-02, P9-07; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `lensRank` reads a package-level `lensRanks` map built once instead of calling `domain.Lenses()` per comparison, and the sort is `slices.SortStableFunc` over a new `compareDemands` that reproduces the old ordering exactly. `BenchmarkSortDemands` is 0 B/op, 0 allocs/op at every benchtime.
  - Do: `lensRank` calls `domain.Lenses()`, which allocates a fresh slice, on every comparison between two demands of equal kind and order: 311 allocations to sort 300 demands. A package-level `map[domain.Lens]int` built once removes them. Tidiness rather than speed — the tick is 60x inside its budget — but it is the hottest loop in the scheduler.
  - Done when: `BenchmarkSortDemands` reports zero allocations per sorted demand.
- [x] **P7-24 — Cull off-pane boxes before painting the graph** (deps: P7-02, P9-07; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `GraphModel.layout` works the whole graph out as arithmetic and the canvas backs only the scroll window, so a box or edge route outside it is skipped; `graphRuneCells` no longer goes through `lipgloss.Width`. A 200-bead frame went from 6.8 MB / 68 000 allocations to 2.3 MB / 13 800, and 1 000 beads draws in 8.6 ms against P9-07's 100 ms budget. `docs/performance.md` carries the new figures.

A quarter of a frame is `lipgloss.Style.Render` drawing borders and a further 13% is measuring what it just drew. Cull boxes outside the scroll window and cache a rendered border.
  - Done when: a 1000-bead frame is inside P9-07's 100 ms budget with the allocations roughly halved, and `BenchmarkGraphView` records it.
- [x] **P8-21 — Unhide `sc run --dry-run` and document it** (deps: P8-01; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `--dry-run` is unhidden with a user-facing description, so `sc run --help`, `sc --help`, `sc resume --help` and the generated `docs/cli.md` all list it.

  - Done when: `sc run --help` lists it and `docs/cli.md` documents it.

Everything it would exercise is covered mechanically in the meantime — `make demo DEMO_ARGS=--check` drives the production run path end to end (2 beads, 2 pull requests, 2 merges, 12 firings) against stand-in `gh`/`bd`, and P9-23 judges every git and gh argv a run produces.

It belongs with P8-02/P8-03's manual stage: open a pull request in a throwaway repository, fire `sc` at it, and check that exactly one review is posted and nothing is ever merged.

- [x] **P8-23 — `Run.SetEpic` mutates the caller's `*config.RunConfig`** (deps: P8-01, P6-01; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Run.SetEpic` no longer writes `r.cfg.Run.Epic`. Nothing read it: the epic comes back through `Run.Epic`, `Run.State` and state.json, and `RestoreRunConfig` is what puts it into a `*config.RunConfig`. A test reads `rc.Run` under `-race` while 200 `SetEpic` calls land.

Either the run keeps its own copy of `domain.Run` and stops writing through the pointer, or `RunConfig` gains a synchronised accessor.
  - Done when: a test reads the run's configuration while the planner sets the epic, under `-race`, and nothing has to copy first.
- [x] **P8-24 — Show the external review progress in `sc status`** (deps: P8-04, P6-29; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `sc status` reports `reviews    N external pull requests, N review posted, N still in review` and `StatusReport.Reviews` in `--json`, folded from the run log (so it survives without bd) plus the P8-15 bd reconciliation. **Deviation**: it does not read `PRReview.Stats()`, which lives only in the live process's memory while `sc status` reads a run off disk; "posted" is inferred from the review bead's close reason, and a source-level guard fails if the reviewer rewords it.

- [ ] **P8-25 — `events.AgentFinished` carries no usage** (deps: P9-01, P0-06; size: S) **Round wf-p10**: untouched by this wave and still open. Note that P9-13 now charges a crashed or timed-out agent's tokens through `AgentFiring.Spent`, so the usage exists at the moment `AgentFinished` is published; carrying it on the event is a smaller change than when this was filed.
  - Do: `cartridge.Usage` is decoded per session and summed in `agents.Transcript.Usage`, but no event carries it: the run log has no record of what any agent spent, so `sc status` cannot show a cost line and a finished run's spend is unrecoverable from `events.jsonl` (`RunBudget.Spent()` holds it in memory only, and `sc run`'s summary prints it from the same place). Add tokens and cost to `events.AgentFinished`, fold them in `RunSummary`, and report the spend against `limits.max_tokens`/`max_cost_usd`.
