- [x] **P8-26 — The e2e `gh` shim must remember the pull requests it opened** (deps: P9-03, P6-15; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `e2eFakeGH` is the package's single gh stand-in and now has a head->number and a number->head registry and OPEN-before-MERGED, so the crash-recovery subtest takes the resume's genuine OPEN branch end to end — entry `PRState == "OPEN"`, bead still `delivering`, `RedeliverPR`, the gate the crash never created, and the poller closing the bead. `recFakeGH` is deleted.
  - Do: `internal/orchestrator/e2e_test.go`'s `gh` shim answers `gh pr list` with `[]` for ever, so a resumed run that legitimately re-delivers a bead still in `delivering` (`PRDeliverer.Deliver` → `FindPR` → `CreatePR`) opens a duplicate pull request. That makes the "killed with the pull request still open" branch of P6-15/P9-03 untestable at the orchestrator level: a failure there would be the shim's, not the product's. Give the shim a head→number registry and a `pr view` that reports OPEN before MERGED.
  - Done when: a crash-recovery test covers the `RedeliverPR` path end to end.
- [x] **P8-27 — A supported way to drive `sc` against a stand-in bd** (deps: P8-01, P3-09; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — The requirement is now written down beside `beads.Shim.Verify` under "What a stand-in bd owes this check" — `bd version --json` with a non-empty version, and bd's own `--readonly` refusal, neither of which a run ever asks for — with `testutil.BDVerifyPrologue` as the ready-made fragment. `scripts/demo/demo.sh` points at it, and `TestTheDemosBDStandInSatisfiesTheShimSelfCheck` lifts the demo's own wrapper out of the script and runs the real self-check over it, so a new probe fails there and names itself. Mutation-checked.
  - Do: `cli.runShimOptions` (the `beads.ShimOptions{SkipVerify: true}` seam the CLI tests use) is unexported, so any non-test driver of a fake bd — the demo, a future rehearsal mode, a docs recording — must additionally implement `bd version --json` and bd's own `--readonly` refusal to get past `beads.Shim.Verify`. The demo does exactly that and is arguably better for it, but the requirement is undocumented and was found only by running it. Either document it beside `beads.Shim.Verify` or give the CLI an env-gated equivalent. The demo also has to append to the exported `config.KnownCartridges`, which would break silently if that list ever became a function.
  - Done when: the requirement is documented (or a supported seam exists) and `scripts/demo/scdemo` uses it.
- [x] **P8-28 — The planner fires a second product owner the instant a plan lands** (deps: P8-01, P6-02, P6-19; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Planner` carries a watermark set at every moment a plan comes into force, and `Demands` refuses the autopilot planning demand while the scheduler's `Fired` counter has not moved past it — `SchedulerStatus.Quiet` is the last dispatch's verdict, and the plan's beads have not reached the work source yet. `idle_replan_interval` is the backstop, so a plan nothing can pick up still re-plans on the D-18 schedule.
  - Do: seen in every P8-03 autopilot run — `po-plan-1`'s plan applied at 19:09:44 and `po-plan-2` was fired at 19:09:44, three seconds before the first engineer. `Planner.Demands` guards autopilot re-planning with `st.ByRole[engineer] > 0` and `!st.Quiet && !st.Idle`, but `SchedulerStatus.Quiet` is the *last* dispatch's verdict: on the tick after a plan lands no engineer has started yet and the previous dispatch fired nothing, so the run still looks quiet and the planning demand is offered again. That is a whole extra product-owner session at full model cost on every plan, and its plan is a duplicate by construction (same repository, beads not yet in flight), which feeds P8-29. Likely fix: a watermark — record `st.Ticks`/`st.Fired` when a plan is applied and refuse the demand until the scheduler has dispatched at least once since.
  - Done when: a test fires a plan and asserts no second planning demand on the following tick.
- [x] **P8-29 — Autopilot re-plans and re-ships work it has already shipped** (deps: P8-03, P5-02, P6-02; size: M) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — The planning request lists the last 20 closed beads after the open ones in autopilot, each rendered with its state, plus a note saying they are finished and delivered and that the repository may still show the problem because a delivered bead can be waiting on a human merge. Brief mode is excluded deliberately. **Deviation**: the second clause ("a manual autopilot run shows no duplicate pull request") needs the real CLI and is left with P8-22.
  - Do: the headline finding of the P8-03 runs. In a 33-minute run: 18 plans, 23 beads and 18 pull requests for about four distinct pieces of work, with the `Mean` off-by-one planned five separate times; in the committed 10-minute run, three pull requests re-fix the same one line and `git show` proves each branch re-applies the identical change. Two causes compound: (i) `Planner.openBeads` skips `domain.BeadStateClosed`, so the "Open beads / Do not propose work that duplicates any of these" block never mentions finished work and `agents.DuplicateOpenBeads` can only catch duplicates of *open* beads — the product owner has no memory of what the run already did; (ii) with safety on, D-10 never touches the default branch, so the repository it reads still contains every bug that has been fixed and merged in a pull request. Likely fix: show the product owner a bounded list of recently closed beads, marked closed, and say in the prompt that they are done. **A human should read this before leaving autopilot running on a real repository.**
  - Done when: a second plan over a repository whose first plan has shipped proposes none of the shipped work, asserted by a test, and a manual autopilot run shows no duplicate pull request.
- [x] **P8-30 — The pull request body carries neither the implementation report nor the tester verdicts** (deps: P8-01, P6-06, P4-06; size: S) (done: wf-p89 d5f3fbf) — every pull request the P8-02/P8-03 runs opened said "_No implementation report was recorded._" and "_No tester verdicts were recorded._" over a bead three testers had just approved: `gitx.PRBody` renders `PRRequest.Report` and `.Verdicts`, `DeliveryRequest` carries both, but `Conductor.Deliver` — the only production caller — filled neither, and `FeedbackThread` exposed neither. The snapshot now publishes the newest implementation report and each lens's latest verdict in lens order (D-09: an approval given in round one still stands, and the tester is not fired again to repeat it), read back out of the prompt history that `closeRound` moves them into the moment a round ends — which is exactly when a bead is approved and delivered. Two tests plus the negative control, and the demo's own pull request bodies now carry the report and all three lenses.
  - Do: fill `DeliveryRequest.Report`/`.Verdicts` in `Conductor.Deliver` from the feedback thread, exposing them on `FeedbackThread` first.
  - Done when: a run's pull request body shows the implementation report and every lens's verdict.
- [x] **P9-11 — Coalesce `state.json` writes: one flush per dispatch** (deps: P6-01, P9-07; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `AgentStarted`/`AgentUpdated`/`AgentStopped` mark the state dirty and flush once, at most `StateFlushDelay` (50 ms) later; every lifecycle transition, `Run.Close` and the new exported `Run.FlushState` still write synchronously. `BenchmarkSchedulerTickCold` went from 3.9 ms to 0.70 ms, measured both ways, and the recovery suite still resumes.
  - Do: `Run.AgentStarted` and `Run.AgentStopped` each call `persistLocked`, which marshals the whole run state and writes it atomically. A cold tick firing 50 agents writes the file 50 times on the dispatch goroutine (and 50 more as they finish), each marshalling a list one agent longer: 67% of the 3.9 ms cold tick by profile, and quadratic in agent count. Mark the state dirty and flush once per dispatch (or debounce on a short timer); `state.json` is a crash-recovery record, not a transaction log.
  - Done when: `BenchmarkSchedulerTickCold` is around 1 ms and the recovery test still resumes.
- [x] **P9-12 — A budget-ended brief run reports the finish reason of a user stop** (deps: P9-01, P0-06; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `events.FinishBudgetReached` ("budget_reached") beside `FinishCreditsExhausted`; `RunBudget.Stop` finishes a brief run with it, `tui.ExitCodeFor` maps it to `ExitOK` (a cap doing what it was set to do is not a failure), and the end-to-end test asserts `"finish_reason": "budget_reached"` out of `sc status --json`.
  - Do: `RunBudget.Stop` finishes a brief run that crossed `limits.max_tokens`/`max_cost_usd` with `events.FinishStopped`, because none of the four reasons means "over budget" and `FinishCompleted` would tell `sc status` the beads settled. The reason is only in `Outcome.Summary`, so a machine consumer cannot tell a budget stop from the user pressing `s`. Add `FinishBudgetReached` beside `FinishCreditsExhausted`, teach the codec and `sc status`, and switch `RunBudget.Stop` to it.
  - Done when: `sc status --json` on a budget-ended run names the budget as the reason.
- [x] **P9-13 — A crashed or timed-out agent's tokens are never charged** (deps: P9-01, P6-07; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `AgentFiring.Spent` is called by `Supervisor.supervise` on every attempt that ran and did not deliver — crash, timeout, kill, rate-limited retry, reroute — and deliberately not on the post-session-hook path, whose session `Result` already metered. `RunBudget.MeterFiring` chains onto both hooks, so a firing is charged whatever its outcome and a failed attempt the cartridge reported nothing for is not counted as a session.
  - Do: `RunBudget.MeterPlanner` chains onto `AgentFiring.Result`, which the supervisor runs only for a session that finished and decoded. An agent killed by `limits.agent_timeout` after twenty minutes of work, or one that crashed on its last attempt, spent real tokens the budget never sees, so a run that mostly times out can overrun `max_cost_usd` indefinitely. The supervisor holds the transcript on the failure path too.
  - Done when: a timed-out session's usage counts against the budget, asserted by a test.
- [x] **P9-14 — The live budget cannot be raised, and the file cannot say "unlimited"** (deps: P9-01, P1-19; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `RunBudget.Raise` replaces the ceiling of the cap that stopped the run, resets grants so the number typed is the number the run stops at, clears the hold and resumes through the new optional `BudgetResumer`; it refuses a ceiling at or under the spend and a run with no cap. The TUI gains a `b` key beside `a` (with `BudgetController` an *optional* half of `tui.Controller`, so no existing implementer changed) and `sc run` wires it. Second half answered by documentation, which the task allows: `limits.max_plan_rejections`/`max_plan_failures` keep their >= 1 floor and `docs/config.md` says why a config file may not say "never give up" while the Go option can.
  - Do: two halves of the same gap. An autopilot run paused on a cost cap can only be resumed into another fixed grant or stopped — there is no way to raise `limits.max_cost_usd` for the live run (a TUI control beside the `a` ammo prompt, or `sc budget <amount>`, would make the pause actionable). And `PlannerOptions.RejectionLimit`/`FailureLimit` take a negative value as "never give up", a mode `limits.max_plan_rejections`/`max_plan_failures` cannot express because both validate as ≥ 1: either document that as deliberate or let -1 through.
  - Done when: a paused run's budget can be raised from the TUI, and the config file can express every mode the Go options have (or `docs/config.md` says why not).
- [x] **P9-15 — `sc status` should report the run lock's foreign-host and reused-pid verdicts** (deps: P9-04, P6-08; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `sc status` asks `orchestrator.InspectLock` — the same P9-04 verdict `sc run` acts on — and reports all six states, with stale split into "pid N took the run lock and is gone" and "…that pid belongs to another program now". `Running()` is now the lock's verdict and the new `Unknowable()` keeps a lock held on another machine from reading as a crash. Five new goldens.
  - Do: `orchestrator.InspectLock` now tells a lock held on another machine, and one whose pid has been recycled into an unrelated program, from a genuinely live one — but `internal/cli/status.go` still asks `ProbeLiveness`, which answers only held/mine/alive. A user whose run was killed and whose pid was reused is told "process running" by `sc status` and "removed a stale run lock" by `sc run`, which contradict each other.
  - Done when: `sc status` distinguishes the six verdicts, with goldens.
- [x] **P9-16 — Give the last few user-facing errors a remedy** (deps: P9-08; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — All six items: the two `sc run` path errors, `--repo`'s, `sc status: empty bead id`, `cartridge.ErrNoActiveCartridge` (which now carries the same `sc cartridge --add claude --model <model>` line as every other hint), and `cli.ErrNotImplemented`, which is deleted. The matching `docs/troubleshooting.md` rows were rewritten.
  - Do: the P9-08 audit found four messages that say what happened but not what to do — `run.go`'s `%s: no such directory` and `%s: not a directory`, `flags.go`'s `--repo %q: not a directory`, and `status.go`'s `status: empty bead id` — plus two inconsistencies: `cartridge.ErrNoActiveCartridge` embeds `sc cartridge --add claude` while `cli.CartridgeAddHint` says `sc cartridge --add claude --model <model>`, so a user is told two different commands for one problem, and `cli.ErrNotImplemented` ("not implemented") should be deleted or given a task id now that no released command returns it.
  - Done when: each says what happened, why and what to do, and `docs/troubleshooting.md` matches.
- [x] **P9-17 — The run budget never meters the planning product owner's session** (deps: P9-01, P6-02; size: S) (done: wf-p89 d5f3fbf) — both P8-02 runs reported one fewer metered session than agents fired ("9 agents fired" / "over 8 sessions"), and the missing one was the planning product owner every time. `sc run` wrapped only the feedback/pull-request-review firing planner in `RunBudget.MeterPlanner`, while the product owner's firing is routed through `PlanRouter`/`SupervisorPlanRunner`, which builds its own `AgentFiring` and so is never seen by a `FiringPlanner`: `limits.max_tokens` and `limits.max_cost_usd` under-counted by the whole planning spend — in autopilot, the one agent that fires for ever. `RunBudget.MeterPlanRunner` and `runSpend.meterPlan` now wrap the `PlanRunner`; anything that reported usage is counted whatever the outcome, because a plan the planner rejected (P6-19) cost real tokens and will be fired again. `make demo DEMO_ARGS=--check` now reports 11 sessions for its 11 finished agents instead of 10.
  - Do: meter the planning firing as well as the scheduler's firings.
  - Done when: a test meters a plan runner, and the run summary's session count matches the agents that finished.
- [x] **P6-54 — `Conductor.Attach` should give the run its resume handler** (deps: P9-03, P6-15, P8-14; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Run.UseResumeHandler` plus `Conductor.Attach`: attaching the delivery handoff gives the run a resume handler when its options carry none (`*Deliverer` already satisfies `ResumeHandler`). An explicit `Options.OnResume` still wins, so `internal/cli`'s wiring test is unaffected.
  - Do: the P6-15 hand-offs (`Deliverer.RedeliverPR`, `Deliverer.ConflictRound`) were dead in the shipped binary for a whole phase because `Options.OnResume` was never filled, and nothing failed. P9-03 fixed the call site in `internal/cli/run.go` and pinned it with a test, but the wiring is still a line an assembler has to remember. `NewConductor` already receives both the run and the deliverer (`ConductorParts.Delivery`), so `Attach` could hand the run a resume handler when its options carry none, making the wiring structural.

A deliberate "finished — press q" state would be better product and would let the recording end on the graph; it touches `internal/cli/run.go`, `tui.ExitCodeFor` and the TUI goldens.

- [x] **P9-18 — Nothing writes `<run-dir>/agents/<id>.log`, so `sc logs <agent-id>` is dead** (deps: P8-01, P0-07, P8-05; size: S) (done: wf-p89 f5684d0) — `logging.RunLogger.Agent` had no caller outside tests, so `runs/<run-id>/agents/` stayed empty on every real run: `sc logs <agent-id>` answered "the run has no agent logs yet", `sc logs <bead-id>` could never work, and `cli.ReportFirstRun` advertised the directory to every new user. `internal/cli/agentlog.go` (`agentLogSink`) now writes `AgentSpawned`/`Output`/`Finished`/`Failed` through the run logger, wired on the **publish path** rather than as a bus subscription — `events.Bus` drops for a slow subscriber and agent output is the highest-volume event in the run — and fail-soft, warning once per agent and never failing a run. What is still outside the net is the wrapped `fmt.Errorf` text in those packages. `internal/cli/troubleshooting_wrapped_test.go` extends the P9-08 contract to the six packages underneath the command layer: every `fmt.Errorf`/`errors.New` message a non-test file builds must match a row of the page or carry a written exemption, with three tests (coverage, staleness, and that the two skipped test-only directories still exist). 723 messages scanned, 17 new rows, and every exemption carries one of five documented reasons.

The other ~580 non-test `fmt.Errorf` messages in beads, gitx, cartridge, agents, orchestrator and proc are held only by their exported sentinels, so a new `gitx: …` or `orchestrator: …` sentence can still reach a user with no row — every user-facing run-lock message, for instance, lives in internal/orchestrator. `docs/troubleshooting.md` names P9-19 as the id for this and says so in its opening promise; `internal/cli/firstrun_test.go` cites it too.

- [x] **P9-20 — `sc run --headless | head` leaves the run lock and the worktrees behind** (deps: P9-04, P8-01; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `fireCannon` installs `runIgnoreBrokenPipe()` for a headless run and wraps the screen in `closedOutputWriter`, so the first broken write becomes `run.Stop("its output was closed")` and the shutdown is the one SIGTERM gets. Proved with a real child `sc` whose stdout pipe is closed after one line: exit 0, `LockFree`, worktrees cleaned. Mutation-tested — without the guard the child dies with "signal: broken pipe" and the lock stands.
  - Do: the process dies on SIGPIPE writing to a closed pipe, so neither the lock release nor the worktree cleanup runs, and the mess stands until the next run's stale-lock notice removes it. It self-heals, and nothing tests that path. Ignoring SIGPIPE on stdout (or treating the write error as a stop) would make the shutdown the same as SIGTERM's.
  - Done when: a headless run whose stdout closes early releases its lock and cleans its worktrees, asserted by a test.
- [x] **P9-21 — A tripped budget re-fires a lens's review until the round closes** (deps: P9-01, P6-04, P6-02; size: S) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd) — closed by P9-01 round 4: the state "reviewing with an empty reviewer list" was overloaded, meaning both "a thread adopted straight into review that `plan` has never seen" and "the last verdict of a round is being folded in". `fbThread.adopted` (set only in `newThread`, cleared by the single writer `fbThread.setReviewers`) separates them, and `demandsFor` may re-derive a round's reviewers only for an adopted thread — so a dispatch landing between `applyVerdict` emptying the list and `plan` running (the bd comment is written in between) no longer re-fires the lens that just answered. Counted by a test: `TestRunChargesEveryAgentSessionAgainstTheRunBudget` now pins exactly `len(domain.Lenses())` tester firings, and `TestABudgetHeldRoundIsNotRefiredWhileItsVerdictIsWrittenToTheBead` reproduces the window deterministically with no goroutines.
  - Do: found while writing P9-01's run-level budget tests. A lens's `DemandReview` is re-offered and fired again between its verdict landing and the bead moving to `changes_requested` — 3 to 8 tester sessions for a three-lens round, varying run to run. It is not budget-specific (the budget only makes it visible, by keeping the loop alive with nothing else to do), but a run held at its cap can spend several more sessions than it needs to on reviews it already has.
  - Done when: a lens that has already voted in a round is not offered that round's review again, asserted by a test that counts firings.

P9-02 now judges agent-issued git when it goes through its shim (`TestAnAgentsOwnGitIsCaughtByTheSameChecker`), which proves the checker discriminates, but nothing enforces it in a real run.

The production run path around them — `internal/gitx/cleanup.go`'s branch cleanup, resume/reattach, the delivery settler and the external pull request reviewer — never has its command lines judged; it is covered only by `internal/orchestrator/e2e_test.go` asserting the tips do not move. Driving the e2e scenarios under `ivScan`/`ivScanGH` would extend the invariant to the whole run (the checkers would have to move to a shared test helper).

- [x] **P9-24 — `sc stop`: stop a run from another terminal** (deps: P9-04, P8-01; size: M) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `sc stop [PATH]` reads the run lock, refuses with its own sentence for every lock it must not signal, sends SIGINT to the holder (`--now` sends the second interrupt D-18 escalates on) and waits (`--wait`, default 2m); it never writes or removes the lock. Proved by stopping a real headless autopilot run from another process. `orchestrator.LockedError` now names it, and nine troubleshooting rows were added.

A `sc stop [<run>]` that reads the run lock, sends SIGINT to the holder and waits for the run to finish would let the refusal name a real command.
  - Done when: `sc stop` stops a live run from another terminal, and the lock refusal and the troubleshooting page name it.
- [x] **P9-25 — Stamp the run lock inside `Run.Start`, not from `internal/cli`** (deps: P9-04, P6-01; size: S) (done: wf-p89 0d22abd) — closed by P9-04 round 4: `acquireLock` (the only path into the lock, called from `Run.Start`) now marshals a full `LockRecord` including the process start stamp in the same atomic link-into-place that takes the lock, so a run started without the CLI is stamped from birth (`TestAcquireLockPublishesTheStartStamp`, which calls `acquireLock` directly and then finds `LockLive` from a foreign probe). `StampRunLock` stays as the compatibility path for a lock published by an older `sc`; `internal/cli/run.go` still calls it, where it is now a byte-for-byte no-op.
  - Do: `StampRunLock` is called from `internal/cli/run.go` after `Run.Start` returns, so the lock is briefly unstamped and any non-CLI embedder never gets a start stamp at all — the pid-reuse hardening then silently degrades to plain liveness. Stamping inside `acquireLock` (or immediately after it, under the run's lock) would make it unconditional.
  - Done when: a run started without the CLI has a stamped lock, asserted by a test.
- [x] **P9-26 — Make the clean case of the redaction hook cheap** (deps: P9-05, P9-07, P0-07; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Redactor.Redact` asks a prefilter first — one byte scan with a 256-entry first-byte table for the literal words a rule cannot match without — and only the flagged rules run a regexp. The words are derived from the regexps themselves with `regexp/syntax`, so a rule added at run time is filtered without being annotated and one the derivation cannot read is simply always run. A clean agent output line went 2.9 us -> 0.21 us (13x); the whole publish path is ~2.8 us against an 11 us budget. Covered by a corpus checked both ways, a property test, a fallback test and a seeded fuzz target.
  - Do: `logging.Redactor.SafeEvent` runs three regular expressions plus an escape scan per untrusted field, so an agent output line costs 8.4 µs through the hook against 2.6 µs for the run log and bus beneath it, and a twenty-field verdict costs 110 µs — the hook is now the larger half of a publish (`internal/logging/redact_bench_test.go`, written up in `docs/performance.md`). The 10 000 events/s budget still holds by 9x, so nothing is broken, but the fix is cheap: one combined regexp, or a byte scan for the token prefixes before any regexp runs. An opt-in `SC_PERF_BUDGETS` assertion over `SafeEvent` would then cover the whole publish path rather than the part below the hook.
  - Done when: a clean agent output line costs about as much as the publish beneath it, and a budget assertion covers the hook.
- [x] **P9-27 — One unreproduced `internal/orchestrator` failure under `make integration`** (deps: P9-03, P6-10; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — The failing test is named and fixed: `TestRunBudgetSurvivesTheStateFile` gave the run `t.Context()`, which Go cancels after the body but before cleanups, so the run's asynchronous finish wrote `state.json` into the directory `t.TempDir()`'s RemoveAll was deleting — "TempDir RemoveAll cleanup: directory not empty", fast and with no test name in the dump, exactly as reported. It now runs on a context of its own and waits for the run to finish before its cleanup returns; 4x `-count=200 -race` clean. Every short wall-clock wait in the package is scaled by `testutil.Patience` (see P6-56). Round wf-p10 follow-up (`de2fc31`): `make check-golden` failed once under the wave's own load in `internal/proc`, so its waits go through `testutil.Patience` too and its process probes now identify a process rather than a pid — this machine's `pid_max` is 4 194 304 and four concurrent whole-suite binaries fork hard enough to wrap the counter, so a `kill(pid, 0)` probe was reporting a stranger that had taken the dead grandchild's pid. `procWatch` compares `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s start time as well.
  - Do: during the round 3 integration (wf-p89, `f5684d0`) one whole-tree `make integration` reported `FAIL slop-cannon/internal/orchestrator 26.030s` with no other package failing. The package's stderr dump ended in supervisor and gate-poller lines, so no test name was captured, and 26 s means none of the three-minute deadlock bounds (`e2eTimeout`, `recKillPointBudget`) was reached — the failure was fast. It did not reproduce in five clean whole-tree `make integration` runs afterwards, nor in the package alone at `-count=4`, under six concurrent test binaries, at `GOMAXPROCS=2 -parallel 16`, or at `GOMAXPROCS=1 -parallel 32`. The likeliest shapes are the 5 s `gateEventually` budget in `gates_test.go` and the 5 s stop waits beside it, both of which are wall-clock waits on a loaded machine. Next time it happens, capture the whole output (`go test -race -tags integration -count=1 ./... 2>&1 | tee`) before anything else.
  - Done when: the failing test is named and either fixed or shown to be an environment limit, and any 5 s wall-clock wait in the package waits on a condition rather than a duration.

- [x] **P4-15 — A resume cannot recreate a wiped worktree while the user has uncommitted work** (deps: P4-08, P9-03; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Manager.create(ctx, bead, allowDirty)` puts the dirty guard under the caller's control and the resume passes true: adding a linked worktree elsewhere has nothing to do with the user's own mid-edit checkout. The invariant test works around it by not wiping a worktree, and says so.

- [x] **P4-16 — A branch whose commits were rebased away still reads as landed on resume** (deps: P4-13, P4-06; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Repo.branchProgress` replaces `branchMoved`: it reads `%H%x00%gs` from the branch's own reflog and reports `known`, `moved` and `rebasedAway` — the newest entry being a finished rebase onto the very commit it produced. **Deviation**: no `sc_base_sha`; after a rebase that dropped every commit the tip is still past the base, so `rev-list` cannot tell the two cases apart, while the reflog states it exactly.

The `sc_base_sha` P4-13's own Do line prescribed — measured on the pre-rebase tip, already consumed by `gitx.MergeOptions.BaseSHA` — is what closes it.

- [x] **P6-55 — The resume handler's deliveries bypass the conductor's delivery queue** (deps: P8-14, P6-15, P9-03; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Conductor.QueueResume` runs a resume hand-off through the same single delivery worker, the same lock and the same bounded retry as an ordinary delivery; `NewConductor` gives the queue to the run, and all three hand-offs route through it. It returns on the first attempt's outcome so a failing hand-off cannot hold `Run.Start` open for the whole backoff. Sampled concurrency across three hand-offs and one delivery: peak 1.
  - Do: `Deliverer.Redeliver`/`RedeliverPR` are called on the `*Deliverer` directly by the run's `ResumeHandler`, so they neither serialise against P8-14's single-worker delivery queue nor get its bounded retry. Two beads reconciled at resume time can still rebase and fast-forward the same default branch concurrently, and a retryable failure there is stranded exactly as the round-4 gap described. Route the resume's re-deliveries through the same queue, or give the deliverer its own default-branch lock.
  - Done when: two resume-time deliveries of different beads are serialised, asserted by a test that samples concurrency, and a retryable one is retried.
- [x] **P6-56 — `schedEventually`'s 5 s wall-clock deadline makes the scheduler suite load-sensitive** (deps: P6-02, P9-07; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `schedPollBudget` (30 s written, up from 5 s) times `schedLoadFactor` (the binary's `-timeout` over the 10 m default, clamped, resolved lazily because `flag.Lookup("test.timeout")` is nil during package-variable initialisation) drives every wait in `scheduler_test.go` and `planning_test.go`, and `testutil.Patience` now scales every other short wall-clock wait in the package (gates, lifecycle, supervisor, eventlog, run_glue, prreview, recovery, e2e). The three- and five-minute deadlock bounds are left absolute and say why. Round wf-p10 follow-up (`de2fc31`): `make check-golden` failed once under the wave's own load in `internal/proc`, so its waits go through `testutil.Patience` too and its process probes now identify a process rather than a pid — this machine's `pid_max` is 4 194 304 and four concurrent whole-suite binaries fork hard enough to wrap the counter, so a `kill(pid, 0)` probe was reporting a stranger that had taken the dead grandchild's pid. `procWatch` compares `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s start time as well.
  - Do: `internal/orchestrator/scheduler_test.go`'s `schedEventually` polls a condition against a fixed 5 s wall-clock deadline. Under load it expires before the scheduler has been scheduled enough ticks and the test fails as "timed out waiting for …" rather than on anything the code did — one iteration in 4000 of `TestSchedulerTellsTwoHeldItemsOfOneRoundApart` during P9-09's round-4 stress run, with the same shape present when the fix under test was removed. It is the same class as P9-27's unreproduced failure. Drive these waits off the fixture's fake clock, or scale the deadline by a `-timeout`-derived factor.

- [x] **P8-36 — Decide whether a run may settle another run's external review bead** (deps: P8-04, P8-11; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — Decided and enforced: a run adopts and settles another run's external review bead exactly as it does its own, under the same `StaleReviewPolicy`, and the reason says the bead was raised by an earlier run. It is never settled while its pull request is still open. See D-28 below for the rationale; `TestPRReviewSettlesAnEarlierRunsReviewBeadOnlyOnceItsPullRequestHasGone` is the guard, including `StaleReviewIgnore` as the escape hatch.
  - Do: `gitx.PRWatcher`'s dedupe listing is deliberately unscoped (`ListFilter{All: true, Labels: {sc:external-pr}}`) so one pull request gets one bead for its lifetime, which also means the current run settles review beads earlier runs left open. Round 4 made the recorded reason truthful (`gitx.StaleReview.ForeignRun` adds "(its review bead was raised by an earlier run)"), but the policy itself — settle, or leave another run's bead alone and only adopt it while its pull request is open — has never been decided. A `StaleReviewPolicy` variant or a run-scoped settle is the shape.
  - Done when: the decision is written down here and enforced by a test.
- [x] **P8-37 — Give the external reviewer a whole-run test for the P8-11 stale path** (deps: P8-04, P8-11; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `TestRunSettlesAReviewBeadWhosePullRequestGoesStale` drives the whole assembled `sc run`: the gh stand-in serves a different listing from the second poll on, so the pull request is adopted on poll 1 and by poll 2 is either gone (`StaleClosed`) or carries the cannon's own label (`StaleCannonPR`). Both cases assert the run completes, the bead is closed with the watcher's reason plus "nothing left to review", and no `gh pr review` and no `gh pr merge` ever happen.
  - Do: `internal/cli/reviewprs_wiring_test.go` drives the happy path through an assembled run, and `pr_poll_interval` makes repeated polls reachable in a seconds-long run, so the stale path is finally testable end to end: list the pull request on the first poll and drop it (or add the `slop-cannon` label to it) on a later one, then assert the review bead is settled with the right reason and no review is posted. Only unit tests cover it today.
  - Done when: a whole-run test covers a review bead going stale, for at least the closed and adopted-by-the-cannon reasons.
- [x] **P8-38 — `sc logs <bead-id> --follow` refuses to wait for the first agent** (deps: P8-05; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `sc logs <bead-id> --follow` prints one stderr notice (keeping the resolver's "this run has: …" list so a typo is still visible) and enters the follow loop, which already adopts logs that turn up later; the same for `sc logs --follow` before `sc.log` exists. The one-shot form still fails fast, and its troubleshooting row now points at `--follow`.
  - Do: with `--follow` and a bead whose agents have not spawned yet, `logsResolve` fails the command with "the run has no agent logs yet" instead of polling until one appears — which is what the follow loop already does for agents that turn up later. Right for a one-shot read, arguably wrong for `--follow`; the troubleshooting row papers over it by telling the user to retry.
  - Done when: `--follow` waits for the first agent log of a bead and starts streaming it, while the one-shot form still fails fast.
- [x] **P9-28 — A warning published in a run's first tick never reaches the screen** (deps: P9-01, P8-01, P6-08; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `StartupReplay`, wired by `TeeRunLog`, holds up to 256 events while the bus behind it has no subscribers and releases them in order the moment one attaches, then becomes a plain forwarder. `sc run` starts the run and only then renders it, so the resume notices and P9-01's "resumed over its budget" banner used to reach only the log; `TestRunResumedCarriesTheBudgetItAlreadySpent` now asserts the notice on the real command's screen.
  - Do: the headless renderer and the TUI subscribe to the bus after `Run.Start`, and `events.Bus` does not replay, so anything published in the run's first milliseconds — P9-01's "resumed over its budget" notice is the one that matters, because it is about money — reaches `sc.log`, `events.jsonl`, `sc logs` and `sc status` but never the user's screen. Either subscribe before `Start` or give the run a small replay buffer for the events published between `Start` and the first subscriber.
  - Done when: a resumed run's budget notice appears on stdout, asserted by a test that drives the real command.
- [x] **P9-29 — A crash between `delivering` and `gh pr create` has no kill point (safety on)** (deps: P9-03, P4-05; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `recModeOpening` with `recPRCreateHold` — a gh wrapper that catches the first `pr create`, records its head branch and blocks, the safety-on twin of `recHoldingCheck`. The crash leaves the bead `delivering` with no PR, no URL, no gate and no completed create; the resume takes the branch only `Redeliver` acts on, the run finishes, and there is exactly one `gh pr create` per head across both processes.
  - Do: P9-03's `Redeliver` hand-off is safety-agnostic and is proven by a safety-off kill point (a blocking check command) plus a unit test, but the safety-on half of the same window — the bead is `delivering` and `gh pr create` has not run yet — has no crash test, because the pull request path runs no check command and the hold seam does not exist there. It needs a `gh` shim that blocks on `pr create` the way `recSwallowGateCreate` intercepts `gate create`.
  - Done when: a SIGKILL in that window is resumed with exactly one pull request opened across both processes.
- [x] **P9-30 — A warning whose text arrives as a function parameter is invisible to the warning guard** (deps: P9-08; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — The guard resolves one level of parameter flow, plus one hop through a return value and through a receiver field, which is what it takes to read the budget banner at `b.log.Warn` — mutation-checked by rewording `describeLocked`. Indexes are keyed by package directory and receiver name so three unrelated `fire` methods cannot lend each other their words. That exposed 21 warnings the audit had never seen: the two foreign-status ones are now documented, the budget banner has a row, and the twelve `Deliverer.retryable` reasons and the seven prefixed forms are exempted with why.
  - Do: `TestTroubleshootingCoversEveryWarningTheRunRaises` resolves literals, string constants, `fmt.Sprintf` and locals assigned in the same function, which covers all 111 warnings raised today. It cannot read a message built in one function and passed into another as a plain parameter — `internal/orchestrator/limits.go`'s budget banner (`b.log.Warn(banner, ...)`) is the one live example, covered only by the page's prose. Resolve one level of parameter flow, or require that a warning's text be built at the call site.
  - Done when: the guard either reads that banner or the build fails until it is built at its call site.
- [x] **P9-31 — `(*structured.ParseError).Error()` never renders its `Cause`** (deps: P2-07, P9-08; size: S) (done: wf-p10 5cdea02) — `Error()` appends the cause as a `  cause: …` line whenever the cause carries words the rendered Detail and Problems do not already contain, so a wrapped sentinel such as `agents.ErrPlanRejected` is readable in a log and not merely matchable. A `*jsonschema.ValidationError` cause is always suppressed, because `Problems` is exactly its flattened leaves — which is what keeps all nineteen `.expected` goldens byte-identical.
  - Do: `internal/cartridge/structured/errors.go` prints Reason, Contract, Detail and Problems and drops Cause, so a wrapped sentinel such as `agents.ErrPlanRejected` is matchable with `errors.Is` but unreadable in any log line. P9-08 round 4 worked around it by keying the troubleshooting row on the Detail; if a future `ParseError` ever wraps a cause whose words are not repeated in Problems, those words vanish.
  - Done when: either the cause is rendered, or the type documents that a cause must never carry words a user needs and a test enforces it.
- [ ] **P1-20 — Wire `graph_poll_interval`, or retire the key** (deps: P1-14, P3-07, P6-32; size: S)
  - Do: the key parses, validates, shows in `sc config show` and has a `docs/config.md` row, but nothing reads it: `beads.NewWatcher` has no production caller at all, so the graph watcher the key configures is unwired end to end. A run syncs the graph through `Conductor.Sync` instead, on `DefaultConductorInterval` (1 s), which is deliberately faster and separately justified — rebinding the key to it would change both the key's meaning and the run's cadence, so wf-p10 made `docs/config.md` say plainly that the key is unread rather than pretend otherwise. Either construct the watcher and pass `cfg.GraphPollInterval.Duration()` as `WatcherOptions.Interval` (then `beads.DefaultPollInterval` can alias `config.DefaultGraphPollInterval`), or make the conductor's interval the key and reconcile the two defaults, or delete the key.
  - Done when: setting `graph_poll_interval` in a config file changes what a run does, or the key is gone from the schema, `sc config show` and the docs.
- [ ] **P4-17 — `Manager.alreadyLanded`'s BaseSHA rule has P4-16's blind spot** (deps: P4-16, P4-13; size: S)
  - Do: `rev-list --count <BaseSHA>..<tip> > 0` is satisfied by the upstream commits a rebase put under the branch, so a delivery whose rebase dropped every commit would be reported `AlreadyLanded` and the bead closed with nothing of its own on the default branch — the very case P4-16 closes on the resume path. It is unreachable today because nothing writes `sc_base_sha`, so it goes live the moment something does. The fix is the same reflog question `Repo.branchProgress` now answers (internal/gitx/deliver_merge.go).
  - Done when: a delivery whose rebase dropped every commit is not reported as landed, with a test that plants that history.
- [ ] **P6-57 — Nothing writes `sc_round`, so a resumed bead's round rests on its comments alone** (deps: P6-47, P6-26, P3-02; size: S)
  - Do: `beads.RoundMetadata`/`beads.MetaRound` exist and `domain.Bead.Round` decodes them, but `grep -rn RoundMetadata` outside `internal/beads` finds no caller: `StateMachine.persist` writes only the status, so `sc_round` stays at the 0 the bead was created with. That is what makes P6-47's evidence weak — the bead's own round, which the resume wants, is never recorded — and it is why `ReviewAgain` documents "a conflict round whose bead carries no `sc_round` reaches here with round 0". Writing `RoundMetadata(t.round)` alongside the `changes_requested`/`in_review` status writes would make a resume exact instead of inferred.
  - Done when: a bead in `changes_requested` carries the round it is in, and P6-47's resume reads it on a real run rather than only in its unit test.
- [ ] **P6-58 — The gate poller polls bd for the whole run even when the run holds no gate** (deps: P6-14, P8-01; size: S)
  - Do: `GatePoller.Poll` calls `bd gate check` on every tick and counts the tick in `GatePollStats.Polls` whether or not the run has ever created a gate (`internal/orchestrator/gates.go`: `p.gates.CheckGates` then `p.stats.Polls++`), so a run that opens no pull request still forks bd every `gate_poll_interval` and `sc status` shows a vitals row like `gates: 43 polls · last 1s ago` for a run with no gates at all. Seen on the P10-05 dogfood run, which had no remote and so never created a `gh:pr` gate. Skip the poll while the poller knows of no gate — delivery already `Kick`s it when it creates one — or say `no gates` in the vitals row instead of counting empty polls.
  - Done when: a run that opens no pull request makes no `bd gate check` call, proven by a test counting the bd shim's calls, and the vitals row says so rather than reporting a poll count.
- [ ] **P7-26 — A thread whose disputed item the PO overruled still reads as unapproved with an open item** (deps: P7-04, P6-04; size: S)
  - Do: recording P6-04 (d) for P7-14 showed the loop approves the bead on the ruling and asks the tester nothing more, so the last thing the lens said is still a change request and the item's last occurrence is still `held`. `FeedbackThread.Approved` is "every tester satisfied" and `Open` counts unsettled occurrences, so `testdata/tui/feedback-arbitration.golden` reads "✓ approved · 2 rounds · ux ✓ qa ✗ test ✓ · 1 open" — truthful about the events, confusing about the run. Either publish the settled item after a binding ruling, or treat an occurrence with a ruling as settled and its lens as answered.
  - Done when: a thread the PO ruled on reads as settled in the Feedback view, and the golden says so.
- [ ] **P7-27 — The cursor gutter is a cell wider under ambiguous-width 2** (deps: P7-16; size: S)
  - Do: P7-16 padded every glyph the views print, but the selected-row gutter is `▌ ` against `  ` for an unselected row, and ▌ is East-Asian-ambiguous: under `RUNEWIDTH_EASTASIAN=1` the selected row starts one column further right than the rows around it, in the Graph list, the Agents history, the Feedback list, the PRs list and the Log. It is one shared idiom in five files plus model.go, so it wants one helper rather than five edits.
  - Done when: `TestGlyphColumnUnderEastAsianWidth`'s child process also checks the gutter, and every list's rows line up in both width modes.
- [ ] **P7-28 — A headless run's event log is 96% raw agent output, with no way to turn it down** (deps: P8-05, P7-11; size: M)
  - Do: the P10-05 dogfood recording is 721 events of which **691 are `agent_output`** — 198 KB for a 3m39s, 5-agent run, including whole files an agent printed to its own terminal (the product owner put 48 numbered source lines into the log — 44 of `internal/cli/root_test.go`, lines 116-159, plus 4 of `.goreleaser.yaml`, lines 38-41 — the engineer another 51, the testers 17, **116 numbered source lines across the run**, and the engineer's failing `go test` output is in there twice. Recount them with `^(\[[^]]*\] )?[0-9]+[-:]` over the `line` field of every `agent_output` record: the first line of each Bash result carries a `[result Bash ok] ` prefix, so a pattern anchored on the digit alone misses 7 of the 116 and lands on 109). `--headless` renders one line per event, `sc logs` has no level, and the committed recordings are therefore mostly other people's file contents; the ratio only gets worse above ammo 4. Either give `--headless` and `sc logs` a verbosity that keeps the milestones and drops `agent_output`, or cap how much of one firing's stdout is published (the per-agent logs under `agents/` already hold the whole thing).
  - Done when: a headless run can be asked for milestones only, with a test, and the recorded evidence stays readable.
- [ ] **P8-39 — `sc status` still adopts the newest run directory even when it holds no `state.json`** (deps: P8-31, P1-02; size: S)
  - Do: P8-31 fixed the resume path in `internal/cli` (`runResumeTarget`), because `internal/config` belonged to another lane. `config.LatestRunID` is unchanged, so `sc status` with no `--run` still reports a directory that recorded nothing ("phase new / 0 beads / 0 events") while a live run is going under an older id. A `config.LatestResumableRunID` that prefers a run with a state file, read by both `sc status` and `runResumeTarget`, is the fix.
  - Done when: `sc status` names the run a `--resume` would continue, with a test that plants an empty newest run directory.
- [ ] **P8-40 — Give `PRReview`'s counters a durable home so `sc status` reads a number** (deps: P8-24, P6-29; size: S)
  - Do: `PRReview.Stats()` and `PRReview.Reviews()` still have no reader anywhere in the binary, and they cannot get one in `sc status`, which reads a run off disk: no event carries a posted review and `state.json` has no reviewer section, so P8-24 has to infer "posted" from the review bead's bd close reason (guarded by a source-level test against the reviewer's own format string). Give `RunVitals` a review section fed from `PRReview.Stats()` where `run.UseVitals` is wired, or publish an event when a consolidated review goes out and fold it in `RunSummary`.
  - Done when: a finished run's `sc status` reports the reviews it posted with bd unavailable.
- [ ] **P8-41 — `--dry-run` reports `review-prs true` for a run that would review nothing** (deps: P8-04, P8-01; size: S)
  - Do: the dry run prints `rc.Run.Config.ReviewPRs` verbatim (`internal/cli/run.go`, the `review-prs` row), but the run's real answer is `cli.decideReviewPRs` (D-11), which turns the wish off when the repository has no remote, when `gh` is missing, or when `gh` is not authenticated — and carries a reason for each. Walking the README Quickstart literally in a temp repository with no remote and an unauthenticated `gh` prints `review-prs true`, while the run itself would say `off: the repository has no git remote`. Resolve the decision in the dry run and print its reason the way the run does; the row's neighbours (`safety`, `cartridge`) already name where their value came from.
  - Done when: `sc <repo> --dry-run` in a repository with no remote says review-prs is off and why, with a test, and the dry-run golden shows it.
- [ ] **P9-32 — `agents.GitGuard` and `internal/testutil/gitguard` are two things called "git guard"** (deps: P9-22, P9-23; size: S)
  - Do: P9-22 added `agents.GitGuard` — a generated `git` script on an agent's PATH, production code — in the same wave that P9-23 made `internal/testutil/gitguard` the shared judge of recorded git argv in tests. They do different jobs, do not collide at compile time and their doc comments distinguish them, but the names will mislead. Renaming one (`testutil/gitargv`, say, or `agents.GitShim`) would keep them apart. Related: `proc.Run` resolves a command against the *parent's* PATH (`exec.LookPath` before `spec.Environ()` is applied), so a mock-cartridge `Exec("git", …)` step runs the real binary rather than the guard — a real firing is unaffected, but the e2e and demo rigs exercise a weaker sandbox than production.
  - Done when: one of the two is renamed, and `proc.Run` resolves against `spec.Env["PATH"]` when it is set (or the gap is documented on `proc.Spec`).
- [ ] **P9-33 — The git guard denies `git init`, so a target repository whose own tests build git repositories cannot be tested from inside an agent worktree** (deps: P9-22, P9-02; size: M)
  - Do: `agents.GitGuardAllowed` deliberately leaves `init` out (and `GitGuardGlobalWriters` refuses `-C`/`-c` anywhere in the argv), which is right for the agent's own tree — but slop-cannon's own suite creates throwaway repositories with `git init` under `t.TempDir()`. In the P10-05 dogfood run every agent that ran `go test ./internal/cli/...` in its worktree saw tests unrelated to the diff fail on `testutil: git init -q -b main: exit status 128: fatal: git may only be run inside <worktree> from an agent session` — the message is `GitGuardOutsideMessage`, not `GitGuardDeniedMessage`, because the throwaway repository lives under `t.TempDir()` and so the cwd rule refuses the call before the missing verb ever matters. The recording names 12 such tests in `--- FAIL:` lines (`TestRunRefusesARepositoryAnotherRunHolds`, `TestRunRedactsWhatAnAgentPrints`, `TestRunPrintsTheFirstRunNotice`, `TestRunNeverQuotesAnExcludedFileInAPrompt`, `TestStopStopsALiveRunFromAnotherTerminal`, `TestRunGuardsTheStderrEveryProducerShares`, `TestRunHeadlessStopsCleanlyWhenItsOutputCloses`, `TestRunAssemblyGivesTheConductorTheRulesGate`, `TestRunAssemblyGuardsTheStateStoreWithTheRulesGate`, `TestRunAssemblyReleasesWhatItBuiltWhenItFails`, `TestRunAssemblyWiresTheDeliverySettler`, `TestRunLogClosesWithRunFinishedAndAgreesWithTheState`) and the tester who ran the whole package counted ~17 failures, while the same suite passes outside the sandbox. That crew reasoned around it and said so in their reports, but it costs a firing's worth of tokens every time and it is exactly how a correct diff earns a spurious `request_changes`; it also hits the safety-off path, whose `check_command` is the project's own test command, and every Go project whose tests shell out to git. The fence worth keeping is the target, not the verb, so **both** halves have to move together: adding `init` to the allow list alone changes nothing, because the cwd rule is what actually fires here. Allow `init` (and `-c`) *and* let the cwd rule admit a path under `TMPDIR`, or under any directory that is not the run's repository — that widens nothing an agent can do to the repository it was fired at.
  - Done when: an agent session can run a target test suite that creates temporary git repositories, a test plants such a suite, and the P9-02 invariant tests still refuse `init` and `-C` against the run's repository.
- [x] **P10-09 — Pin `.goreleaser.yaml`'s ldflags to `internal/cli` with a test** (deps: P10-03, P1-10; size: S) (done: wf-p10 d512204) — `TestVersionLdflagsMatchGoreleaser` sits beside `TestVersionLdflagsMatchMakefile` in `internal/cli/root_test.go`, reads `../../.goreleaser.yaml` and requires `-X slop-cannon/internal/cli.<name>=` for each of Version, Commit and Date, naming the variable in the failure message; it keeps the sibling's `var _ = []*string{&Version, &Commit, &Date}` too, so it does not lean on its neighbour for the half that makes a Go-side rename a compile error. Mutation-checked: `sed -i 's|internal/cli.Commit=|internal/cli.CommitX=|' .goreleaser.yaml` fails it with ".goreleaser.yaml no longer injects cli.Commit". This is the work the P10-05 dogfood run produced, reviewed and applied here.
  - Do: `internal/cli/root_test.go`'s `TestVersionLdflagsMatchMakefile` reads the Makefile, so renaming `cli.Version`/`Commit`/`Date` fails a test. `.goreleaser.yaml` now carries the same three `-X` flags and nothing guards them, so a rename would silently ship a release binary that prints `dev`. A sibling test reading `../../.goreleaser.yaml` is a few lines.
  - Done when: renaming one of the three variables fails a test that names the release config.
- [ ] **P10-10 — `make release-dry-run`** (deps: P10-03; size: S)
  - Do: the dry run is a header comment in `.goreleaser.yaml` today. The Makefile already has the golangci-lint install pattern to copy: `GORELEASER_VERSION := v2.18.0`, a `bin/goreleaser` target, then `goreleaser check` plus `goreleaser release --snapshot --clean --skip=publish`.
  - Done when: `make release-dry-run` produces `dist/` from a clean tree and `make clean` removes it.
- [x] **P10-12 — Two lenses on one bead run git in the same worktree at the same time** (deps: P8-14; size: S) (done: wf-p10 `aab67cd`) — Found by chasing a `make check-golden` failure in round 3 rather than re-running it: the whole suite under `UPDATE_GOLDEN` failed once with `TestE2EKillAndResume` timing out after 3m, bead `e2e-4` stuck `in_review` while nine review firings died on `conductor: committing e2e-4's leftovers: gitx: git commit …: exit status 128: Another git process seems to be running in this repository, or the lock file may be stale`. D-05 puts up to two testers on one bead and the scheduler fires them in parallel; both build their prompt through `Conductor.BeadContext`, which commits D-14's leftovers and diffs the branch **in that bead's single worktree**, and git holds `index.lock` for the length of a commit. `BeadContext` now takes a mutex per bead — per bead, not one over all of them, because the beads have a worktree each — the way `Conductor.deliver` already serialises the repository-mutating half of a delivery. `TestGlueBeadContextSerialisesOneBeadsWorktree` fires two contexts at one bead and fails if they meet inside `CommitLeftovers`; deleting the lock fails it with "expected 1, actual 2". The original e2e failure needed the whole suite's load and did **not** reproduce in four isolated `-race` runs of that test, so the attribution is the error text plus the demonstrated overlap, not a reproduction; six `-race` runs of `TestE2E*` are green with the fix.
  - Do: done, above. Left open deliberately: the deliverer's own `CommitLeftovers` (`internal/orchestrator/delivery.go`) is serialised only by the global `Conductor.deliver`, not by this per-bead lock. Nothing observed overlaps them today (a bead is delivered after its lenses are finished), but if one ever does, the delivery should take the same per-bead lock — and the agents' own sessions can run git in a worktree two lenses share, which no lock inside `sc` can serialise.
  - Done when: two firings on one bead never run git in its worktree at the same time, with a test that fails without the fix.
- [ ] **P10-13 — `make check-golden` throws away the evidence when regeneration fails** (deps: P10-03; size: S)
  - Do: the target runs `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 go test -count=1 ./... >/dev/null`, so when a test fails during regeneration the whole output is discarded and the user sees only `make: *** [Makefile:54: check-golden] Error 1` — no package, no test name, no failure text. This cost a round-3 integrator a full extra suite run to find out that `TestE2EKillAndResume` was the failure (P10-12). Tee the output to a file, or drop the redirect and let the suite print; the Makefile is off-limits to most agents, so this needs whoever owns it.
  - Done when: a failing regeneration prints the failing package and test.
- [ ] **P10-11 — Confirm the GitHub owner/repo and the Homebrew tap once a remote exists** (deps: P10-03, P10-01; size: S)
  - Do: the repository has no `origin`, so `.goreleaser.yaml` (`SC_REPO_OWNER`/`SC_REPO_NAME`), `install.sh` (`SC_REPO`), the generated cask and the README's Install section all fall back to a guessed `maxh213/slop-cannon`. The release workflow's own inference path and the `curl | sh` URL are therefore unproven against GitHub itself. `maxh213/homebrew-tap` and a `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN` secret have to exist before `HOMEBREW_TAP_SKIP_UPLOAD=false` is worth setting. A test that reads the default repo out of `install.sh` and asserts the README's URLs match would keep the three in step, and `CHANGELOG.md` should join `archives.files` now that it exists.
  - Done when: the three places name the real repository, the first tag push is observed green, and the README's install line is the one that works.

---

## Appendix A — beads (`bd`) facts verified on 2026-09-02

Verified live against `bd` 1.2.2 (latest release, 2026-08-15; repo moved to `github.com/gastownhall/beads`, Go module path still `github.com/steveyegge/beads`) in a scratch repo, plus the upstream docs. Agents must not re-derive these; if a newer bd changes them, update this appendix and the affected tasks.

**Install and backend**
- `bd` embeds the Dolt engine; embedded mode is the default and needs **no** `dolt` binary (verified with `dolt` removed from `PATH`). The standalone `dolt` CLI is only for server mode. `CGO_ENABLED=0 go install` builds are server-mode only; `brew install beads`, the upstream `scripts/install.sh`, or a cgo build give embedded mode.
- Embedded mode is single-writer with a file lock. 30 concurrent `bd` writers all succeeded but queued (6.4 s total, ~0.2 s each); 20 readers concurrent with 10 writers all succeeded. `sc` serialises calls anyway (D-21).
- `dolt.auto-commit` defaults to `on` in embedded mode: every write is a Dolt commit; `bd history <id>` shows them. No manual commit/sync step is needed. `bd doctor --json` printed nothing in embedded mode; use `bd ping --json`, `bd info --json`, `bd where --json`, `bd context --json`.
- Typical latency: 0.15–0.2 s per command.

**Dolt itself (verified from dolthub/dolt, dolthub/driver, and dolthub.com docs)**
- Dolt is a MySQL-compatible SQL database with Git-style commits, branches, and merges; beads stores issues in tables under Dolt's own ref namespace, so nothing about issue state touches the code branches or branch protection.
- Embedded use goes through the `database/sql` driver `github.com/dolthub/driver` (beads pins `driver/v2` 2.2.0 and the Dolt engine at the v2.2.0 commit); DSN `file:///path?commitname=…&commitemail=…&database=…`; no server process. The engine holds an exclusive directory lock (`dolthub/fslock`, a `flock` on the noms `LOCK` file); the driver's `Config.BackOff` retries engine open on lock contention, and beads sets it to retry until context cancellation with a 5 s max interval, which is why concurrent `bd` processes queue instead of erroring.
- Transactions: Read Committed isolation; "if two writers attempt to write the same data, one writer is allowed to commit their transaction and the other is rolled back"; autocommit wraps each write in `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`; `@@dolt_transaction_commit` can turn every SQL commit into a Dolt commit (this is what `dolt.auto-commit: on` uses). Dolt serialises writes and is rated at roughly a few hundred writes per second, far above anything `sc` produces.
- Server mode (`dolt sql-server`, port 3307 in beads) is the multi-writer path and needs the standalone `dolt` CLI. Latest Dolt is 2.3.1 (2026-08-19); beads pins the CLI at **2.2.0** because 2.3.0/2.3.1 intermittently break `DOLT_RESET('--hard')` on a few percent of fresh databases. This affects server mode only; embedded mode links its own engine. The `dolt` 2.3.1 on this machine is therefore irrelevant to `sc` unless server mode is added later, and `sc doctor` should say so if it ever checks it.

**Init side effects (why D-13 uses the flags it does)**
- Default `bd init` writes `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/settings.json`, `.codex/`, `.agents/skills/beads/`, installs `pre-commit`, `post-merge`, `pre-push`, `post-checkout`, `prepare-commit-msg` hooks, appends to `.gitignore`, and **commits** `.beads/` to the repo.
- `--skip-agents --skip-hooks` removes the files and hooks but still commits. `--setup-exclude` adds `.beads/` (and `**/RECOVERY*.md`, `**/SESSION*.md`) to `.git/info/exclude` and does not commit, but still appends the `.gitignore` block. `--stealth` edits global git config. `--init-if-missing` makes re-runs exit 0. `bd init` prints a one-time anonymous-metrics notice; `bd metrics off` disables collection.

**JSON contract**
- `BD_JSON_ENVELOPE=1` wraps every `--json` output as `{"schema_version":1,"data":…}` (plus `pagination` on truncated `bd ready`); it becomes the default in bd 2.0. Errors: `{"error","code","hint"}`, exit 1.
- Legacy shapes without the envelope: `create` returns an object with `schema_version`; `show`, `update`, `close`, `list`, `ready` return arrays (`show` even for one id).
- Issue fields seen: `id, title, description, acceptance_criteria, status, priority (int), issue_type, owner, assignee, created_at, created_by, updated_at, started_at, closed_at, close_reason, metadata (object), labels ([]), dependency_count, dependent_count, comment_count`; `show --include-comments` adds `comments[] {id, issue_id, author, text, created_at}`; `show --include-dependents`/`dep list` add `dependencies[]` with `dependency_type`; `blocked` adds `blocked_by[]`.
- `list` defaults: limit 50, tree format, closed excluded → always `-n 0 --flat`, `--all` for closed. `ready` default limit 100 → `-n 0`. `ready` returns only status `open` (category `active`).

**Statuses, types, priorities**
- Built-in statuses: `open` (active), `in_progress`, `blocked`, `hooked` (wip), `deferred`, `pinned` (frozen), `closed` (done). Custom: `bd config set status.custom "name:category,…"` stored in the database; categories `active|wip|done|frozen` control `bd ready` and default `bd list` visibility. `bd statuses --json` lists both.
- Core types: `task, bug, feature, chore, epic, decision, spike, story, milestone`; `gate` and `event` are created by `bd gate create`/`bd set-state`. Priority `0`–`4`, `0` highest, default `2`.
- IDs are hashes with the repo prefix (`sc-68s`); `--parent` children get hierarchical ids (`sc-dml.1`), graph-plan children get flat ids.

**Dependencies**
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` (aliases `--blocked-by`, `--depends-on`); types `blocks` (default, affects readiness), `parent-child`, `waits-for`, `related`, `tracks`, `discovered-from`, … Cycles are rejected at write time; `bd dep cycles` audits.
- `bd create --deps <id>` = new depends on id. `bd create --deps blocks:<id>` = **new blocks id** (reverse). Never use the typed form.
- `bd close` refuses when blockers are open (`--force` overrides). `bd blocked --json` explains why.

**Graph plan (`bd create --graph plan.json`)**
```json
{"commit_message":"optional",
 "nodes":[{"key":"a","title":"…","type":"task","priority":1,"description":"…","acceptance_criteria":"…",
           "labels":["sc:run:r1"],"metadata":{"sc_run":"r1"},"parent_key":"epic","deps":[{"target":"b","type":"blocks"}]}],
 "edges":[{"from_key":"x","to_key":"y","type":"blocks"}]}
```
Unknown fields are silently dropped with a warning on stderr; `--dry-run` validates. `deps[].target` resolves plan keys first, then literal ids. Result maps keys to ids. **Correction (P0-09 capture, D-24)**: against bd 1.2.2 the node-level `acceptance_criteria` and `deps` fields above were reported as unknown and dropped (`edge_count: 0`); use top-level `edges[]` for dependencies and see P3-11 for acceptance criteria. Samples: `internal/testutil/testdata/bd/create-graph-dry-run.{json,stderr}`.

**Claims, comments, labels, metadata**
- `bd update <id> --claim` is atomic: assignee = actor, status `in_progress`, idempotent for the same actor; `bd ready --claim --json` claims the first ready bead. Actor comes from `--actor` / `BEADS_ACTOR` / git user.name. `bd update -s open --assignee ""` releases.
- `bd comment <id> --stdin` (or positional text) writes a comment with author = actor; `bd comments add <id> -f <file>` works, but `-f -` does **not** read stdin. `bd comments <id> --json` lists.
- Labels: `-l a,b` on create, `--add-label/--remove-label/--set-labels` on update, `bd label add/remove/list`. `<dimension>:<value>` is the upstream state-cache convention; `bd set-state` uses it but also creates an `event` child bead per change (visible only with `--include-infra`), so `sc` uses statuses instead.
- Metadata: `--metadata '<json>'` or `@file`, `--set-metadata k=v`, filter with `bd list --metadata-field k=v`; prefixes `bd:` and `_` are reserved.

**Corrections verified during Phase 3 (2026-09-02, bd 1.2.2)**
- `bd init` refuses `-C <dir>` on a directory without a project; run it with cwd = repo root (D-26).
- Errors (P3-12): bd 1.2.2 does **not** emit the `{"error","code","hint"}` envelope. `bd show nope-1 --json` prints `{"data":{"error":"no issues found matching the provided IDs"},"schema_version":1}` with exit 1; `bd close` blocked refusals, `--readonly` refusals and `bd -C <plain-dir> …` print a stderr line only. `beads.KindOf` keys on exit status plus `data.error`/stderr text and keeps the `code` path for later versions. Most writes also print `warning: beads.role not configured` on stderr (bd checks the global git config, not the `.git/config` it wrote itself).
- Graph plans (P3-11): accepted node fields are `key, title, type, priority, description, labels, metadata, parent_key, parent_id, assignee`; no spelling of acceptance criteria is accepted (`acceptance`, `acceptance_criteria`, `notes`, `design`, `owner`, `id`, `deps` are warned about and dropped), so `bd update --acceptance` after creation stays the path; metadata values must be JSON strings (a number fails the whole call; `beads.GraphMetadata` stringifies); `edges[]` accept `from_key/to_key` or `from_id/to_id`; `parent_deps` counts nodes with a parent; the run epic appears in `bd ready` (`Client.Ready` filters it). Samples: `internal/beads/testdata/bd/graph-*.json`.
- `--readonly` (P3-09) blocks issue-level writes but **not** `config set/unset`, `config set-many`, `config apply`, `import` (it inserted an issue), `metrics`, `dolt`, `init`, `restore`, `hooks`, `setup`, `rename`, `backup init`/`backup sync` (which can push the whole database off-machine), `federation add-peer`, `audit record`, `comments add` and — found in the P3-09 review, re-verified live on 2026-09-03 — the Dolt-level `branch <name>` (creates a branch, exit 0) and `vc commit`/`vc merge` (both run, exit 0). The shim therefore does not rely on bd for any verb: it runs an **allow-list** of read commands (`beads.ShimAllowed`, with `beads.ShimAllowedExceptions` naming the write subcommands of an otherwise-allowed command) and refuses everything else — including any verb a future bd adds — before bd runs. `--readonly` is also a plain pflag bool, so a later `--readonly=false` (or `=0`, `=F`) anywhere in the arguments wins and re-enables writes — verified live: `update`, `comment` and `create` all succeeded through a shim without a guard — so the generated shim refuses any argument equal to `--readonly` or starting with `--readonly=` before it execs bd (`beads.ReadOnlyFlag`; arguments after a bare `--`, and the value of `-C`/`--actor`/`--db`/`--directory`/`--dolt-auto-commit`, are inert for pflag and pass through). bd does block the ordinary write verbs itself — `delete`, `note`, `tag`, `set-state`, `defer`, `kv set`, `remember`, `prune`, `purge`, `compact`, `flatten`, `gc`, `migrate`, `worktree create` (all checked 2026-09-03) — but that is now belt and braces: the allow-list refuses them first. Outside a beads project bd answers `no beads database found` before it looks at `--readonly`.
- Claims (P3-05): `bd update --claim` accepts `--metadata @file` in the same call and writes nothing when the claim is refused; it refuses every status except an unassigned `open` bead or the same actor's `in_progress` bead (`issue not claimable: status <s>`), so `changes_requested → in_progress` needs a plain `-s in_progress --assignee` update (`Client.Reclaim`).
- Gates (P3-08): `bd gate check` prints per-gate verdict lines (`✓ resolved`, `○ pending`, `⚠ ESCALATE` on stdout, `✗ … error checking` on stderr) before the JSON envelope; a CLOSED or missing PR is reported ESCALATE with or without `--escalate` and the gate stays open; `--escalate` only shells out to Gas Town `gt escalate`; `bd gate resolve --json` prints text; `bd close <gate>` also closes a gate; `bd gate list --json` has no field for the blocked issue (it is parsed from the `Ad-hoc gate blocking <id>` description).
- Dolt (P3-06): `bd history <id>` lists every Dolt commit made after the bead was created, whichever bead the write touched; `bd history nope-1 --json` prints `data: null` with exit 0; `bd config get dolt.auto-commit` prints `on` even with no config line; with `--dolt-auto-commit off` writes add no commit until `bd dolt commit`.
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` accepts a non-existent depends-on id with exit 0.

**Multi-agent features worth knowing**
- `--readonly` global flag blocks writes (`operation 'update' is not allowed in read-only mode`); `--sandbox` disables Dolt auto-push; `-C <dir>` like `git -C`.
- Gates: `bd gate create --type=gh:pr|gh:run|timer|human --blocks <id> --await-id <n>`; `bd gate check [--type=gh:pr] [--escalate]` uses `gh pr view`/`gh run view`; `bd gate list --json`; `bd gate resolve <gate>`.
- `bd merge-slot create|acquire|release` serialises merge-conflict work across agents. `bd batch` runs several simple writes in one transaction (`close`, `update status=…`, `create`, `dep add`). `bd swarm validate|status <epic>` reports parallel waves. `bd epic status --json`. `bd graph --all` / `--dot` / `--html` renders the DAG (useful for debugging the TUI layout).
- Worktrees: plain `git worktree add` shares the main `.beads/` via the git common dir; `bd worktree create` also edits `.gitignore`. `BEADS_DIR` overrides discovery.
- Env: `BD_NON_INTERACTIVE`, `BD_JSON_ENVELOPE`, `BEADS_ACTOR`, `BEADS_DIR`, `BD_DOLT_AUTO_COMMIT`, `BEADS_DOLT_SERVER_MODE`, `BEADS_HOOK_TIMEOUT`.

P6-01…P6-10 done, along with the discovered tasks folded into them: P6-11 (`--resume` restores the frozen `ConfigSnapshot`, now called from `cli.ResolveRunWithNotes`), P6-12, P6-13, P6-14, P6-15, P6-16, P6-17, P6-18, P6-19, P6-20, P6-21, and the cross-phase P5-11, P5-12 and P1-16 (`max_beads_per_plan`). The eleven lanes were written in parallel against interfaces, so the integrator's first job was reconciliation: `host.go` now owns every concept more than one component needs — `Publisher` (the package's only event sink, replacing the lanes' separate `EventSink`/`GateHost`/`Publisher`), `EventHost`, `RunHost` with its compile-time assertions, `mergeEnv` and `BeadStateSettled` — and `doc.go` says how the parts fit together. Three real defects found in the seams were fixed rather than deferred: `Supervisor.stamp` blanked the workdir of a worktree-less firing, which refused every planning and arbitrating product owner; the scheduler's atomic bd claim published no `BeadStateChanged`, so a bead appeared to jump from `open` straight to `in_review` in the run log, `sc status` and the TUI graph (fixed with `Demand.From`); and both the scheduler and the supervisor renewed the same lease (`Supervisor.Fire` now sets `NoLease`). Exit criteria: `TestE2EBriefRunWithSafetyOn`/`Off` and `TestE2EKillAndResume` drive the production stack over a real fixture repository with a bare origin, a stateful `bd` fixture tracker, a `gh` shim and the mock cartridge (12.5 s for all four, inside P6-09's 30 s); ammo is never exceeded under a randomised property test and under the real runs, sampled every millisecond. P7-10's `ControlsModel` was wired into `Model` (dispatch, `ControlResultMsg`, overlay, footer, `?` overlay) and `sc tui-demo` given a recording stand-in controller, then driven in a real 100x30 pty: `p`, `a`, `S` and `x` all reach the controller and `r` refuses a bead that is not failed or blocked; no existing golden frame moved. Phase 7 is therefore done too. Discovered tasks recorded: P6-22…P6-31, P1-17, P7-20, P8-14, P8-15, P10-07. Three intermittent failures were chased to their causes rather than re-run away. (i) `TestPlannerAutopilotKeepsReplanningThroughTwentyFourHours` compared `Attempts` (incremented when a firing starts) with `Empty` (incremented when it lands), which the round still in flight loses; it now compares `Plans` with `Empty`, and `planningEventually`'s conditions are all "at least" rather than exact, because the fake clock jumps a second per poll and a loaded machine can slip several idle re-plans between two polls. (ii) `TestE2EKillAndResume` livelocked under concurrent race-instrumented runs: **git holds `.git/index.lock` for the whole of an add, a commit or a rebase step and never clears it after a SIGKILL**, so the killed run's worktree was permanently unusable, every engineer firing for that bead failed inside `CommitLeftovers` before it reached the cartridge, and the scheduler re-offered and re-fired it — 612 attempts in 45 s with no `AgentFailed` to show for it, because `Supervisor.Fire` returns a planning error before it registers the agent. `gitx.Resumer` now clears a stale index lock in each worktree it reattaches (`TestResumer_Resume_ClearsAStaleIndexLock`), which is the fix; the livelock the incident exposed is recorded as P6-31. (iii) The e2e conductor now paces itself by its own pass duration and the rig's gate poller ticks at 100 ms, so bookkeeping cannot starve the single serialised bd client, and `e2eRig.wait` prints a full diagnosis (scheduler, planner, gates, threads, bead states, cartridge rules, bd calls, agent failures) instead of "the run did not finish". The kill-and-resume test then ran 48 times four-way under `-race` without a failure, and the whole package 32 times four-way. Two things a reader should know: `orchestrator.ClearStaleGate` is tested but still has no production caller (P6-22, the retry half of P6-14), and nothing yet assembles the run from these parts — `sc run` still resolves a `RunConfig` and stops (P8-01, with the run-level glue the e2e test had to invent recorded as P8-14). Note for the human: the `bd` on `PATH` on this machine is still 1.0.5, below the D-21 minimum; the verification above used `bin/bd-1.2.2` through a temporary shim directory.

The only skip in the whole tagged suite is `TestLive_RealCLI`, which needs a logged-in Claude CLI and is never run in CI. Reconciliation was the first job again but needed almost nothing: the eleven lanes kept every new symbol in their own owner-prefixed files (`fb*`, `sup*`, `plan*`, `gate*`, `delivery*`), the new narrow interfaces (`PlanPrioritiser`, `GateBeadReader`, `ReleaseOnlyStore`) are distinct roles over `*beads.Client` rather than duplicates, and `host.go` still owns the concepts more than one component needs. One correction was made: `doc.go` claimed *every* status write goes through the `StateMachine`, which P6-03 made true of the shutdown release path this round, so the one genuine exception — the scheduler's atomic bd claim, which `Scheduler.announceClaim` publishes rather than performs — is now named in the package doc instead of being contradicted by it. The round's substance is the review gaps: P6-03's shutdown release now takes the D-07 `released` edge or leaves the bead where it is; P6-04 splits a mixed round into an evaluation and a re-review and makes `Restore` write the transition the killed run owed; P6-05 drives the D-21 shim, the P6-19 rejection path and Q-04 with real tests (Q-04 lands as `PlanPrioritiser` plus a restate-with-a-larger-priority convention, because the plan schema is `additionalProperties: false` — the first-class field is P5-14); P6-06 takes the D-09 baseline before the rebase and scores the resolution with a rebase-safe change-set comparison, which is what finally makes D-10's no-re-review path reachable; P6-07 stops `pick` firing a cartridge the selector refused and stops the copy-worktree degrade escaping through a symlink; P6-08 folds `PlanProduced`, survives a record with no bead id, streams the log, scrubs untrusted text and tells a crashed run from a live one; P6-10 remembers and retries a `bd close` bd refused, which is the only thing that can ever close the bead once `bd gate check` has consumed the gate. P7-10 was re-driven in a real 45x200 pty and all six keys reach the controller; the driver has to answer the `OSC 11` and `CSI 6n` queries the TUI sends or Bubble Tea swallows the keystrokes as query responses, which is the trap the previous attempt hit. Phase 7 is done. Discovered tasks recorded: P6-32 (nothing publishes `BeadCreated`/`GraphChanged`), P6-33 (the run log has no cap or rotation), P6-34 (the planning shim is never removed), P6-35 (two firings of one lens in one round look like a retry), P1-18 (`max_conflict_rounds` needs a key and a Decision), P4-14 (`gitx.ChangeRatio` is tip-to-tip), P5-14 (a first-class plan field for Q-04), P7-21 (the footer flash is clipped by the short keymap), P7-22 (no configuration-change event), P8-16 (`Feedback.Restore` has no caller), P8-17 (wire `Options.Beads` with `Options.Leases`), P8-18 (the feedback context provider must commit through `DeliveryGit`), P9-10 (three hand-written escape scrubbers should be one). Nothing yet assembles the run from these parts — `sc run` still stops at resolving a `RunConfig` (P8-01, with the run-level glue the e2e test invents recorded as P8-14) — so `PlanPrioritiser`, `Options.Beads` and `Feedback.Restore` have no production caller by design until then.
